## Phase 24 — Commercial readiness — completed 2026-08-20

Built directly per the user's standing "no prompt files" instruction — inspected the existing implementation (widget auth, RBAC, audit log, escalation, knowledge ingestion, retrieval SQL) via a dedicated recon pass first, then asked at four real architectural forks before writing any code, then implemented all nine phase items in one combined pass (the user's explicit choice, matching Phase 23's style, after being asked whether to split).

**Resolved forks, asked before implementation:** (1) RBAC via **Clerk custom org roles**, not an app-level roles table — session claims already carry `orgRole`, avoiding a second, driftable source of truth. (2) Widget keys: a new **`widget_keys` table** (many keys per business, each with its own `allowed_origins` list and active/revoked status) replacing the v1 single-column design, enabling zero-downtime rotation. (3) "Team" for business-hours/SLA assignment means **the business's existing Clerk org members** — no separate named-teams concept. (4) File/URL knowledge ingestion: **Supabase Storage + scheduled auto-refresh** for URL sources (not manual-refresh-only).

**Business profile wired into the AI prompt.** `lib/rag.ts`'s `SYSTEM_TEMPLATE` and `askSalesEmployee()` now accept and interpolate `description`/`website`/`contactEmail`/`contactPhone` (only the fields a business actually filled in — never a blank line) — previously dashboard-display-only since Phase 13b. `lib/widget-auth.ts`'s `resolveBusinessFromWidgetKey()` now also returns this profile.

**Answer citations.** New `messages.source_chunk_ids uuid[]` column (migration `20260821000000`) persists which knowledge chunks grounded an assistant reply — previously computed every turn (`askSalesEmployee()`'s `sourceChunkIds`, since Phase 8) but only ever written into the Phase 23 response cache, never durably attached to the message. `app/(dashboard)/dashboard/conversations/_components/message-bubble.tsx` gained an on-demand "Sources (N)" expander (new `getCitationDetailsAction`, `lib/knowledge.ts`'s `getCitationDetails()`) — chunk content/document title fetched lazily, not preloaded for every message.

**Widget key rotation and multiple origins.** New `widget_keys` table (migration `20260821010000`) — one business has many keys, each with its own `allowed_origins text[]` and `active`/`revoked` status; the old `businesses.widget_key`/`widget_allowed_origin` columns are dropped, existing keys/origins backfilled into the new table first (embedded snippets keep working with zero business-owner action). `lib/widget-keys.ts` (CRUD) and a rebuilt `/dashboard/widget-settings` (create/edit-origins/revoke, one card per key) replace the old single-key page. `lib/widget-auth.ts`'s `resolveBusinessFromWidgetKey()` now joins `widget_keys` → `businesses` and checks `status = 'active'` + origin membership in `allowed_origins`.

**Four-tier RBAC.** `org:admin`/`org:member` (Clerk built-ins, unchanged) plus two new custom Clerk roles this app reads but cannot create itself (Clerk's Backend API has no custom-role CRUD — confirmed live against the real Clerk instance, not assumed): `org:sales_agent` and `org:analyst_viewer`. **The user still needs to create these two roles in the Clerk Dashboard** (Organizations → Roles & Permissions) using exactly those key strings — until then, every session simply ranks below `org:sales_agent` and is denied every gated write (`lib/auth.ts`'s `hasMinRole()` treats an unrecognized role as rank `-1`, never "trust it"). `lib/business-context.ts`'s `requireBusinessContext()` now also returns `orgRole`. Applied: products/services/FAQs/knowledge writes require `org:member`+; conversation control/reply/dismiss and lead status updates require `org:sales_agent`+; widget keys and webhook endpoints require `org:admin` (unchanged hard admin-only gates like business profile/deletion/export were untouched). **Known gap:** dashboard nav/UI does not yet hide controls a role can't use — an `org:analyst_viewer` sees write buttons that will simply reject their click server-side (safe, not a security gap, but not polished).

**Extended audit log.** `audit_log`'s action enum (migration `20260821020000`) grew from 3 to 11 values: `knowledge.published`/`unpublished`, `widget_key.created`/`origins_updated`/`revoked`, `webhook_endpoint.created`/`deleted`, `business_hours.updated`, alongside the original three. `/dashboard/audit-log`'s `ACTION_LABEL` map extended to match.

**Knowledge draft/publish versioning.** `knowledge_documents` gained `status` (`draft`/`published`, default `published` so no existing content silently goes dark), `version`, `published_at` (migration `20260821030000`); new `knowledge_document_versions` append-only history table snapshots title/content on every publish. New manual/file/url documents start as `draft` — auto-generated product/service/FAQ documents keep inserting with no explicit status (picking up the `published` default; they mirror a live catalog row, so there's no draft workflow for them). `match_knowledge_chunks()` (the pgvector retrieval function) now joins `knowledge_documents` and filters `status = 'published'` — a draft's chunks are never retrieval-eligible, enforcing AGENTS.md rule 4 at the SQL layer, not just the UI. `lib/knowledge.ts` gained `publishKnowledgeDocument()`/`unpublishKnowledgeDocument()`; `/dashboard/knowledge` shows a draft/published pill and Publish/Unpublish button per document.

**File and URL knowledge ingestion.** `knowledge_documents.source_type` extended to `file`/`url` (migration `20260821050000`), plus `source_url`/`storage_path`/`refresh_interval_hours`/`last_refreshed_at` columns. New private Supabase Storage bucket `knowledge-files` (business-id-prefixed object paths, RLS policies mirroring the table convention). `lib/file-ingestion.ts` (`.txt`/`.md` only — deliberately no PDF/DOCX parsing dependency this phase, a documented limitation, not a silent gap) and `lib/url-ingestion.ts` (fetch + a hand-rolled `lib/html-extract.ts` HTML-to-text stripper, no new dependency) both create draft documents that flow through the same publish gate and ingestion queue as manual documents. URL sources support scheduled auto-refresh (`refreshDueUrlKnowledgeSources()`, opt-in `refreshIntervalHours` per source) alongside a manual "Refresh" button. `/dashboard/knowledge` gained upload/import forms and a source-type badge per document.

**Outbound webhook on new qualified lead.** New `webhook_endpoints` (config) and `webhook_deliveries` (queue) tables (migration `20260821040000`), same claim-based `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` pattern as Phase 23's ingestion queue (`claim_webhook_deliveries()`). `lib/webhooks.ts` (endpoint CRUD, `enqueueLeadQualifiedWebhooks()`) and `lib/webhook-delivery.ts` (HMAC-SHA256-signed delivery via `X-Webhook-Signature`, exponential backoff, 5-attempt dead-letter — mirrors `lib/ingestion-queue.ts` deliberately, not reinvented). The only lead-creation path in the app (`lib/tools/request-callback.ts`'s `executeRequestCallback`, confirmed via grep — `lib/leads.ts`'s own `createLead()` export has zero callers) enqueues a delivery to every active endpoint and triggers `after(() => processWebhookDeliveries())` on a new (not updated) lead. New `/dashboard/webhooks` page (admin-gated) to add/reveal-secret/delete endpoints.

**Business hours + SLA-based escalation routing.** `businesses` gained `timezone`, `sla_minutes` (nullable — null disables SLA routing), `next_assignment_cursor` (round-robin pointer); `conversations` gained `assigned_to_user_id`/`attention_flagged_at`; new `business_hours` table (migration `20260821060000`, one row per configured day, empty = always-open so routing works with zero setup). `lib/team-assignment.ts`'s `assignNextTeamMember()` round-robins across the business's real Clerk org members (Backend API `getOrganizationMembershipList`) — "who's notified first," not an exclusive claim; any staff member can still take over any conversation. `lib/conversations.ts`'s `flagConversationNeedsAttention()` now stamps `attention_flagged_at` once and assigns a member if none is set. `lib/sla-routing.ts`'s `runSlaEscalationSweep()` re-routes conversations still flagged past their business's SLA to the next team member, only while within business hours (`lib/business-hours.ts`'s `isWithinBusinessHours()`, timezone-aware via `Intl.DateTimeFormat`) — no notification channel exists yet (Phase 25 owns "notifications that reach the team outside the browser tab"), so this phase's routing is reassignment/audit-trail only, not an alert. New `/dashboard/business-hours` page (admin-gated) for the weekly schedule + SLA minutes.

**Shared cron backstop, not one job per queue.** `app/api/cron/process-ingestion-queue/route.ts` (Vercel Hobby plan caps Cron Jobs — Phase 23 already established this constraint) now runs all four background sweeps in one daily invocation: knowledge ingestion, webhook delivery, URL source refresh, and SLA escalation. Each has its own immediate `after()` trigger for the common case; this route is the shared backstop for whatever those miss.

**Checks:** `npm run lint` — pass, zero errors/warnings. `npx tsc --noEmit` — pass. `npm run build` — pass, all 29 routes including the two new dashboard pages (`/dashboard/webhooks`, `/dashboard/business-hours`). `npm test` — pass, all 20 pgTAP files (17 pre-existing + 3 new: `018_widget_keys_tenant_isolation.sql`, `019_webhook_endpoints_tenant_isolation.sql`, `020_business_hours_tenant_isolation.sql`), against production (this session's `supabase` CLI remains linked to production, not staging — same standing limitation Phase 22g/23 recorded).

**Files changed (new):** `lib/widget-keys.ts`, `lib/webhooks.ts`, `lib/webhook-delivery.ts`, `lib/team-assignment.ts`, `lib/business-hours.ts`, `lib/sla-routing.ts`, `lib/file-ingestion.ts`, `lib/url-ingestion.ts`, `lib/html-extract.ts`, plus new dashboard pages/components under `app/(dashboard)/dashboard/webhooks/`, `app/(dashboard)/dashboard/business-hours/`, `app/(dashboard)/dashboard/knowledge/_components/{file-upload-form,url-import-form,refresh-url-button,publish-toggle-button}.tsx`, `app/(dashboard)/dashboard/widget-settings/{create-widget-key-form,widget-key-list}.tsx`. **Modified:** `lib/rag.ts`, `lib/widget-auth.ts`, `lib/auth.ts`, `lib/business-context.ts`, `lib/knowledge.ts`, `lib/conversations.ts`, `lib/messages.ts`, `lib/audit-log.ts` (type only), `lib/supabase/types.ts`, `app/api/chat/route.ts`, `app/api/cron/process-ingestion-queue/route.ts`, `lib/tools/request-callback.ts`, every products/services/faqs/knowledge/leads/conversations/widget-settings `actions.ts`, `/dashboard/knowledge/page.tsx`, `/dashboard/widget-settings/page.tsx` (rewritten), `/dashboard/audit-log/page.tsx`, `_components/nav-items.tsx`. **Deleted:** `app/(dashboard)/dashboard/widget-settings/widget-origin-form.tsx` (superseded by the widget-keys UI).

**Migrations:** `20260821000000` (messages citations), `20260821010000` (widget_keys), `20260821020000` (audit log actions), `20260821030000` (knowledge versioning), `20260821040000` (webhook tables), `20260821050000` (file/url sources + storage bucket), `20260821060000` (business hours + SLA) — all applied and verified live against production (this session's only linked target, same standing staging gap as Phase 22g/23).

**Environment variables:** none added — no new API keys or secrets this phase (webhook signing secrets are generated per-endpoint and stored in the database, not env vars).

**Known limitations, not glossed over:** (1) **The two new Clerk custom roles (`org:sales_agent`, `org:analyst_viewer`) do not exist yet** — the user must create them in the Clerk Dashboard before the new tiers actually differentiate anyone; until then every real session is `org:admin` or `org:member` as before, and RBAC gating is inert-but-safe (never open, just not yet differentiated). (2) File ingestion supports `.txt`/`.md` only — no PDF/DOCX parsing (would need a new dependency this phase deliberately didn't add). (3) `lib/html-extract.ts` is a hand-rolled regex-based extractor, not a real HTML parser — good enough for ordinary business web pages, not a guarantee against malformed/adversarial HTML. (4) SLA routing reassigns and logs (`sla_breach_reassigned`) but sends no notification — there is no outside-the-browser-tab notification channel yet (Phase 25). (5) The dashboard does not yet hide/disable controls a viewer's role can't use (server-side gating is real; the UI just doesn't pre-filter). (6) None of this phase's nine features had a live, real-browser click-through pass this session (unlike Phase 23's live-verified ingestion/caching work) — every check that ran (lint, typecheck, build, all 20 pgTAP tenant-isolation tests) passed against real production infrastructure, but no one has yet clicked through Publish/Unpublish, key rotation, a real webhook delivery, or the business-hours form in an actual browser session. (7) Same standing gap as Phase 22g/23: migrations were only applied to production, not staging (this session never had the second Supabase account's credentials).

## Phase 23 — Reliability & scale — completed 2026-08-20

Built directly per the user's standing "no prompt files" instruction — inspected first, asked at each real architectural fork (staging approach, queue mechanism, Vercel plan), then implemented. The user chose one combined implementation (not staged sub-phases like Phase 22) and a DB-table-plus-Vercel-Cron-poller queue mechanism, confirmed the `ai-sales` Vercel project is on the **Hobby (free) plan** — which caps Cron Jobs at once/day — which directly shaped the design below.

**Background knowledge ingestion queue, dead-letter handling, idempotent ingestion.** Embedding moved fully off the request path. `knowledge_documents` gained `ingestion_status`/`ingestion_attempts`/`ingestion_last_error`/`ingestion_next_attempt_at`/`ingestion_updated_at` (migration `20260820220000`) — no separate jobs table; one document row *is* the queue entry. A new `security definer` function, `public.claim_knowledge_ingestion_jobs(p_limit)`, atomically claims due jobs via `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` (`execute` revoked from `anon`/`authenticated`, `service_role`-only, verified live via `has_function_privilege`). `lib/ingestion-queue.ts`'s `processIngestionQueue()` (service-role client — no Clerk session exists in this path, and it must reach across every business) claims up to 5 due jobs and reuses `regenerateChunksForDocument` (delete-then-reinsert — already idempotent by construction) for each; a failure schedules exponential backoff (1m/2m/4m/8m/16m) and re-marks `'pending'`, or past 5 attempts dead-letters to `'failed'` with the real error message recorded (text only, truncated, never a raw error object). `lib/knowledge.ts`'s `createKnowledgeDocument`/`updateKnowledgeDocument` and `lib/knowledge-sync.ts`'s `syncGeneratedDocument` (product/service/FAQ-generated documents) now call the new `enqueueIngestion` instead of embedding synchronously.

**Two triggers, no dedicated worker process** (this app has none — Vercel serverless + Supabase only): every knowledge-mutating Server Action calls `after(() => processIngestionQueue())` (`next/server`, confirmed available/stable in the installed Next 16.3.0) right after responding, so the common case processes within seconds without the user's own request waiting on a Gemini call. `app/api/cron/process-ingestion-queue/route.ts`, scheduled daily via new `vercel.json` (`0 4 * * *`), is only the backstop for whatever the immediate trigger missed — deliberately not the primary path, since Hobby-tier cron can't run more often. The route checks `Authorization: Bearer $CRON_SECRET` (Vercel's own convention for signing cron requests) and fails closed (404, not an open endpoint) when `CRON_SECRET` is unset — new optional env var, `lib/env.ts`/`.env.example`/`docs/security.md`.

New `app/(dashboard)/dashboard/knowledge/_components/ingestion-status-pill.tsx` (Pending/Processing/Ready/Failed pill, `title` attribute surfaces the real error on Failed) and `retry-ingestion-button.tsx` (re-enqueues a dead-lettered document), wired into `knowledge/page.tsx` and a new `retryIngestionAction` in `knowledge/actions.ts` — this is the "ingestion status UI (processing/failed/complete per knowledge item)" exit item, scoped to the existing Knowledge page (the only page that lists `knowledge_documents` rows directly; product/service/FAQ-generated documents have no separate list view to extend).

**Live-verified end-to-end, not just reviewed, with the user's explicit approval for each real-environment action taken (a Clerk sign-in-token mint, and restarting the local dev server twice with `GEMINI_API_KEY` temporarily broken):** a test document seeded at 4 prior attempts, run through the real cron endpoint with `GEMINI_API_KEY` deliberately invalid, dead-lettered on the very next attempt exactly as designed (`status: 'failed'`, `attempts: 5`, a real captured error) — confirmed both by direct DB query and by a real authenticated browser session (Clerk sign-in-token flow, headless Chromium via Playwright, installed isolated in the session scratchpad only, never added to the project) showing the "Failed" pill and Retry button on `/dashboard/knowledge` (screenshot retained in-session, not committed). Clicking Retry in that same real session reset it to processing and — once the real key was restored and the server restarted again — it reached `'complete'` with a real chunk row. Re-running ingestion on the same document a second time produced exactly the same one chunk, not two — idempotency confirmed by direct count. The cron route correctly returned `404` for both no-auth-header and wrong-secret requests.

**Safe response caching for low-variance questions.** New `public.ai_response_cache` (migration `20260820220500`, service-role-only, zero `authenticated`/`anon` grants, same pattern as `ai_response_metrics`) + `lib/response-cache.ts`, wired into `lib/rag.ts`'s `askSalesEmployee()`. A turn is cache-read/cache-written only when **all** hold: first conversational turn, grounded, not escalating, and no *side-effecting* tool call (`request_callback` specifically — a read-only `check_product_details`/`check_faq_topic` call does **not** disqualify caching, since FAQ-style lookups are exactly the low-variance case this targets). 1-hour TTL, purged daily. A hit skips retrieval and every Gemini call the turn would otherwise make.

**A real bug in the first implementation was caught by this session's own live testing, not shipped:** `askSalesEmployee()`'s `history` parameter always includes the current turn's own just-persisted message as its last element (`app/api/chat/route.ts` inserts the incoming message before fetching history, by design, so a human reviewer sees every prospect message regardless of control state) — so a genuinely first turn has `history.length === 1`, not `0`. The initial `history.length === 0` cache-eligibility gate therefore never fired on any real request; found via a live test showing zero cache-hit events and zero cache rows despite an eligible question, root-caused via a temporary debug log (removed before finalizing), and fixed with an explicit `isFirstTurn()` helper (`history.length <= 1`). **Noted but explicitly out of this phase's scope, not fixed:** this same duplication means the current question is sent to Gemini twice per turn — once as `history`'s last message, once via the prompt template's own final `{question}` slot — a pre-existing inefficiency this phase's live testing surfaced but did not touch (changing core prompt-building behavior needs its own scrutiny, not a drive-by fix inside a caching feature).

**Live-verified end-to-end:** two identical first-turn questions in separate conversations produced byte-identical cached answers, the second in ~2s versus ~17s for the first real Gemini call — confirmed via the `ai_response_cache_hit` log event and a direct DB row. The same question asked of a second business (Ghost Test Co.) did not receive Acme's cached answer (cross-tenant isolation holds). A follow-up message in an existing multi-turn conversation, same question text, correctly bypassed the cache (~20s, real Gemini call, no cache-hit log line) — proving the first-turn gate actually gates.

**Operational-table cleanup.** New migration `20260820221000` schedules two daily `pg_cron` sweeps (03:30/03:35 UTC, alongside Phase 22d's existing 03:00 conversation-retention job): `delete_expired_rate_limit_counters()` (windows are all ≤5 minutes; anything older than 1 day is unambiguously stale) and `delete_expired_ai_response_cache()` (rows past their own `expires_at`). Both `security definer`, `execute` revoked from `anon`/`authenticated`. Functionally pgTAP-tested (`017_operational_cleanup_functions.sql`), not just reviewed — confirms a stale row is deleted and a fresh one is left untouched, for both functions.

**HNSW index re-check.** Re-queried live (same check as Phase 19b): `knowledge_chunks` still has exactly 5 rows across 1 business. No migration added — nowhere near a volume where an index is justified; re-check again once real customer usage exists.

**Concurrent-load test.** New `scripts/run-load-test.mjs` (no new dependency, hand-rolled `fetch`, matching `run-evals.mjs`/`run-pgtap-tests.mjs`'s own convention) fires N concurrent, independent conversations against a real widget key and reports success/error counts and latency percentiles — deliberately scoped to prove the widget path holds *within* its designed IP rate limit (30/5min), not to re-prove the rate limiter itself (already covered live by Phase 18/21). **Run for real** against local dev, real Gemini, real Supabase: 8 concurrent conversations, 8/8 succeeded, 0 failed (p95 latency was high in this run, expected on a single local dev Node process serializing 8 simultaneous Gemini calls — not representative of Vercel production, where each request is its own isolated serverless function; this run proves correctness under concurrency, not production-scale latency). New `npm run loadtest` script.

**Deferred, per the phase brief's own conditional wording ("once usage justifies it"):** moving handoff polling to realtime/SSE/WebSockets. Real usage remains test-business-only; polling (Phase 15b) stays simple and already load-tested at this volume. Not built, explicitly not silently dropped — documented in `docs/architecture.md`.

**Checks:** `npm run lint` — pass, zero errors/warnings. `npm run typecheck` — pass. `npm run build` — pass, all 27 routes including the new `/api/cron/process-ingestion-queue`. `npm test` — pass, all 17 pgTAP files (15 pre-existing + 2 new: `016_ai_response_cache_tenant_isolation.sql`, `017_operational_cleanup_functions.sql`), against production (this session's `supabase` CLI remains linked to production, not staging, same standing limitation Phase 22g recorded — no session this cycle had the second account's credentials to re-link).

**Files changed:** `lib/knowledge.ts`, `lib/knowledge-sync.ts`, `lib/ingestion-queue.ts` (new), `lib/response-cache.ts` (new), `lib/rag.ts`, `lib/env.ts`, `lib/supabase/types.ts`, `app/(dashboard)/dashboard/knowledge/actions.ts`, `app/(dashboard)/dashboard/knowledge/page.tsx`, `app/(dashboard)/dashboard/knowledge/_components/ingestion-status-pill.tsx` (new), `app/(dashboard)/dashboard/knowledge/_components/retry-ingestion-button.tsx` (new), `app/api/cron/process-ingestion-queue/route.ts` (new), `vercel.json` (new), `scripts/run-load-test.mjs` (new), `package.json`, `.env.example`, `docs/security.md`, `docs/architecture.md`.

**Migrations:** `20260820220000_add_ingestion_status_to_knowledge_documents.sql`, `20260820220500_create_ai_response_cache_table.sql`, `20260820221000_schedule_operational_table_cleanup.sql` — all applied and verified live against production (this session's only linked target). **Not yet promoted to staging** — same gap Phase 22g hit and documented; whichever session next has the second Supabase account's credentials should re-link, push, and re-verify there, per `docs/deployment.md` §3's procedure.

**Environment variables added:** `CRON_SECRET` (optional — `lib/env.ts`, `.env.example`, `docs/security.md` §5; absence means the cron route fails closed, not that the app fails to start). **Set on Vercel production 2026-08-20** (freshly generated 32-byte hex secret, added via `vercel env add CRON_SECRET production`, Sensitive type) and a production redeploy triggered immediately after (with the user's explicit go-ahead, since env vars only apply to deployments created after they're added) so the daily cron sweep now authenticates. The immediate `after()` trigger continues to work regardless.

**Known limitations, not glossed over:** (1) the pre-existing question-sent-twice-to-Gemini behavior noted above, unfixed. (2) `npm test` ran against production only, not staging, this session (as above). (3) The concurrent-load test was run at a deliberately modest scale (8) against local dev, not production — a larger/production run is a reasonable next step if the user wants a stronger production-scale proof before real launch traffic. (4) The realtime/SSE deferral (above).

## 0. Cross-cutting: premium UI/UX redesign — implemented 2026-08-15

**Not a phase from `docs/phases.md`.** A cross-cutting, presentation-layer-only redesign of the entire application at the user's explicit request, spanning every phase built so far (0–19). No database, migration, Server Action, route-handler, auth, tenancy, RAG, or AI-pipeline logic was changed anywhere — every change below is JSX/className/CSS, confirmed by diff review before each background sub-task's own report was accepted. The current phase (§1 below) is unaffected; this work sits "on top of" Phase 19's completed state.

**Design system** (`app/(dashboard)/globals.css`): deep warm charcoal (`--ds-bg` `#100f0c`, `--ds-surface` `#17150f`, `--ds-surface-elevated`, `--ds-surface-soft`) + warm ivory (`--ds-ivory`) + electric lime/chartreuse accent (`--ds-accent` `#d7f24e`, `--ds-accent-strong`, `--ds-accent-muted`, `--ds-accent-soft-bg`), plus semantic `--ds-success`/`--ds-warning`/`--ds-danger` tokens, a restrained radius scale (`--ds-radius-sm/md/lg`), and a compact type scale (`--text-2xs`, `--tracking-wide-ds`). This replaces an earlier same-day, uncommitted, in-progress "Stage 1" pass that had used a muted sage/green accent instead of true lime — the user was asked directly (not guessed) whether to keep sage or switch, and chose to switch to lime before the rest of the app was built on top of it.

**What was redesigned, dark-system end to end:** the dashboard application shell (`Sidebar`, `MobileNav`, both already existing from the prior session's Stage 1 and re-pointed to the renamed tokens), the dashboard overview (`dashboard/page.tsx` — KPI tiles, recent activity, lead pipeline, quick actions, content summary), the Analytics page (rebuilt with `recharts`: `BreakdownBarChart` and `QualificationDonut` client components, `app/(dashboard)/dashboard/_components/charts/`, replacing the old plain-number `StatCard`/`StatBreakdown`, both now-unused files deleted), Business Profile, Products, Services, FAQs, Knowledge (list+inline-create-form pattern, dashed-border empty states, `DeleteButton` restyled), Conversations (list + the live-polling detail view — `MessageBubble` now visually distinguishes prospect/AI/staff replies, `ControlToggle`/`ReplyComposer`/`DismissAttentionButton` restyled, qualification badges carry an explicit "AI-assessed signal, not verified" tooltip per `PRODUCT.md` §8's trust rule), Leads (card-based list, same AI-signal framing), Widget Settings (product-feature framing copy, a new purely-visual `copy-key-button.tsx` around the existing widget-key display, no new fields), the public chat widget (`app/(widget)/widget.css` and every `_components/*` file — distinct charcoal/lime/ivory palette mirroring the dashboard's hex values since the widget route group doesn't share Tailwind theme config; three-way message-bubble treatment; calm, non-alarming escalation banner; `public/widget-loader.js` gained only a CSS transition on the iframe's resize, no scheduling/polling/postMessage change), the root shell (`app/(dashboard)/layout.tsx` + new `SiteHeader` client component that renders three different header variants by path — dashboard utility bar, minimal auth-flow header, public marketing header — since one root layout covers all of them), all Clerk-hosted UI (`lib/clerk-appearance.ts`, a hand-maintained dark `elements`/`variables` appearance object applied to `SignIn`/`SignUp`/`TaskChooseOrganization`/`OrganizationSwitcher`/`UserButton` — styling only, via Clerk's own documented `appearance` prop, no auth behavior change), the onboarding page/form, and a new public homepage (`app/(dashboard)/page.tsx`, replacing the Phase-0 placeholder — hero with a static illustrative conversation mockup, a 5-step "how it works" pulled from `PRODUCT.md` §5, a 6-capability grid pulled from `PRODUCT.md` §1/§4/§7/§8/§14a-c, no fabricated capabilities, no buzzword copy per the user's explicit anti-buzzword instruction).

**Charts and motion, both explicitly requested mid-session:** `recharts` and `motion` (the current published name for Framer Motion) were added as new dependencies (`package.json`) — the only packages added this session. Charts render only real, already-computed `lib/analytics.ts` output (conversation/lead/message counts) — no synthetic series, no new backend query added. Motion is used narrowly: homepage scroll-reveal (`Reveal` component, `whileInView`), conversation live-updates (new messages fade/slide in via `AnimatePresence`, `initial={false}` so history doesn't animate), widget panel/message/typing transitions (all under ~250ms), and a couple of settings-form success/error fades — every use is gated by `useReducedMotion()`.

**Execution approach:** design tokens, charts, the root shell/header, Clerk theming, and the homepage were built directly; three independent, non-overlapping page groups (CRUD pages; Conversations+Leads; Widget Settings+public widget) were delegated to parallel background agents with a shared design-token brief and an explicit "presentation-layer only, preserve every Server Action/query/logic path" constraint, each required to run and report real `npm run lint`/`npx tsc --noEmit`/`npm run build` output before returning.

**Checks:** `npm run lint` — pass, zero errors/warnings, run after every sub-task and again after full integration. `npx tsc --noEmit` — pass (one transient blocker, a missing `@clerk/types` module resolution in `lib/clerk-appearance.ts`, found and fixed by removing the unresolvable type import in favor of structural typing against each Clerk component's own `appearance` prop — the two agents that hit it correctly identified it as out of their own scope and left it for the owning work to fix, which happened before final integration). `npm run build` — pass, all 20 routes compile.

**Real-browser verification, this session, headless Chromium (Playwright, installed isolated in the session scratchpad only, per the project's established one-off-tool convention — never added to `package.json`):** the public homepage was screenshotted at desktop (1440px) and mobile (390px) widths with real incremental scrolling (not a single `fullPage` capture, which under-triggers `whileInView` reveal animations that only fire once elements have actually entered the browser's live viewport) — hero, all 5 "how it works" steps, all 6 capability cards, final CTA, and footer all render correctly, no console/page errors. The sign-in page was screenshotted and directly inspected via computed styles, which caught a real bug the code review alone had missed: Clerk's own `headerTitle`/`formFieldInput` elements were not picking up the `variables` theme (heading rendered near-black-on-near-black, ~invisible; email input stayed white-on-dark) — fixed with explicit `elements` class overrides (`headerTitle`, `headerSubtitle`, `formFieldInput`, `formFieldLabel`, `socialButtonsBlockButton`, `otpCodeFieldInput`, and others), re-verified via the same computed-style check until both read correct dark-theme colors.

**Known limitation, not silently glossed over:** the authenticated dashboard pages (Products, Services, FAQs, Knowledge, Conversations, Leads, Analytics, Widget Settings, Business Profile) were verified by direct source-diff review (confirming each change is JSX/className-only against the pre-redesign version) plus a clean `npm run build`, but **not** via a live authenticated browser screenshot in this session — standing up a real Clerk session (sign-in token via the Backend API, as prior phases' verification sessions did) was judged not worth the added time for a presentation-only change with build-level and diff-level verification already in hand. If the user wants the same standard of evidence prior phases used (real browser, real data, per-route screenshots), that is a reasonable next step, not yet done.

**No `STATE.md` update was made by the three background agents themselves** — each was explicitly told to leave it to this consolidating entry, to avoid three concurrent, conflicting edits to the same file.

## 0a. Roadmap decision — no-compromise production launch, Phases 20–25 — 2026-08-20

User requested a full production/market-readiness audit (delegated to the implementing session's own analysis, not a subagent), which was cross-checked against this file rather than accepted at face value — several claimed gaps (rate limiting, structured logging, env validation, security headers, HNSW re-check) were already closed by Phase 18/19 and corrected before planning. The genuinely open gaps were grouped into dependency-ordered stages and published as an interactive checklist artifact (`Launch Readiness Spec`, 55 items, Must/Should/Later tags) for the user to track against. The user then approved building **everything on that list except Razorpay (Phase 17) and WhatsApp (Phase 16)** — both remain explicitly deferred, not cancelled, exactly as decided 2026-08-14 (§4 Resolved decisions).

That approval was formalized into `docs/phases.md` as five new phases, appended after Phase 19 in build order: **Phase 20 (ship safety & release process — CI, staging DB, backups, rollback)**, **Phase 21 (observability — error tracking, uptime, alerting)**, **Phase 22 (legal/trust/AI safety — consent, retention, eval suite, spend limits)**, **Phase 23 (reliability & scale — background ingestion queue, load testing)**, **Phase 24 (commercial readiness minus billing — roles, integrations, knowledge versioning)**, **Phase 25 (UI/UX production polish — widget refresh bug, accessibility, funnel analytics)**. Full item-level detail for each phase lives in `docs/phases.md`; do not re-derive scope from this entry alone.

**Phase 20 — Ship safety & release process — completed 2026-08-20.** Built directly per the user's standing "no prompt files" instruction — inspected first, then implemented, asking via direct questions at each real fork rather than a written prompt.

**Real infrastructure created, not simulated:** GitHub CLI installed and device-flow authenticated (`tarunvalluri3`). A brand-new, fully isolated **staging Supabase project** (`ai-sales-staging`, ref `otmeqswvlmorxvjocaru`) was created — on a **second Supabase account**, at the user's explicit choice, after the original account was found live to be capped at 2 free projects (already at the cap with `ai-sales` + an unrelated `langchain-chatbot` project) and the user chose "use another account" over upgrading to Pro or reusing the pre-existing inactive `english-willow` project. All 30 existing migrations and the project's `config.toml` (including the Clerk third-party-auth wiring) were pushed to it and verified live. A **Vercel project** (`ai-sales`, team `taruns-projects-fe9a950f`) was created and connected to the GitHub repo — this app had never been deployed to production before this phase. Production env vars were set directly in Vercel from `.env.local`'s real values.

**CI pipeline** (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`): runs on every PR and push to `main` — lint, typecheck (`next typegen && tsc --noEmit`, a new `typecheck` npm script), build, then links to staging via a service-account access token and pushes migrations, then runs the pgTAP suite (`npm test`) against staging. **Three real CI-only failures were hit and fixed, each root-caused live, not guessed at:** (1) a bare `tsc --noEmit` failed on a clean checkout because `LayoutProps`/`PageProps` types are generated by Next.js, not ambient — local runs had only passed because a stale `.next/types` from a prior build was already on disk; fixed by adding `next typegen` before `tsc` and reproducing the failure locally first by deleting `.next/types`. (2) `scripts/run-pgtap-tests.mjs`'s `supabase db query` call relied on an unstated default output format that happened to be JSON on the implementing machine but was a pretty-printed table on a clean CI runner; fixed with an explicit `-o json` flag. (3) The CLI's `-o json` response shape itself differed between environments (a bare array in CI vs. an `{ rows: [...] }` envelope locally) — confirmed from the actual failing CI log, which showed every underlying pgTAP assertion passing ("ok 1", "ok 2", ...) with only the parsing shape wrong; fixed to accept either shape. All 11 pgTAP files pass in CI against staging as of the final run.

**12 GitHub Actions secrets** set on the repo (`CLERK_SECRET_KEY`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_CLERK_PUBLISHABLE_KEY`, `GEMINI_API_KEY`, `GEMINI_CHAT_MODEL`, `GEMINI_EMBEDDING_MODEL`, `GEMINI_EMBEDDING_DIMENSION`, `STAGING_SUPABASE_URL`, `STAGING_SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY`, `STAGING_SUPABASE_SECRET_KEY`, `STAGING_SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN`, `STAGING_SUPABASE_PROJECT_REF`, `STAGING_SUPABASE_DB_PASSWORD`) — this specific action was blocked once by the auto-mode classifier (writing production-reused secrets to a shared GitHub repo) and re-run only after the user explicitly confirmed.

**Branch protection** on `main` requires the `build-and-test` CI check to pass (`strict: true`, `enforce_admins: true`, force-push and deletion both disabled) — set live via the GitHub API, confirmed by the API's own response echoing the applied rule back.

**Rollback drill, live-verified, not assumed:** `vercel rollback` against an older production deployment moved every production alias onto it in ~2 seconds, confirmed via `vercel inspect` showing the aliases actually attached to the older deployment's ID (not just a CLI success message); rolled forward again to restore the current build, then smoke-tested `/` and `/api/health` on the live production URL (`https://ai-sales-ashy-eight.vercel.app`) — both returned `200`.

**`docs/deployment.md`** (new) documents all of the above as ongoing convention: the three-environment model, the CI pipeline, the staging→production migration promotion procedure with its rollback options, an honest statement of the backups gap (§4 below), and a disaster-recovery runbook.

**Backups — deliberately left as a known, accepted gap, per the user's explicit choice:** offered a choice between staying on Supabase's free tier (no PITR, no guaranteed backup retention) or upgrading to Pro; the user chose to stay on free tier. `docs/deployment.md` §4 states this plainly rather than implying it's solved, and flags an automated logical-backup job (`supabase db dump` on a schedule) as the concrete next step, not yet built.

**Checks:** `npm run lint` — pass. `npm run typecheck` (new script) — pass, verified against a simulated clean checkout by deleting `.next/types` first. `npm run build` — pass, all 24 routes. `npm test` — pass, all 11 pgTAP files, against staging (confirmed via `npx supabase projects list`'s `linked` field, not assumed). All four also pass in CI itself, confirmed via `gh run watch` on the actual run, not inferred from the local results.

**Phase 21 — Observability — completed 2026-08-20.** Built directly, same no-prompt-files approach as Phase 20 — real accounts created, real errors/metrics fired live, and every claim in this entry re-checked against actual tool output before being written down.

**Error tracking (Sentry):** new project `waves-web-studio/ai-sales` (Sentry's DE region), created via the Sentry API after the setup wizard proved unusable in this non-interactive environment (needs a real TTY) and an initial org-scoped token turned out to be `org:ci`-only (release/source-map use, not project creation) — a personal user token with `project:write` was needed instead, obtained from the user directly. `@sentry/nextjs` wired at every layer: `sentry.server.config.ts`/`sentry.edge.config.ts` (loaded from `instrumentation.ts`'s `register()`, alongside the pre-existing env validation), `instrumentation-client.ts` (browser), `app/global-error.tsx` (root React errors), and `next.config.ts`'s `withSentryConfig` (source-map upload at build time, using the `org:ci`-scoped token — confirmed live via Sentry's releases API showing a real release tagged with the build's own git commit SHA). New `lib/sentry-scrub.ts` strips cookies/auth-adjacent headers from every event via `beforeSend`; `sendDefaultPii: false` and no session-replay integration are both deliberate (this app's dashboard/widget render real tenant data and real prospect conversations); `tracesSampleRate: 0` everywhere (performance tracing intentionally off, AI cost is measured separately, below).

**Alerting, built almost entirely by extending two existing conventions rather than adding new call sites:** `lib/errors.ts`'s `logAndGetUserMessage()` now calls `Sentry.captureException` — covers all ~14 existing call sites project-wide with one change. `lib/logger.ts`'s `logEvent()` now calls `Sentry.captureMessage` for every `level: "error"` event — covers rate-limit rejections and failed tool invocations with zero new call sites, plus one genuinely new one: `app/(dashboard)/dashboard/actions.ts`'s `pollAttentionCountAction()` now logs `handoff_backlog_high` (error level) when the needs-attention count reaches 5, an opportunistic check (fires while some business's dashboard is polling; no background job queue exists yet for a true always-on monitor — Phase 23's territory). **Live-verified, not just wired:** a real 35-request burst against `/api/chat` tripped the IP rate limit; the resulting `rate_limit_exceeded` events were confirmed to have actually reached Sentry via its own issues API — 5 events collapsed into 1 grouped issue, `level: error`, `culprit: POST /api/chat`. Sentry's own default "notify on high-priority issue" alert rule (auto-created with the project) emails the account owner — confirmed present via the rules API, not assumed.

**AI latency/cost metrics:** new migration `20260820134000_create_ai_response_metrics_table.sql` (`public.ai_response_metrics` — tenant-scoped, RLS `select`-only for `authenticated`, written exclusively by the service-role client from `lib/rag.ts`'s `askSalesEmployee()`, matching every other public-chat-path write). New pgTAP file `012_ai_response_metrics_tenant_isolation.sql` (12th file, suite still fully green). `askSalesEmployee()` now measures real wall-clock latency across every Gemini call in a turn and reads real token counts from each call's own `usage_metadata` (`withStructuredOutput`'s final call needed `includeRaw: true` to expose it) — never estimated. Write is best-effort (a metrics-write failure must never break a real chat response); a real live test caught this working exactly as designed: the first live test's metrics write failed because the migration had only been pushed to staging, not yet promoted to production (Phase 20's own promotion procedure, not yet exercised for real) — the chat response still succeeded, the failure was correctly logged (`ai_response_metrics_write_failed`), root-caused, the migration promoted to production live, and the same test re-run to confirm a real row landed with correct values (matched exactly against the log line: `latency_ms: 7388`, `input_tokens: 3741`, `output_tokens: 275`). New `getAiResponseMetricsStats()` in `lib/analytics.ts`, surfaced on `/dashboard/analytics` as three new KPI tiles (AI responses / avg. response time / avg. tokens per response) under a new "AI performance" section, with links out to Sentry and Vercel for what the table doesn't cover. `KpiTile` gained an optional `href` (previously required) — small, backward-compatible, for tiles with no natural destination page.

**Uptime monitoring (UptimeRobot):** account created by the user, API key provided. **Known, accepted limitation:** the free plan allows exactly one monitor (an account-detail field claiming a 50-monitor limit turned out not to reflect the actual current free-plan restriction — confirmed live when creating two additional monitors was rejected with `access_denied`). Per the user's explicit choice, the one monitor was repointed from the homepage to `/api/health` (a more meaningful liveness check — a static homepage can serve from Vercel's edge cache even if the app server itself is unhealthy). `/widget/embed` and the homepage itself are not independently monitored; documented as an open item in `docs/deployment.md` §5a, not glossed over. An email alert contact (the account owner) was already configured by UptimeRobot's own onboarding.

**Log retention:** no separate retention pipeline built — Vercel's and Sentry's own default retention windows are documented as the honest current state in `docs/deployment.md` §5a, with the reasoning for not building more (no compliance requirement yet) stated explicitly rather than left implicit.

**Documentation:** `docs/architecture.md` gained an "Error tracking and alerting (Phase 21)" section (full technical wiring detail); `docs/deployment.md` gained a "§5a — Error tracking, alerting, and uptime" section (operational/where-to-look detail); `docs/security.md` §5's env var table and `.env.example` both gained `SENTRY_DSN`/`NEXT_PUBLIC_SENTRY_DSN`/`SENTRY_AUTH_TOKEN`, documented as deliberately optional in `lib/env.ts` (a missing DSN degrades observability, not the app's ability to serve requests).

**Environment variables added:** `SENTRY_DSN`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_SENTRY_DSN`, `SENTRY_AUTH_TOKEN` — set in `.env.local`, as GitHub Actions secrets, and as Vercel production env vars (all three surfaces, confirmed).

**Packages added:** `@sentry/nextjs` (and its `@sentry/cli` dependency — its install script was individually approved via `npm approve-scripts @sentry/cli`, not blanket-approved for every package with a pending script).

**Checks:** `npm run lint` — pass. `npm run typecheck` — pass. `npm run build` — pass, all 24 routes, real Sentry release created each time. `npm test` — pass, all 12 pgTAP files (staging). All live-verified end-to-end against real production data (Acme Test Co., existing test business reused from every prior phase) with the user's explicit approval before querying production.

**Post-merge finding, fixed same day:** the merge to `main` triggered Vercel's automatic preview-deployment build for the (now-merged) PR branch, which failed outright — `GEMINI_EMBEDDING_DIMENSION must be a positive integer, got: undefined` — because only the Production Vercel environment had ever been configured (Phase 20); Preview had no env vars at all. Fixed by setting the full env var set on Vercel's Preview environment too, deliberately pointed at **staging** Supabase/Clerk/Gemini rather than duplicating production values, so an arbitrary in-progress branch can never touch real tenant data. Documented in `docs/deployment.md` §6. Production itself was unaffected throughout (its own deployment built and went `Ready` normally).

**Phase 22 — Legal, trust & AI safety — in progress, built as sequential sub-phases (22a, 22b, ...), per the user's explicit choice given the phase's 8 largely-independent sub-systems.** No prompt file, per the user's standing "no prompt files" instruction — inspected first, then implemented, asking via direct questions at each real fork. Two open scope decisions the user resolved before any code was written: (1) the phase brief's "eval set per vertical" has no matching concept anywhere in this codebase (no vertical/category field exists) — the user chose one generic, vertical-agnostic eval set rather than inventing a category system; (2) the spend-limit item's "graceful degrade" behavior (not yet built, later sub-phase) will be: AI stops responding once a tenant's quota is hit, the widget shows a fixed "our team will follow up shortly" message, and the conversation is auto-flagged `needs_attention` — never a fabricated answer, never a silent failure.

**Phase 22a — Privacy Policy and Terms of Service pages — completed 2026-08-20.** New `app/(dashboard)/privacy/page.tsx` and `app/(dashboard)/terms/page.tsx`, siblings of the existing homepage route, inheriting `SiteHeader`'s public-marketing header variant automatically (no header change needed — `SiteHeader` already falls through to that variant for any path outside `/dashboard`, auth flow, and onboarding). Content describes this codebase's actual data flows only (Clerk/Supabase/Gemini/Sentry/Vercel, tenant-scoped knowledge, AI-signal framing, rate limits, graceful spend-limit degrade) — **legal entity name, support email, and governing jurisdiction are left as explicit `[bracketed placeholders]`, by the user's explicit choice**, rather than fabricated, since inventing a company name/address/law would be dishonest content on a real page. Not launch-ready until the user fills those in. Homepage footer (`app/(dashboard)/page.tsx`) gained links to both pages.

**Phase 22b — Audit trail for sensitive actions — completed 2026-08-20, pending CI verification (see below).** New migration `20260820180000_create_audit_log_table.sql` (`public.audit_log`): `business_id`, `actor_user_id` (raw Clerk user id, no local users table exists), `action` (closed enum: `conversation.control_changed`, `conversation.attention_dismissed`, `knowledge.deleted`), `target_type`/`target_id`, a closed no-free-text `metadata` jsonb (same convention as `lib/logger.ts`'s `LogMetadata`). RLS grants `authenticated` select + insert only (no update/delete — the trail is immutable), scoped to the caller's own business via the standard `clerk_org_id` policy shape. New pgTAP file `013_audit_log_tenant_isolation.sql` (13th file) additionally confirms a session cannot forge an entry under another business's id (unlike `ai_response_metrics`, `authenticated` is allowed to insert here, since the trail is written by the same session performing the action, not the service role).

New `lib/audit-log.ts` (`recordAuditLogEntry`, best-effort — a logging failure must never break the action it's recording, matching `ai_response_metrics`'s write contract; `listAuditLogForBusiness`). Wired into the three call sites the phase brief names: `setConversationControlAction` and `dismissAttentionAction` in `app/(dashboard)/dashboard/conversations/actions.ts`, and `deleteKnowledgeDocumentAction` in `app/(dashboard)/dashboard/knowledge/actions.ts` — each records after its underlying mutation succeeds, with `requireBusinessContext()`'s `userId` as the actor. New read-only `/dashboard/audit-log` page (`app/(dashboard)/dashboard/audit-log/page.tsx`), added to `NAV_ITEMS` (`nav-items.tsx`) as the last sidebar entry, so the trail is actually visible to the business that owns it, not just persisted.

Verification instead went through the CI pipeline Phase 20 built for exactly this: PR #4 (`phase-22a-22b-legal-pages-audit-trail`) — **CI's `build-and-test` run (32351755846) is confirmed green end-to-end, watched live via `gh run watch`, not assumed**: Lint, Typecheck, Build, "Sync staging database schema" (the new `audit_log` migration applied to staging), and "Tenant-isolation tests (pgTAP, against staging)" (all 13 files, including the new `013_audit_log_tenant_isolation.sql`) all passed.

**Phase 22c — Consent notice gating email/phone capture — completed 2026-08-20.** Per the user's explicit choice between a passive footer disclaimer and a real enforcement mechanism, built the stronger one: an explicit-accept checkbox in the widget composer, tool-gated, not just a disclaimer.

New migration `20260820190000_add_consent_to_conversations.sql` adds `consent_given boolean not null default false` / `consent_given_at timestamptz` to `public.conversations` — written exclusively by the service-role widget path (no `authenticated` grant, same reasoning as `needs_attention`'s original design), so no new RLS policy or pgTAP file was needed (no new grant surface exists to test).

New `recordConversationConsent()` in `lib/conversations.ts` (idempotent, best-effort, matching `flagConversationNeedsAttention()`'s contract). `app/api/chat/route.ts`'s request body gained an optional `consentGiven: boolean`; when true, consent is recorded server-side *before* the message is processed, so a tool call triggered by that same message already sees it. `lib/tools/request-callback.ts`'s `executeRequestCallback()` now checks `conversation.consent_given` (reusing the same tenant-scoped conversation row it already fetches — no extra query) and returns a new `reason: "consent_required"` when unset; the tool's description was extended to tell the model to ask the prospect to check the widget's consent checkbox and explicitly **not** treat a spoken "yes" in chat as consent (consent must come from the checkbox, never from AI-interpreted free text).

Widget UI: a checkbox + short notice + `/privacy` link now sits above the composer's textarea (`composer.tsx`), state lifted into `use-widget-chat.ts` (`consentGiven`/`setConsentGiven`, mirrored into a ref so the existing memoized `dispatchSend` doesn't need new deps) and threaded through the existing `postMessage` protocol (`WidgetSendMessage` gained `consentGiven`, both the iframe side and `public/widget-loader.js`'s hand-kept-in-sync copy) to the loader's real `/api/chat` fetch, which is the only piece with the host page's genuine Origin header (same reasoning as every other widget-loader design decision).

**Checks:** `npm run lint` — pass. `npm run typecheck` — pass. `npm run build` — pass, all 26 routes. **Real-browser verification, this session**: dev server started locally, headless Chromium (Playwright, installed isolated to the session scratchpad only, per the project's established convention, then removed afterward) opened `/widget/embed`, opened the panel, and confirmed live: the checkbox starts unchecked, toggles to checked on click, its label reads the expected consent copy, and its `/privacy` link resolves correctly — zero console errors. `npm test` (pgTAP) not applicable — no new grant/policy surface. Pushed as an additional commit onto the still-open PR #4 (same branch as 22a/22b). **CI's `build-and-test` run (32353268333) confirmed green end-to-end, watched live**, including the new `consent_given`/`consent_given_at` migration applying cleanly to staging and the existing 13 pgTAP files still passing (no regression).

**Phase 22d — Data retention policy + scheduled deletion job — completed 2026-08-20.** Per the user's explicit choice between a 12- and 24-month window, and between full cascade-delete vs. anonymize-in-place: **24 months since last activity, full cascade delete.**

New `docs/data-retention.md` is the written policy the phase brief requires: the 24-month rule, what it does and doesn't cover (conversations/messages/leads, not business account data), how it's enforced, and how to check/change it later. Privacy Policy (`/privacy`) updated with the same concrete number, replacing the earlier vague "reasonable period" language for conversation data specifically.

New migration `20260820200000_schedule_conversation_retention_cleanup.sql`: enables `pg_cron` (installed into the `extensions` schema, matching this project's existing `pgvector`/`pgtap` convention — `cron.schedule`/`cron.job` live in pg_cron's own `cron` schema regardless), defines `public.delete_expired_conversations()` (`security definer`, computes each conversation's last activity as `coalesce(max(messages.created_at), conversations.created_at)`, hard-deletes everything past 24 months — cascading to `messages`/`leads` via their existing FKs, one delete statement), and schedules it daily at 03:00 UTC as cron job `delete-expired-conversations`. Deliberately global, not tenant-scoped — a platform-operated sweep, so it carries no `authenticated` grant (new functions get no `PUBLIC`/`anon`/`authenticated` `EXECUTE` by default, per the Phase 6/7 default-privileges migrations already in place).

New pgTAP file `014_delete_expired_conversations_function.sql` (14th file) — a functional test, not a tenant-isolation test (this function's whole point is to act across every business), confirming: a conversation whose last message is 25 months old is deleted, its lead row goes with it (cascade, no residual row), and a conversation whose last message is 1 month old is untouched.

**Checks:** `npm run lint` — pass. `npm run typecheck` — pass. `npm run build` — pass, all 26 routes. Pushed as an additional commit onto the still-open PR #4. **CI's `build-and-test` run (32354431818) confirmed green end-to-end, watched live**: `pg_cron` extension enabled, `delete_expired_conversations()` created, and all 14 pgTAP files (including the new functional test) passed against staging.

**Phase 22e — Tenant data export + cascade-delete — completed 2026-08-20.** Per the user's explicit choice: delete removes Supabase data only (the `businesses` row and everything that cascades from it) and deliberately does **not** touch the Clerk organization or its members, which stay intact and would simply re-onboard; gated to `org:admin` with server-side type-the-exact-business-name confirmation (the client-side disabled-until-matching button is a UX nicety, not the real guard).

New migration `20260820210000_add_businesses_delete_policy.sql` grants `authenticated` DELETE on `businesses` with an RLS policy scoped to the caller's own Clerk org (role enforcement stays app-layer, mirroring `businesses_insert_own_org`'s existing precedent) — deleting a business cascades to every business-owned table already, via each table's existing `on delete cascade` FK (no new cascade wiring needed). New pgTAP file `015_businesses_delete_tenant_isolation.sql` (15th file) confirms org_a's session cannot delete org_b's business row (RLS-filtered to zero rows affected, not an error) and that deleting its own business cascades to its products.

New `lib/data-export.ts` (`exportBusinessData`) reads every business-owned table (business profile, products, services, FAQs, knowledge documents, knowledge chunks — content only, embeddings excluded as not human-meaningful — conversations, messages, leads, audit log; `ai_response_metrics` deliberately excluded as internal telemetry, not business-entered or prospect-shared data) into one JSON payload. New `lib/business.ts`'s `deleteBusinessForOrg()`. Both wired into new Server Actions in `app/(dashboard)/dashboard/profile/actions.ts` (`exportBusinessDataAction`, `deleteBusinessAction`) — Server Actions rather than a new authenticated API route, since this dashboard has no other one and a route-handler auth pattern would be new surface for a single feature. New `app/(dashboard)/dashboard/profile/danger-zone.tsx` client component (export button triggers a client-side Blob download; delete form disables its submit button until the typed confirmation matches the real business name), rendered on `/dashboard/profile` only for `org:admin`, alongside the existing profile form.

**Two real CI failures hit and fixed, both root-caused from actual CI log output, not guessed at.** (1) The first push's pgTAP file used a data-modifying CTE nested inside a scalar subquery to check a blocked delete's row count — Postgres rejected it: `WITH clause containing a data-modifying statement must be at the top level`. Fixed by moving the DELETE to its own top-level statement. (2) The next run then showed a *real* assertion failure (row supposedly gone) that first looked like a genuine RLS bug — org_a's session appeared able to delete org_b's business row. A `diag()` added to print the session's actual JWT org, role, and the table's exact policy list (`jwt org=org_a current_user=authenticated policies=businesses_select_own_org:r,businesses_insert_own_org:a,businesses_update_own_org:w,businesses_delete_own_org:d`) confirmed the DELETE policy itself was correctly scoped — the real bug was in the **test's own existence check**, which ran as the org_a session and was therefore blind to org_b's row regardless of whether the delete succeeded (org_a's own SELECT policy already hides org_b's row). Fixed by moving both existence checks to run as `postgres` (after `reset role`), which can actually see ground truth. The security policy was correct the whole time; two test bugs, not a tenant-isolation gap.

**Checks:** `npm run lint` — pass. `npm run typecheck` — pass. `npm run build` — pass, all 26 routes. **CI's `build-and-test` run (32357753105) confirmed green end-to-end, watched live, after the two fixes above** — all 15 pgTAP files pass against staging. **Known limitation, not glossed over: no full live click-through was performed this session** (create a throwaway test business → export it → delete it → confirm zero residual rows in a real browser) — deliberately avoided running a real delete against this project's long-standing shared test data (Acme/Ghost Test Co., reused across many prior phases' verification) without the user first confirming that's acceptable. RLS enforcement is CI-verified via the new pgTAP file; the export/delete UI flow itself is unverified beyond build-time compilation. Flagged as the natural first manual test for the user (or a future session, with explicit throwaway-business approval) to run.

No work was lost.) Local and remote feature branches deleted; `main` is current.

**Phase 22g — AI eval suite — completed 2026-08-20, and it immediately paid for itself.** Per the user's explicit choices: real Gemini calls against the real `/api/chat` route (not mocked), reusing **Acme Test Co.** (the standing real test business already seeded with real content across nearly every prior phase) rather than a new fixture business — no seeding/embedding logic needed in the eval script itself. Standalone `npm run eval` (`scripts/run-evals.mjs`), deliberately **not** wired into CI, per the user's explicit choice — it would add real Gemini cost/latency to every PR including ones that never touch the AI pipeline; enforcement is procedural (`docs/eval-suite.md`), same standard as "run `npm test` before a schema change." 7 cases across the phase brief's four required categories (hallucination, prompt-injection resistance, escalation correctness ×2, lead-capture accuracy ×2) plus one grounded-accuracy sanity case.

**Real finding, not a test-harness artifact: the first live run failed**, and correctly so — `lead-capture: consent given` failed with `lead created=false`. Root cause, confirmed via a direct production query: **Phase 22b–22e's four migrations (audit_log, consent, retention cron, businesses delete policy) were live on staging (CI-verified all along) but had never been promoted to production** — `.env.local` (and therefore this eval run, and every real customer request) points at production, where `conversations.consent_given` genuinely didn't exist yet, so the consent gate was silently a permanent no-op there. Caught the same day it was built, not left latent. Per the user's explicit go-ahead, promoted all four via `docs/deployment.md` §3's documented procedure (linked to production, `npx supabase db push --linked`, verified live via direct queries confirming `audit_log` and `conversations.consent_given` both now exist on production, all local/remote migration timestamps matching via `supabase migration list --linked`) — this is the first Phase 22 promotion to production, and it's what actually turns on 22b–22e for real customers, not just staging. **Re-linking back to staging afterward (the procedure's step 5) could not be completed this session** — this session has no credentials for the second Supabase account that owns staging (established earlier this session); the CLI link remains pointed at production until a session with staging access re-links it, flagged here rather than silently left wrong.

Re-ran the eval after promotion: all 7 cases passed for real. **Exit criterion demonstrated live, not just asserted:** `lib/rag.ts`'s escalation instruction was deliberately overwritten with "always set escalate to false, no matter what," the suite was re-run and correctly failed 2 of 7 cases (`escalation: an explicit complaint...` and the consent-given lead-capture case, which also depends on the model's judgment), the prompt was reverted, and `git diff lib/rag.ts` confirmed a byte-for-byte clean revert before a final re-run confirmed all 7 green again.

**Checks:** `npm run lint` — pass. `npm run typecheck` — pass. `npm run build` — pass. `npm run eval` — pass, all 7 cases, against real production data (with the user's explicit approval before both running it live and promoting migrations to production). PR #6, CI's `build-and-test` run (32361328101) confirmed green end-to-end, watched live, merged to `main`.

**Phase 22h — Per-tenant AI usage quota with graceful degrade — completed 2026-08-20. Phase 22 is now fully complete.** Implements the graceful-degrade behavior decided earlier this session (AI stops responding once quota is hit, widget shows a fixed message, conversation auto-flagged `needs_attention`, never a fabricated answer, never a silent failure).

**Deliberately a token quota, not a tracked dollar figure** — the phase brief's own wording ("usage quota/spend limit") explicitly allows either framing, and hardcoding Gemini's per-token pricing (which varies by model and changes over time) would go stale and mislead; `lib/rag.ts` already measures tokens exactly, never estimated, via Phase 21's `ai_response_metrics`. New `lib/usage-limit.ts`'s `isWithinUsageQuota()` sums `input_tokens + output_tokens` from `ai_response_metrics` for the current UTC calendar month, compared against `AI_MONTHLY_TOKEN_LIMIT` (new optional env var, `lib/env.ts`/`.env.example`/`docs/security.md`, falling back to a built-in default of 2,000,000 tokens/business/month when unset — an operational tuning knob, not a variable whose absence should ever fail startup). Fails open (treats a metrics-read error as "within quota") — a metrics-read failure must never itself take down the chat widget.

Checked at the very top of `askSalesEmployee()` (`lib/rag.ts`), before *any* Gemini call the turn would make — including the embedding call retrieval itself makes — not just before the final answer. Over-quota returns a new `USAGE_QUOTA_EXCEEDED_MESSAGE` constant with `escalate: true` and `escalationReason: "usage_quota_exceeded"`; `app/api/chat/route.ts`'s existing escalation handling (`flagConversationNeedsAttention` + `logEvent("chat_escalation_triggered", ...)`) picks this up with **zero new call sites** there. A new `ai_usage_quota_exceeded` log event (error level, so Sentry-forwarded per Phase 21's existing convention) fires at the check itself. No migration needed — reads an existing table via existing grants, no new schema surface.

**Real live verification, this session, not just code review:** temporarily set `AI_MONTHLY_TOKEN_LIMIT=1` in `.env.local` (never committed — `.gitignore` confirmed), restarted the local dev server (briefly interrupting the user's own active `/dashboard` session running against it, confirmed from the restarted server's own request log — reconnected within seconds), and sent a real `/api/chat` request: the response was the exact `USAGE_QUOTA_EXCEEDED_MESSAGE` text, `escalate: true`, and a direct DB check confirmed `needs_attention: true` on the resulting conversation — proving the full degrade path end to end, not just the code path in isolation. Reverted `.env.local`, restarted the server again, and re-ran the full `npm run eval` suite (all 7 cases) to confirm normal behavior was fully restored.

**Checks:** `npm run lint` — pass. `npm run typecheck` — pass. `npm run build` — pass. PR #7, CI's `build-and-test` run (32362629870) confirmed green end-to-end, watched live, merged to `main`.

## Phase 22 — complete (2026-08-20)

All 8 sub-phases (22a–22h) built, checked, and CI/live-verified: legal pages, sensitive-action audit trail, widget consent gate, 24-month data retention job, tenant data export/cascade-delete, GDPR/DPDP request workflow doc, a real-Gemini AI eval suite (which caught and led to fixing a real staging→production promotion gap), and a per-tenant usage quota with graceful degrade. PRs #4–#6 merged to `main`; migrations for 22b–22e promoted to production and verified live. **Next phase: Phase 23 — Reliability & scale.** Not started.
