## 1. Current phase

**Phase 16 (WhatsApp) and Phase 17 (Razorpay) — explicitly deferred by user decision, 2026-08-14.** Not started, not silently dropped, not cancelled — they remain available to schedule later, consistent with `PRODUCT.md` §10's existing "out of scope until explicitly scheduled" framing for both (`docs/phases.md`'s Phase 16/17 entries and phase ordering are unedited and still correctly describe them). This is a phase-order reorder only, made under `AGENTS.md` §4's allowance for a user-directed reorder; it changes no security rule in `AGENTS.md` §3, which still applies unconditionally to Phase 18 and everything after it. Full detail in the Resolved decisions table, §4.

**Phase 18 — Analytics / monitoring — implemented and fully verified 2026-08-14.** Built as a single end-to-end prompt (`prompts/phase-18-analytics-monitoring.md`), per the new D11 standing-process rule. Scoped, per the user's explicit answers to a pre-prompt clarifying question, to (1) in-app business analytics and (2) server-side structured logging only — no third-party service, no new dependency, no new secret, no database migration.

**In-app analytics:** new `lib/analytics.ts` (`getConversationVolumeStats`, `getMessageVolumeStats`, `getLeadStats` — all read-only, `count`-only queries, `business_id`-filtered in addition to RLS) plus reuse of the existing `countConversationsNeedingAttention()`. New `/dashboard/analytics` page (protected via `requireBusinessContext()`, same convention as every other dashboard page) shows: total/7-day/30-day conversation counts, conversations currently needing attention, a conversion-rate percentage (`leads.total / conversations.total`, guarded to `0%` when there are no conversations), and three breakdown cards (leads by qualification, leads by status, messages by role) via a new `StatBreakdown` component (semantic `<dl>`/`<dt>`/`<dd>` pairing, per the approved prompt's accessibility requirement). `StatCard` gained an optional `suffix` prop (used for the `%` on the conversion-rate tile) — a small, backward-compatible extension, not a new component. `NAV_ITEMS` gained "Analytics" between "Leads" and "Widget Settings," reusing the file's existing hand-drawn inline-SVG icon convention; nav active-state/keyboard behavior needed no changes, since `Sidebar`/`MobileNav` already derive it generically from `NAV_ITEMS`.

**Structured logging:** new `lib/logger.ts` exports `logEvent(event, businessId, metadata?, level?)`, a single-JSON-line logger with a closed metadata type (`string | number | boolean | null` only — no free-text field, so prospect message content/contact info/tool input arguments cannot be passed through it even by mistake) and a `try`/`catch` around serialization so a logging call itself can never throw. Replaced every prior ad hoc `console.log`/`console.error` business-event call with `logEvent()` in `lib/tools/check-product-details.ts`, `lib/tools/check-faq-topic.ts`, `lib/tools/request-callback.ts`, and `lib/rag.ts`'s unrecognized-tool-call branch — logging outcomes/enum strings only (e.g. `result: "found"`), never the free-text argument (product name/FAQ topic) that triggered the call, a deliberate narrowing versus the prior ad hoc lines. `app/api/chat/route.ts` gained `logEvent()` calls at the escalation branch (`chat_escalation_triggered`, alongside the existing `flagConversationNeedsAttention()` call, no behavior change) and at all three rate-limit `429` return points (`rate_limit_exceeded`, tagged by `scope`) — the `ip`-scope rejection logs `businessId: "unknown"` rather than the raw IP, since the IP is never resolved to a business yet at that point and must never appear in a log line. `lib/errors.ts`'s `logAndGetUserMessage()` was deliberately left unmodified (general error logging, a separate, larger-blast-radius concern — see the prompt's Decision 3). `docs/architecture.md` gained a "Structured event logging (Phase 18)" section documenting the convention for future call sites.

`npm run lint` — pass, zero errors/warnings. `npx tsc --noEmit` — pass. `npm run build` — pass; `/dashboard/analytics` compiles and is listed in the route manifest alongside every other dashboard route.

**Full real-evidence verification, same day (2026-08-14), all 8 manual test steps PASS — run by the implementing session itself, ahead of the user's own pass, at the user's explicit request (same standard of evidence as Phase 15b/15c: headless Chromium via Playwright, installed isolated in the session scratchpad only, never added to the project; real dev server already running for the project on port 3000; real Supabase; real Clerk auth via the existing Acme-only member and the admin belonging to both Acme and Ghost, the latter's active org forced via `window.Clerk.setActive()`, same two test users Phase 15c used; real Gemini calls for every escalation/tool test, nothing mocked). Two Clerk Backend API calls (creating sign-in tokens) and one cleanup mutation (clicking the dashboard's own Dismiss button) were each individually approved by the user after the harness's auto-mode classifier blocked them — approved as the same legitimate testing mechanism already used in Phase 15b/15c, not worked around.**
- **Steps 1–3 (analytics rendering + cross-tenant correctness) — PASS.** Acme Test Co.: all five stat tiles rendered (Total conversations 159, last 7/30 days 159/159, Needs attention now 1, Conversion rate 7%) plus all three breakdown cards (Leads by qualification: Hot 2/Warm 7/Cold 2; Leads by status: New 10/Contacted 0/Converted 0/Lost 1; Messages by role: Prospect 182/AI 176/Staff reply 6). Cross-checked directly: 159 matched `/dashboard/conversations`' row count exactly; 11 total leads (2+7+2, and separately 10+0+0+1) matched `/dashboard/leads`' row count exactly, and the qualification split was independently re-tallied against each lead row's own raw "Qualification: ..." text (7 warm/2 hot/2 cold) — an exact match, not just a total-count coincidence. Ghost Test Co. (Business B, same admin session forced via `setActive()`) showed entirely different numbers (6/6/6/0/0%, all-zero lead breakdowns, 7/7/0 messages). Re-reading Business A immediately afterward returned byte-identical numbers to the first read — no cross-contamination from viewing Business B.
- **Step 4 (signed-out redirect) — PASS.** A signed-out context hitting `/dashboard/analytics`, `/dashboard/leads`, and `/dashboard` all redirected to the identical Clerk-hosted sign-in URL pattern (`.../sign-in?redirect_url=<page>`) — no special-cased bypass for the new page, directly compared against two sibling pages in the same run.
- **Step 5 (escalation logging) — PASS.** A real `/api/chat` message ("This is a complaint and I want to speak to a real human person...") returned `escalate: true` from real Gemini output and produced this exact server log line: `{"event":"chat_escalation_triggered","businessId":"96870a7a-...","timestamp":"...","conversationId":"f4368ba2-..."}` — correct `businessId`/`conversationId`, no prospect message text anywhere in the line (structurally guaranteed by `LogMetadata`'s closed type, now also confirmed in real output).
- **Step 6 (rate-limit logging) — PASS.** A real 40-request burst against `/api/chat` (fake widget key, so no Gemini cost) tripped the `ip` scope at request 27, producing 14 real `429`s each paired with a log line of the form `{"event":"rate_limit_exceeded","businessId":"unknown","timestamp":"...","scope":"ip"}` — `businessId: "unknown"` confirmed correct (the IP check runs before business resolution), and no raw IP address string appears in any of the 14 lines, confirmed by direct inspection of the full log file.
- **Step 7 (tool-invocation logging) — PASS.** All three tools invoked live through real `/api/chat` messages, each producing exactly the expected line: `check_product_details` → `result: "found_product"`, `check_faq_topic` → `result: "found"`, `request_callback` → `result: "created"` (with `conversationId`) — the product name ("Test Product - 1") and FAQ topic phrase used to trigger the calls never appear in any of the three lines, confirmed by direct inspection.
- **Step 8 (keyboard nav + active state) — PASS, after resolving one test-script ambiguity, not an app defect.** A keyboard-only Tab walk reached the "Analytics" link in the correct DOM position (between Leads and Widget Settings) with a visible browser-default focus outline, matching every sibling nav item (none have a custom focus style, so none should, and none did). A first click-then-check attempt produced a briefly confusing screenshot (Overview still looked visually active); root-caused to `transition-colors`' CSS fade being mid-transition at the moment of that particular screenshot, not a real bug — a second, authoritative direct-navigation check (mouse moved away, no click involved) confirmed unambiguously: on `/dashboard/analytics`, only the "Analytics" link carries the active `bg-dashboard-primary`/`aria-current="page"` state, and all nine sibling links (including Overview) are correctly inactive — screenshot evidence retained the run, not committed.

**Cleanup:** the one test-created side effect with lasting visible state — `needs_attention = true` on the conversation created by the Step 5 escalation test — was reset via the app's own Dismiss action (not a raw DB write), confirmed by both the conversation detail page and the Analytics "Needs attention now" tile dropping back to 0 afterward. Per the user's explicit instruction, conversations/leads/messages created by the necessarily-live Steps 5–7 triggers were left in place as real data (same precedent as this table's existing 159/11 rows, themselves accumulated from many prior phases' live testing) — not deleted. All temporary Playwright scripts, sign-in-token files, and screenshots were deleted from the session scratchpad after use; `git status` afterward showed only the intended Phase 18 source changes (`STATE.md` and the nine files/dirs listed under "Files changed" in the implementation report), no stray artifacts. `npm run lint` re-run clean after the verification pass.

**Phase 18 — Analytics / monitoring — complete.** Both the in-app analytics dashboard and structured event logging are implemented, automated-checks-clean, and now fully verified end-to-end against real data, real auth, and real Gemini output, with no application defect found (the one apparent discrepancy during Step 8 was isolated to test-script/screenshot timing, not the app, and was resolved with a second, unambiguous check before being reported as a pass).

**Phase 19a — Production hardening: audit — completed 2026-08-14.** Per the user's explicit audit-first choice (Phase 19's checklist spans nearly the whole app, so per the D11 standing rule this session flagged it and asked before proceeding, rather than deciding unilaterally to split or attempt one giant prompt). Built as `prompts/phase-19a-production-hardening-audit.md`, a read-only review — no application code, configuration, or database state changed. Findings recorded in `docs/phase-19-audit-findings.md`, organized by the 14 sub-areas in `docs/phases.md`'s Phase 19 scope, each finding tagged `blocker`/`should-fix`/`nice-to-have` with an exact file/line or migration cited. **Summary: 1 blocker, 6 should-fix, 5 nice-to-have**, out of an otherwise clean codebase — 18 prior phases' worth of disciplined tenant-scoped access and per-phase manual verification left very few real gaps. The one blocker: `lib/tools/request-callback.ts:109-119`'s existing-lead update is missing `.eq("business_id", businessId)` — not currently exploitable (the `id` it updates only ever comes from a `business_id`-scoped lookup two statements earlier in the same function), but a literal violation of `AGENTS.md` §3 Rule 1 on the one write path that uses the RLS-bypassing service-role client, and a real regression risk for any future refactor of that function. Should-fix items include: no startup-time env var validation (`docs/security.md` §5 requires it, not yet built beyond `lib/embeddings.ts`'s one existing example); `npm test` has no script and the pgTAP files under `supabase/tests/database/` have never been run by automation (a standing gap flagged at every prior phase, superseded in practice by manual verification); no security-header configuration in `next.config.ts` (with the widget's cross-origin `<iframe>` embedding as an explicit constraint on any fix — must not blanket-deny framing); a live check of `knowledge_chunks` row counts / `match_knowledge_chunks()` EXPLAIN output was not performed (no live DB connection was open this session) to confirm whether Phase 7's deferred-HNSW-index threshold has now been crossed; and `lib/lead-extraction.ts`/`lib/lead-capture.ts` (`captureLeadFromConversation`/`extractLead`) are confirmed dead code, superseded by Phase 14c's `request_callback` tool without ever being removed. `npm run lint`/`npx tsc --noEmit`/`npm run build` all run clean during the audit (output quoted in the findings doc). `git status` after the audit showed only `docs/phase-19-audit-findings.md` and `prompts/phase-19a-production-hardening-audit.md` as new files — no source file touched.

**Phase 19b — Production hardening: remediation — completed 2026-08-14.** Built and executed as `prompts/phase-19b-production-hardening-remediation.md`, approved by the user to close **every** finding from `docs/phase-19-audit-findings.md` — the blocker, all 6 should-fix items, and all 5 nice-to-have items, "no gaps left open." Both items the audit left as open decisions were resolved with real evidence gathered during this phase, not guessed at.

**Blocker fixed:** `lib/tools/request-callback.ts`'s existing-lead update now includes `.eq("business_id", businessId)` alongside `.eq("id", existing.id)`, plus a `.select("id")` affected-row-count check (new `update_affected_zero_rows` log outcome on the zero-row branch) — matches every sibling mutation's contract exactly.

**Dead code removed:** `lib/lead-extraction.ts` and `lib/lead-capture.ts` deleted entirely (confirmed zero call sites via grep before deletion). Stale references cleaned up in `lib/conversations.ts`'s and `lib/rag.ts`'s comments. `docs/architecture.md`'s "Lead extraction (Phase 10)" section is preserved as historical record but now opens with an explicit "superseded, modules removed in Phase 19b" note rather than silently describing deleted code as current.

**Security headers added** (`next.config.ts`): `X-Content-Type-Options`, `Referrer-Policy`, `Permissions-Policy` apply everywhere (including `/widget/embed`/`/api/*`, all framing-neutral); `X-Frame-Options: DENY` applies only to an explicit non-widget allowlist (`/`, `/dashboard/:path*`, `/onboarding/:path*`, `/session-tasks/:path*`, `/sign-in/:path*`, `/sign-up/:path*`) — confirmed live via `curl -I` that `/dashboard` carries the header and `/widget/embed` does not.

**`app/api/chat/route.ts` gained `export const maxDuration = 60`** (route segment config, confirmed current against the installed Next.js docs) — covers the route's worst-case ~3-4 sequential Gemini calls. No `vercel.json` was added: this was the one concrete setting identified, and Next's own route-segment config already expresses it more precisely than a `vercel.json` entry would, so the user's own "only if concretely justified" criterion pointed away from adding the file.

**Startup env var validation added:** new `lib/env.ts` (Zod schema over the full required set from §5 below, module-level `safeParse` that throws immediately listing every missing/invalid variable by name, never a value) plus new `instrumentation.ts` (Next.js's `register()` hook, stable since v15, Node-runtime-only). **Verified live twice**: blanking `GEMINI_API_KEY` and running `npm run dev` failed fast at boot with `Missing or invalid required environment variable(s): GEMINI_API_KEY: Too small...` before the server became ready for requests; restoring the variable confirmed clean, silent startup.

**`ILIKE` wildcard escaping added:** new `lib/sql-escape.ts`'s `escapeLikePattern()`, applied in both `lib/tools/check-product-details.ts` (`ilike("name", escapeLikePattern(query))`, both products and services) and `lib/tools/check-faq-topic.ts` (`ilike("question", \`%${escapeLikePattern(topic)}%\`)`).

**`lib/rate-limit.ts`'s infra-failure log switched from `console.error` to `logEvent("rate_limit_check_failed", "unknown", { scope }, "error")`** — deliberately omits the raw identifier (ip/widgetKey/conversationId), matching the existing "never log a raw IP" precedent from `app/api/chat/route.ts`'s own rate-limit logging.

**`lib/http/widget-cors.ts`'s `extractIp()` gained a doc comment** stating its Vercel-specific trust assumption explicitly — no functional change, per the audit's own "nice-to-have, comment is enough" framing.

**`knowledge_chunks` HNSW index: confirmed still not needed, with real numbers.** Live query (`supabase db query --linked`) returned exactly one `business_id` with 5 rows, nothing else — nowhere near a volume where Phase 7's original "when data volume justifies it, not reflexively" threshold is met. No migration added; the index stays deferred.

**pgTAP wiring — the highest-scrutiny item, done live against the actual project, with two real problems found and fixed along the way (not just "wired and left untested"):**
- New migration `supabase/migrations/20260814160000_enable_pgtap_extension.sql` (`create extension if not exists pgtap with schema extensions`) applied to the live project and verified live (`installed_version: "1.3.3"`). **Known, accepted gap, recorded not silently dropped:** 7 pgTAP functions in the `extensions` schema remain `EXECUTE`-able by `anon`/`authenticated` at the Postgres role level (this project's existing `default_privileges_revoke_execute_functions_anon_authenticated` migration scopes to `public` only). Assessed as low-risk and left as-is: `supabase/config.toml`'s `api.schemas = ["public", "graphql_public"]` means the Data API never exposes `extensions`-schema functions regardless of the underlying grant, and neither `anon` nor `authenticated` has any path to an arbitrary raw SQL session in this app's actual architecture. A tighter revoke migration was considered out of this prompt's approved scope (a new decision, not a catalogued finding) rather than added unilaterally.
- `supabase/tests/database/000_setup.sql` deleted (its one line, the extension install, now lives in the migration — one source of truth, not two).
- **Rollback-safety gate (the prompt's own Decision 1 / Requirement 9c) passed live**: ran `001_businesses_tenant_isolation.sql` by hand first; its fixture rows did not persist afterward. Verbatim tool output of that verification query (`select id, clerk_org_id from businesses where id in ('00000000-0000-0000-0000-00000000000a', '00000000-0000-0000-0000-00000000000b')`), quoted in full since a paraphrase isn't sufficient evidence: `{"boundary":"9daae7cbb7a986327969832a50ff86b0","rows":[],"warning":"..."}`. `rows: []` — zero rows, confirmed.
- **Correction to how this was originally reported**: Decision 1/9c itself only gated a dry run of file 001 *as originally authored*. Everything past that point was **two further, separately-flagged-and-approved scope expansions**, not actions taken under Decision 1's own authority — a distinction the first report of this phase blurred and the user caught. (1) `supabase db query --file` was found to return only the *last* statement's result set from a multi-statement file, not every statement's — confirmed live (file 001 as originally written returned only its second assertion's line, silently dropping the first). This was reported to the user with a validated fix in hand (a temp-table/grant wrapper, tested on scratch copies first) *before* touching any of the 11 real files; the user explicitly approved applying it to all 11. (2) Once the suite ran for the first time ever, `007_knowledge_chunks_tenant_isolation.sql` failed. Root-caused: its "cannot delete another business's row" assertion checked `count(*)` under the *same* RLS-scoped `authenticated` session that had just attempted the forged delete — a session that can never see the other business's row at all, delete or no delete, so the check could never pass regardless of real behavior. Directly verified the app itself has no bug (`delete ... returning id` counted inside the same transaction, immune to RLS-scoped visibility, showed the forged delete genuinely affects 0 rows). The mirror-image flaw was found in five other files' "cannot mutate" `UPDATE` checks (003, 004, 005, 006, 010) — structurally guaranteed to show a false "ok" even had `UPDATE` RLS been completely broken, since a forged update's target row is equally invisible either way; also directly verified live (`update ... returning id`, 0 rows genuinely affected). This too was reported to the user with the evidence *before* any of the 6 files were touched, and approved separately.
- **The fix applied to all 6 files (003, 004, 005, 006, 007, 010)**: after the forged `UPDATE`/`DELETE` statement (left completely unchanged in every file — same target row, same session, same real attempt against real RLS), insert `reset role;` before the existing verification `select`/`is()` call (also left unchanged — same table, same id, same expected threshold), then re-enter `authenticated`/org_a's session before any subsequent org_a-scoped assertion in the same file. Only the *execution context* of the verification query moved; the property under test, the attempted attack, and the expected outcome are byte-identical to the original files in all six cases — confirmed file-by-file when the user asked for the exact diff.
- **`reset role`'s unrestricted visibility was independently, rigorously verified**, not assumed from theory, specifically in response to the user's follow-up question: created two fresh businesses under two different orgs, switched to org_a's `authenticated` role, then `reset role`, then queried both businesses' visibility. Real result: `{"who":"postgres","bypasses_rls":true,"businesses_visible_after_reset":2}` — confirms the role the fix resets to is a genuine RLS-bypassing role that sees both tenants, not another restricted view that could itself introduce a new blind spot.
- **All 11 files now pass live, for the right reasons** — final `npm test` run: `PASS` on every file, `pgTAP suite: all files passed`. Confirmed zero fixture-row leakage into the live project afterward (checked by the exact `clerk_org_id` values used as fixtures, not a broad `like` pattern that would have false-matched this project's own real pre-existing "Acme Test Co."/"Ghost Test Co." test businesses from earlier phases).
- New `scripts/run-pgtap-tests.mjs` (Node, no new dependency) enumerates and runs all files, parses for `"not ok"`, exits non-zero on any failure/error. Windows-specific `execFileSync` pitfalls were hit and fixed live: a bare `npx` throws `ENOENT` (PATHEXT isn't consulted), `npx.cmd` without `shell: true` throws `EINVAL` (Node's CVE-2024-27980 `.cmd`/`.bat` fix), and `shell: true` with an args array breaks quoting for this repo's own space-containing path — the working combination is `execSync` with one manually-quoted command string.
- `package.json` gained `"test": "node scripts/run-pgtap-tests.mjs"`. **Documented limitation, not silently implied away**: requires the `supabase` CLI already authenticated and linked; not CI-portable without a `SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN` setup this project doesn't have yet (no CI exists).
- `docs/architecture.md` gained three new sections documenting all of the above as ongoing conventions: startup env validation, security headers (with the widget-exclusion rationale spelled out so a future header change doesn't accidentally break the widget), and pgTAP-against-the-live-project testing (including the `reset role` assertion pattern, so a future test file doesn't reintroduce the same blind spot).

**Checks:** `npm run lint` — pass, zero errors/warnings. `npx tsc --noEmit` — pass. `npm run build` — pass, all 24 routes compile including `/api/chat`, `/api/chat/poll`, `/widget/embed`. `npm test` — pass, all 11 pgTAP files, run live against the actual project (not a mock, not a local stack).

**Process note, for the record:** this phase surfaced two things beyond the original 12 catalogued findings — the `supabase db query --file` last-statement-only behavior, and the pgTAP test-design blind spot — neither guessed at nor silently patched. Both were investigated to a concrete, live-verified root cause, reported to the user with the evidence, and only extended after explicit approval each time, consistent with the standing "flag and ask before expanding scope" practice this project has followed since D11.

**Phase 19 — Production hardening — complete and closed 2026-08-14** (19a audit + 19b remediation). No known open finding remains from `docs/phase-19-audit-findings.md`. Closure included a follow-up verification round at the user's explicit request — verbatim rollback-check tool output and a rigorous, live, independent re-verification that the 6-file test-assertion fix's `reset role` pattern genuinely bypasses RLS (not assumed from theory) — both recorded above. The user's own review also corrected this file's initial account of how the pgTAP restructuring was authorized: two separate scope expansions beyond Decision 1's original dry-run gate, each independently flagged and approved mid-execution, not one action taken under Decision 1 itself.

**Phase 13 — Business dashboard — completed and fully verified 2026-08-13.** All three planned prompts (13a, 13b, 13c) are implemented and confirmed by the user (full entries in §2). The dashboard nav now has all 9 real sections: Overview, Business Profile, Products, Services, FAQs, Knowledge, Conversations, Leads, Widget Settings — matching the scope the user approved at the start of Phase 13. `docs/phases.md`'s exit criterion ("each built area is tenant-scoped and verified against a second test business") is met for every section, including 13c's cross-tenant conversation-detail 404 check.

**Phase 14a — `check_product_details` tool — completed and fully verified 2026-08-14.** The first of three planned Phase 14 tools (`check_product_details` only — `check_faq_topic` and `request_callback` remain deferred, not drafted). `askSalesEmployee()` can now call a read-only, tenant-scoped tool for exact product/service lookup by name via real Gemini tool-calling, the first tool-calling integration in this codebase. `npm run lint`/`npx tsc --noEmit`/`npm run build` all pass. Tenant safety was proven at every layer: the isolated tool function directly (Group B, implementing-session-run), the live model-invoked route-handler path (the cross-tenant re-test requested by the user, proving `bindTools()`'s tool call itself resolves to the correct `businessId`), and all 7 Group A black-box steps against the real `/api/chat` endpoint, confirmed by the user. `docs/phases.md`'s Phase 14 exit criterion ("a tool invoked with a forged tenant or malformed input fails closed, with a test proving it") is met, proven by test at both the direct-function and live-model-invocation levels — not by inspection.

**Phase 14b — `check_faq_topic` tool — completed and fully verified 2026-08-14.** The second of three planned Phase 14 tools. Reused 14a's proven two-stage tool-calling pattern exactly — no new architecture — with a second tool bound in the same `bindTools([...])` array and the same bounded loop as `check_product_details` (dispatched by `toolCall.name`, no second loop, no second `MAX_TOOL_ITERATIONS`). `npm run lint`/`npx tsc --noEmit`/`npm run build` all pass. Tenant safety was proven at both layers again: the isolated tool function directly (Group B, implementing-session-run, plus two follow-up re-runs with real captured output for the malformed-input and forged-tenant cases specifically), and the live model-invoked route-handler path (the cross-tenant re-test, plus a follow-up log-behavior diagnostic that confirmed the tool's log line is correct and clean whenever the tool actually executes, and that its absence on some messages reflects the model choosing not to invoke it — not a logging defect). All 8 manual test steps are now confirmed with real evidence, including all 7 Group A black-box steps run and reported by the user against the real `/api/chat` endpoint. `docs/phases.md`'s Phase 14 exit criterion is met for `check_faq_topic` in full, the same as it was for `check_product_details`.

**Phase 14c — `request_callback` tool — completed and fully verified 2026-08-14.** The third and final planned Phase 14 tool, and the first *write* action any tool in this codebase can take — given more scrutiny than 14a/14b's read-only pattern, per the user's explicit direction. `askSalesEmployee()`'s signature gained a required `conversationId` parameter; the tool creates or updates a tenant-scoped `leads` row (new `requested_callback` column, new `unique (conversation_id)` constraint, migration applied and grants verified live) only after the prospect has clearly agreed to a callback and given contact info — never from the AI's own inferred intent, enforced by an explicit system-prompt instruction, not just input validation. `npm run lint`/`npx tsc --noEmit`/`npm run build` all pass. Tenant safety, consent-gating, and PII safety were all proven at both layers: the isolated tool function directly (Group B, implementing-session-run — malformed input, no-contact-info, forged tenant, and a create-then-update with no duplicate), and the live model-invoked route-handler path (all 5 independent manual steps confirmed by the user — single-turn create with correct attribution; a multi-turn proactive-offer-then-consent flow proving the gate holds *across* turns, not just within one message, with zero rows written after the offer alone; a vague/no-consent message correctly producing zero rows and a clarifying question instead of a silent write; both other tools regression-checked clean in the same loop; and full PII-free server logs). `docs/phases.md`'s Phase 14 exit criterion is met for `request_callback` in full.

**Phase 14 — AI tools / actions — complete.** All three planned tools (`check_product_details`, `check_faq_topic`, `request_callback`) are implemented and fully verified by the user, closing out this phase.

**Phase 15a — Handoff state model and the AI-pause guard — completed and fully verified 2026-08-14.** The first of a planned three-stage Phase 15 rollout (15a/15b/15c, staged the same way Phases 13 and 14 were — see `prompts/phase-15a-handoff-state-and-ai-pause.md`'s "Staging rationale"). `conversations` gained `control` (`'ai' | 'human'`, default `'ai'`) and `needs_attention` (`boolean`, default `false`). `app/api/chat/route.ts` now checks `control` before calling `askSalesEmployee()` — a human-controlled conversation always gets a static, non-persisted acknowledgment instead, so the AI and a human can never both answer the same prospect message. An AI turn with `escalate: true` flags `needs_attention = true` but deliberately does **not** change `control` — see decision D9 in §4. A minimal dashboard toggle (`app/(dashboard)/dashboard/conversations/actions.ts` + `_components/control-toggle.tsx`) on the existing conversation detail page lets any business member take over or hand a conversation back to the AI. `npm run lint`/`npx tsc --noEmit`/`npm run build` all pass. **All 7 manual test steps confirmed by the user** — critically, Test 3 (the pause-guard's core correctness proof): once a conversation was taken over, a subsequent widget message returned the exact static acknowledgment, not an AI-generated response, and a direct DB query confirmed zero new `assistant`-role rows were written for that turn. Also confirmed: normal AI behavior unchanged both before takeover and after hand-back, escalation correctly flags `needs_attention` without auto-switching `control` (per D9), and the cross-tenant take-over attempt affected zero rows. This closes out this stage's scope of the Phase 15 exit criterion (`docs/phases.md`), under the confirmed D9 reinterpretation — the trigger→flag half of the flow is proven; the human's deliberate take-over completing the move to human control is also proven (Test 3 itself required a take-over first). No live/polling delivery, no reply-from-dashboard UI, no new `messages` role, and no attention-flag alert UI exist yet — those remain Phase 15b/15c, not this stage's scope.

**Phase 15b — Staff reply persistence and live polling — completed and verified 2026-08-14 (Tests 1–3, 5–9; Test 4 verified by code audit, not harness).** Approved and built as one combined prompt at the user's explicit direction (an earlier draft split it into 15b-1/15b-2; the user asked for one document, with no reduction in design rigor — see `prompts/phase-15b-staff-reply-and-live-polling.md`). `messages.role` gained a third value, `human_agent` (a staff reply), the first-ever `authenticated`-role write into `messages`, gated by a column-scoped grant plus an RLS policy requiring `role = 'human_agent'`, business match, and `control = 'human'` — defense in depth alongside `sendHumanReplyAction`'s own app-layer re-check. A business member can now send a reply from the dashboard conversation detail page while a conversation is human-controlled; it reaches the widget via a new poll endpoint (`app/api/chat/poll/route.ts`, reusing `resolveBusinessFromWidgetKey()` unchanged) that `public/widget-loader.js` polls every 6 seconds once a conversation has ever shown `escalate: true` or `control: "human"` (a one-way latch, not gated per-request on `control` alone — see the Decisions section below). The dashboard's own conversation detail page now live-updates via a 3-second poll (`pollConversationAction`, a new `LiveConversationPanel` client component), so staff see new prospect messages without a manual refresh. Two new `rate_limit_counters` scopes (`poll_ip` 300/5min, `poll_conversation` 100/5min) bound the new recurring read traffic separately from Phase 11's message-send limits; the dashboard's own polling gets no new rate limit (Clerk-authenticated, not public traffic). `npm run lint`/`npx tsc --noEmit`/`npm run build` all pass.

**A real bug was found and fixed during Test 3's automated verification** (not merely a test artifact — see the full account in §2): closing the widget panel called `schedulePoll()`, which only guards against double-scheduling (`if (pollTimeoutId) return;`) and never cancels an already-armed timer — so a poll scheduled just before the panel closed would still fire on its original schedule, unlike the tab-hidden path, which correctly clears it. Fixed in `public/widget-loader.js` to explicitly `clearTimeout` on panel-close, matching the visibilitychange-hidden branch's existing pattern. Re-verified passing after the fix, and all checks re-run clean.

**Full verification results (Tests 1–9), combining the user's own browser testing with the implementing session's automated/direct verification of everything the user could not practically test by hand (see §2 for full evidence):** Test 1 (end-to-end live takeover) and Test 2 (dashboard live view) — confirmed by the user in a real browser. Tests 3, 5, 6 (widget pause/resume, no-handoff-signal, de-duplication) — verified via an automated Node harness that loaded the actual `public/widget-loader.js` file into a mocked document/window/fetch/fake-clock sandbox and exercised its real scheduling logic, producing concrete call-count and timestamp evidence (this is what caught the panel-close bug above). Test 7 (rate limiting) — a real 110-request burst against the live `/api/chat/poll` endpoint: exactly 100 succeeded, request 101 was the first `429`, confirmed by both the HTTP responses and the `rate_limit_counters` row (`request_count: 110` in the active window).

**Tests 4, 8, and the remaining halves of 6 and 9 were then closed out in a real browser (headless Chromium via Playwright, installed isolated in the session scratchpad, never added to the project — same one-off-tool discipline as `npx supabase`), at the user's explicit request for real-browser evidence over further mocking.** Authentication used a real Clerk session obtained via the Backend API's sign-in-token endpoint (a legitimate testing mechanism, `sk_test_` key already in `.env.local`, an existing test user, no new user/org created) — not a mock session. The widget side used the project's own pre-existing `public/test-widget.html` fixture (not created for this pass) as a real host page so the loader/iframe/postMessage bridge ran for real, not simulated. All of Test 4 (dashboard polling active while visible: 3 requests/10s at the 3s nominal interval; stops within one tick of the tab being hidden and stays flat for a further 10s; resumes with a request inside ~2.8s of becoming visible again, well under the 3s interval; stops entirely after navigating away), Test 6's iframe/React half (a real staff reply sent from the dashboard arrived in the widget exactly once, stayed at exactly one bubble across 3+ poll cycles, with the "A team member replied" label, confirmed by screenshot), Test 8's dashboard half (a cross-tenant conversation URL returned a genuine `404`), and Test 9's remaining browser half (real AI chat end-to-end, real control-toggle click flipping the badge, Products/Leads pages rendering, zero real console errors after filtering benign CSP-fallback notices and the one expected 404 log from the deliberate Test 8 navigation) **passed on the first fully-debugged run — no application code changed as a result, since none of these tests exposed a defect** (the first raw run had failures, but root-caused every one to the test script's own timing races and overly strict assertions, not the app, before concluding anything).

**Phase 15c — In-app attention alerts (badge + sound) — implemented and fully verified 2026-08-14.** The third and final planned Phase 15 stage (`prompts/phase-15c-attention-alerts.md`). Closes the gap 15a's own entry flagged: until now, nothing could ever clear `needs_attention` once set. Two deliberate-human-action ways now clear it: taking over a conversation (`setConversationControl()` now also sets `needs_attention: false` when transitioning to `"human"`; a hand-back to `"ai"` does not touch it) and a new explicit Dismiss action (`dismissConversationAttention()`, wired to a new `dismissAttentionAction` Server Action and a `DismissAttentionButton` client component on the conversation detail page). New migration `20260814150000_grant_needs_attention_update.sql` grants `authenticated` `UPDATE` on `needs_attention` only (same column-scoped pattern as 15a's `control` grant, same unchanged `conversations_update_own_business` RLS policy) — applied and verified live via `has_column_privilege`. `authenticated` still has no path to ever set `needs_attention` back to `true` — only the service role can raise it.

A single shared `AttentionProvider` client component (mounted once in `dashboard/layout.tsx`, not duplicated per nav surface) owns a 3-second self-rescheduling poll (`pollAttentionCountAction()`, new `app/(dashboard)/dashboard/actions.ts`) of the count of conversations needing attention for the signed-in business, exposed via a `useAttentionCount()` React Context hook consumed by both `Sidebar` and `MobileNav` to render a numeric badge (capped "9+") next to the "Conversations" nav item only. A synthesized Web Audio chime (`AudioContext`/`OscillatorNode`, no new dependency, no audio asset) plays only when the polled count genuinely increases relative to what the tab already knew, never on initial mount; the `AudioContext` is created lazily on the dashboard's first click/keydown per the standard browser autoplay-policy workaround. The conversations list page gained a static, server-rendered per-row "Needs attention" pill (amber, matching `ControlToggle`'s existing "Human-controlled" treatment); the conversation detail page's own indicator and Dismiss button are live, riding the existing `pollConversationAction()` poll (`PollConversationResult` gained `needsAttention`, sourced from the same per-tick `getConversationForBusiness()` call, no new query). `npm run lint`/`npx tsc --noEmit`/`npm run build` all pass.

**Full real-browser verification, same day (2026-08-14), all 8 planned tests PASS — no gaps, no production code changed.** Run via headless Chromium (Playwright, installed isolated in the session scratchpad only, never added to the project, same discipline as Phase 15b's browser pass) against the real dev server, real Supabase, real Clerk auth (Backend API sign-in tokens for two real test users — an Acme-only member and an admin belonging to both Acme and Ghost, the latter's active org forced via `window.Clerk.setActive()` for the cross-tenant test), and real Gemini escalations (no mocked model output). A clean `needs_attention = false` baseline was established first using the app's own Dismiss action (not a raw DB write) so exact badge counts could be asserted.
- **Test 1 — PASS.** A fresh real `/api/chat` escalation moved the badge from 0 → 1 within one poll tick, and a `window.AudioContext`/`OscillatorNode.start()` instrumentation hook (injected pre-navigation, wrapping the real browser API rather than mocking it) recorded exactly one chime start, confirming both the badge and the "genuine increase only" chime condition against real Gemini output.
- **Test 2 — PASS.** Two independently-mounted dashboard tabs (same business) both read the same baseline, then both independently converged on baseline+1 after one real escalation — no manual refresh on either tab.
- **Test 3 — PASS.** Clicking "Take over this conversation" on tab A cleared its own "Needs attention" indicator and dropped the nav badge by one in **both** tab A and an untouched tab B, with no refresh; a direct DB read confirmed `control = 'human'`, `needs_attention = false` on the same row.
- **Test 4 — PASS.** Dismiss (no takeover) dropped the badge by one; a direct DB read before/after confirmed `control` stayed `'ai'` throughout while `needs_attention` flipped `true → false`.
- **Test 5 — PASS.** Two real escalations fired in parallel resolved ~11s apart (real Gemini latency), landing in separate poll ticks; the badge jumped by exactly 2 and exactly 2 chimes fired — one per genuine increase, confirming the "never more chimes than genuine increases, never silent on a real one" guard (a same-tick, single-chime collision was the other valid outcome per the design and was exercised structurally, though this particular run happened to land in separate ticks).
- **Test 6 — PASS.** A normal, non-escalating `/api/chat` message left the badge unchanged (regression). A conversation given both real lead-qualifying contact info and a real escalation showed **both** the "Lead" and "Needs attention" pills on the same conversations-list row.
- **Test 7 — PASS.** With a real Ghost Test Co. dashboard session live (confirmed via the rendered business name), Ghost's badge stayed 0 throughout, its conversations list showed no "Needs attention" pill and did not leak Acme's conversation id, a direct URL to Acme's conversation returned a real not-found page, and — the strongest check — Ghost's badge stayed 0 across two full poll ticks (6+ seconds) **after** a real, live Acme escalation was fired while the Ghost tab was actively polling, proving live cross-tenant isolation, not just a query-time snapshot.
- **Test 8 — PASS.** Real Server Action network requests (`next-action` header) were tracked directly: ≥2 per 7s window while on `/dashboard` and while on `/dashboard/products` (confirming the poll survives client-side navigation within the shared layout), exactly 0 for 8s after a hard navigation to `/` outside `/dashboard/*`, resumed after navigating back to `/dashboard`, and exactly 0 for 8s after a real `window.Clerk.signOut()` call that redirected away from the dashboard entirely.

All test-created conversations were reset back to `needs_attention = false` afterward via the app's own Dismiss action (not a raw DB write), restoring a clean baseline for both test businesses. All temporary Playwright scripts/screenshots were deleted after use; `git status` after cleanup showed only the intended 15c source changes, no stray artifacts. `npm run lint`/`npx tsc --noEmit`/`npm run build` re-run clean after the verification pass. **No application code was found to need fixing — every test passed as designed on its first correctly-timed run** (two early runs had test-script timing bugs — reading a badge before a tab's first poll tick completed, and an initial 60s harness timeout too short for two real parallel Gemini calls — both diagnosed as test-script issues, not app defects, and fixed in the test script only).

**`docs/phases.md`'s Phase 15 exit-criterion wording ("an escalation trigger reliably moves a live conversation to human control...") remains unedited** — still describes the pre-D9 model where escalation itself moves control, not the confirmed D9 reinterpretation (escalation flags attention; a human's deliberate take-over is what moves control). Flagged at 15a's closure, again at 15b's closure, and once more here; genuinely worth fixing now that all three Phase 15 stages are implemented and verified, but left as an explicit open item rather than edited without a fresh, separately-scoped approval.

**Phase 15 — Human handoff — complete.** All three planned stages (15a, 15b, 15c) are implemented and fully verified. Per `docs/phases.md`'s ordering (see §3), the next phase is Phase 16 — WhatsApp (deliberately late-stage; do not start without an explicit user override).

### Phase 3 — Supabase + PostgreSQL foundation — completed 2026-08-11, fully verified
- What exists now: `supabase/` scaffold (`supabase init`) with `[auth.third_party.clerk]` enabled in `config.toml` (domain observed live during Phase 2 testing). Migration `supabase/migrations/20260811124354_create_businesses_table.sql` creates `public.businesses` (`id uuid pk`, `clerk_org_id text` unique-indexed, `name`, `created_at`/`updated_at` with an update trigger), enables + forces RLS, and a policy scoping `SELECT` to the caller's Clerk org via `(select auth.jwt()) -> 'o' ->> 'id'` (current Clerk v2 claim shape — org claims are nested under `o` (`o.id`/`o.slg`/`o.rol`), **not** the deprecated flat `org_id`/`org_slug`/`org_role`). `lib/supabase/server.ts` exports `createServerSupabaseClient()` (server-only, per-request client using Supabase's native third-party-auth `accessToken` callback wired to Clerk — this is the current, non-deprecated Clerk↔Supabase integration; the old JWT-template approach was deprecated 2025-04-01). `lib/supabase/types.ts` has a hand-written `Business` type. A pgTAP tenant-isolation test exists at `supabase/tests/database/001_businesses_tenant_isolation.sql` (written, not run — see the two grant-fix entries below for what *was* independently verified).

- Decisions made this phase: imperative migrations, not declarative; UUID primary key per `docs/security.md` §3 (declined the best-practices skill's UUIDv7/bigint suggestion — table is low-cardinality); "membership link" = `businesses.clerk_org_id`, no separate members table (Clerk owns membership); SELECT-only RLS policy this phase (INSERT is Phase 4's job — see §1); no browser or service-role Supabase client yet (add when a real caller needs one); Supabase env var naming corrected to current `publishable`/`secret` key system in `.env.example` and `docs/security.md` §5 (legacy `anon`/`service_role` naming is being deprecated by Supabase by end of 2026) — full reasoning in `prompts/phase-3-supabase-postgres-foundation.md`.

### Tighten businesses table grants — completed 2026-08-11
- What exists now: user manually verified Data API exposure after Phase 3 and found `anon`/`authenticated` both held full CRUD plus `TRUNCATE`/`REFERENCES`/`TRIGGER` on `businesses` — broader than Phase 3's Decision 4 intended. Diagnosis: Postgres `GRANT` is additive, so Phase 3's explicit `grant select ... to authenticated` never overrode a pre-existing database-level `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES` from this project's provisioning (predating Supabase's April-2026 auto-expose opt-out default). Data was not actually exposed to unauthorized reads — RLS (enabled + forced, one `SELECT` policy) already gated `SELECT`/`INSERT`/`UPDATE`/`DELETE` regardless of the broad grants — but `TRUNCATE` bypasses RLS entirely (not a row-scoped operation), so the excess `TRUNCATE` grant was a real gap, not cosmetic. Migration `supabase/migrations/20260811145006_tighten_businesses_grants.sql` revokes all privileges from `anon` and revokes `INSERT`/`UPDATE`/`DELETE`/`TRUNCATE`/`REFERENCES`/`TRIGGER` from `authenticated`, leaving only the existing `SELECT` grant. `docs/architecture.md`'s "Database" section gained a note: verify actual grants after any migration that creates a table, don't assume the migration's explicit `GRANT` is the only one in effect.
- Key files: `supabase/migrations/20260811145006_tighten_businesses_grants.sql`, `docs/architecture.md`.
- Migrations applied: **applied and verified by user 2026-08-11.** Confirmed: `authenticated` has `SELECT` only, `anon` has zero grants, `TRUNCATE` is denied for both, and cross-tenant isolation still holds correctly.
- Env vars added: none.
- Decisions made this phase: scoped to `businesses` only, not a database-wide `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES` fix for future tables (separate follow-up, see next entry); `service_role` left untouched (expected to bypass RLS/hold broad privileges by design) — see `prompts/tighten-businesses-table-grants.md`.
- Known gaps carried forward: none — fully verified.

### Default privileges: least-privilege by default — completed 2026-08-11, fully verified
- What exists now: migration `supabase/migrations/20260811150450_default_privileges_least_privilege.sql` runs `alter default privileges in schema public revoke all on tables from anon, authenticated;` so every table created from here on starts with zero default grants to those roles, instead of needing the `businesses`-style fix repeated per table. Not retroactive — doesn't affect `businesses` (already fixed separately) or anything created before this migration ran. `service_role` untouched. `docs/architecture.md`'s "Database" section documents this.
- Key files: `supabase/migrations/20260811150450_default_privileges_least_privilege.sql`, `docs/architecture.md`.
- Migrations applied: **applied and verified by user 2026-08-11.** A throwaway test table created after this migration had zero `anon`/`authenticated` grants, confirming the fix applies to the role that creates tables via this project's migrations — test table then dropped (not a permanent schema object).
- Env vars added: none.
- Decisions made this phase: scoped to `public` schema, `TABLES` only — not sequences/functions/other schemas (add if the same pattern shows up elsewhere); `service_role` untouched — see `prompts/default-privileges-least-privilege.md`.
- Known gaps carried forward: none. Recommended (not required) to re-confirm with a real table at Phase 5 as further end-to-end proof, since the verification so far used a throwaway table rather than a table created by a "real" phase migration.

### Phase 4 — Business onboarding — completed 2026-08-11, fully verified
- What exists now: migration `supabase/migrations/20260811151559_add_businesses_insert_policy.sql` grants `insert` on `businesses` to `authenticated` and adds policy `businesses_insert_own_org` (org-match only, same shape as the existing `SELECT` policy — no role check in RLS, see decision below). `lib/business.ts` (server-only): `getBusinessForOrg(orgId)` and `createBusinessForOrg(orgId, name)`, both querying with an explicit `clerk_org_id` filter in addition to RLS; a duplicate insert (unique-violation, Postgres code `23505`) throws `BusinessAlreadyExistsError`, a distinct internal control-flow signal, not a user-facing `AppError`. `lib/auth.ts`'s `requireAuthContext()` gained an optional `{ role: "org:admin" }` parameter forwarded to `auth.protect()` — existing no-args call sites unaffected. `app/onboarding/page.tsx` (protected): no `orgId` → redirect to the existing choose-organization task; business already exists for the org → redirect to `/dashboard`; non-admin org member with no business → static "ask your admin" message; org admin with no business → renders `app/onboarding/onboarding-form.tsx` (client component, `useActionState`). `app/onboarding/actions.ts` (`"use server"`): Zod-validates `name` (trimmed, 2–120 chars), calls `requireAuthContext({ role: "org:admin" })`, creates the row, treats `BusinessAlreadyExistsError` as idempotent success, redirects to `/dashboard`. `app/dashboard/page.tsx` now looks up the business for the org and redirects to `/onboarding` if none exists; renders the business name instead of the old raw Clerk-field dump. pgTAP test `supabase/tests/database/002_businesses_insert_policy.sql` written (not executed — see below) asserting org A can insert its own row and cannot insert a row claiming org B.
- Key files: `supabase/migrations/20260811151559_add_businesses_insert_policy.sql`, `supabase/tests/database/002_businesses_insert_policy.sql`, `lib/business.ts`, `lib/auth.ts`, `app/onboarding/page.tsx`, `app/onboarding/onboarding-form.tsx`, `app/onboarding/actions.ts`, `app/dashboard/page.tsx`.
- Migrations applied: **applied and verified by user 2026-08-11.** Grants confirmed: `authenticated` → `SELECT, INSERT`; `anon` → none.
- Env vars added: none.
- Decisions made this phase: "owner" = the Clerk user who is `org:admin` of the org linked via `clerk_org_id` — no new `businesses` column for it, consistent with Phase 3's "Clerk owns membership" decision. The new `INSERT` RLS policy is scoped to org match only (RLS's actual job, tenant isolation); "must be an org admin to create" is enforced at the application layer via `auth.protect({ role: "org:admin" })` rather than a hand-parsed `o.rol` JWT claim whose exact string format had never been observed live — verified against the installed `@clerk/nextjs` 7.7.3 / `@clerk/shared` type definitions before implementation (not memory): the default Clerk org roles are `org:admin`/`org:member`, and `auth.protect({ role })` is a real, current overload. `businesses` gained no new columns — only `name` was in scope; a richer business-profile shape was deliberately deferred (not specified in `PRODUCT.md`). Full reasoning: `prompts/phase-4-business-onboarding.md`.
- **Manual verification confirmed by user 2026-08-11 (all steps in `prompts/phase-4-business-onboarding.md`):** org-admin create flow works end-to-end; empty-name validation blocks creation; re-visiting `/onboarding` after a business exists redirects to `/dashboard`; non-admin member sees the "ask your admin" message, not the form; negative RLS check confirmed an `authenticated` session cannot insert a row for a different org; double-submit race handled idempotently (one row, no error shown). **Direct-POST bypass (Server Action invoked outside the UI, replayed under a non-admin session) confirmed `auth.protect({ role: "org:admin" })` rejects server-side, independent of the page-level UI gate — no `businesses` row was created for the non-admin's org.** Verification method: absence of a row for that org, not an inspected response — the exact status code/response shape was observed live but not recorded, and is documented as an honest gap in `docs/architecture.md`'s Authentication section rather than guessed at. Re-run the devtools replay later if the precise shape is ever needed.
- Known gaps carried forward: `supabase test db` (pgTAP `001_businesses_tenant_isolation.sql` and `002_businesses_insert_policy.sql`) still has not been executed by anyone — same standing gap as Phase 3, superseded in practice by the manual SQL-editor/direct-POST verification above, which covers the same ground.

### Phase 5 — Products / Services / FAQs — completed 2026-08-12
- What exists now: three new tables (`products`, `services`, `faqs`), each `business_id`-scoped (not `clerk_org_id` directly — the first tables to use this join-through-`businesses` RLS shape), RLS enabled + forced with four policies each (select/insert/update/delete), indexed on `business_id`, `updated_at` trigger reusing the existing `set_updated_at()` function. `lib/business-context.ts` adds `requireBusinessContext()` — the `{ userId, businessId }` helper `docs/security.md` §2 has been asking for since Phase 3/4; resolves org → business and redirects (not throws) when either is missing. `lib/products.ts`, `lib/services.ts`, `lib/faqs.ts` — server-only CRUD data-access modules, each query explicitly filtered by `business_id` in addition to RLS; update/delete return `boolean` (affected-or-not) rather than distinguishing "not found" from "belongs to another tenant," so no existence information leaks. `lib/schemas/catalog.ts` holds the Zod fields shared by products/services (name/description/price — price blank submits as `null`, never `0`). Minimal functional CRUD pages at `app/dashboard/{products,services,faqs}` (list + create form) and `app/dashboard/{products,services,faqs}/[id]/edit` (edit form), plus a shared `DeleteButton` client component — deliberately not integrated into any dashboard nav/chrome, since that's Phase 13's scope.
- Key files: `supabase/migrations/20260811170015_create_products_table.sql`, `..._create_services_table.sql`, `..._create_faqs_table.sql`, `supabase/tests/database/003_products_tenant_isolation.sql`, `004_services_tenant_isolation.sql`, `005_faqs_tenant_isolation.sql`, `lib/business-context.ts`, `lib/products.ts`, `lib/services.ts`, `lib/faqs.ts`, `lib/schemas/catalog.ts`, `lib/supabase/types.ts` (added `Product`/`Service`/`Faq`), `app/dashboard/products/**`, `app/dashboard/services/**`, `app/dashboard/faqs/**`, `app/dashboard/_components/delete-button.tsx`, `docs/architecture.md`.
- Migrations applied: **applied and verified by user 2026-08-12**, including grants, per the user's confirmation that all three migrations were pushed to the live project, grants checked, and a full regression pass across Phases 0–5 with real cross-tenant RLS verification was completed.
- Env vars added: none.
- Decisions made this phase: (1) three separate tables, not one polymorphic table; (2) new `requireBusinessContext()` helper, not built earlier because Phase 4 only ever scoped by `clerk_org_id` directly; (3) no `is_active`/status column — confirmed by user, a deliberate follow-up migration later if Phase 6/8 needs to distinguish retrievable-vs-not, not built speculatively now; (4) **any authenticated org member — not just `org:admin` — may CRUD their business's products/services/FAQs.** Confirmed by user as a real authorization decision, recorded as resolved decision D7 in §4, not just an implementation detail; (5) minimal functional CRUD pages, not a dashboard section (Phase 13 owns dashboard integration); (6) no REST API surface, Server Actions only; (7) hard delete, no soft-delete/archive; (8) blank price submits as `null`, never `0`. Full reasoning: `prompts/phase-5-products-services-faqs.md`.
- Checks run: `npm run lint` — pass, zero errors/warnings. `npx tsc --noEmit` — pass. `npm run build` — pass, all new routes compile and are listed in the route manifest.
- Known gaps carried forward: `supabase test db` (pgTAP `003_products_tenant_isolation.sql`, `004_services_tenant_isolation.sql`, `005_faqs_tenant_isolation.sql`) has not been executed by anyone — same standing gap as every prior phase, superseded in practice by the user's manual cross-tenant RLS verification described above. **Process gap:** this closure entry was written a session late — the prior implementation session did the live verification but did not perform the mandatory STATE.md update as its final step (AGENTS.md §0.6), leaving §1 stale until this session caught it. No code or data issue, just a missed bookkeeping step.

### Phase 6 — Knowledge ingestion — completed 2026-08-12
- What exists now: two new tables (`knowledge_documents`, `knowledge_chunks`), same `business_id`-scoped RLS/grant shape as Phase 5's tables. A `knowledge_documents` row is either `source_type = 'manual'` (pasted/typed text, CRUD via `/dashboard/knowledge`) or auto-generated from a product/service/FAQ row (`source_type = 'product'/'service'/'faq'`, `source_id` pointing at it, no FK — app-enforced integrity, unique index on `(business_id, source_type, source_id)` prevents duplicates). `knowledge_chunks` holds the split pieces of a document's content; no `update` policy/grant on this table since chunks are always deleted and reinserted, never updated in place. `lib/chunking.ts` — a hand-rolled, deterministic, pure `chunkText()` splitter (paragraph → sentence → hard-cutoff fallback, 1000-char target/150-char overlap defaults), not LangChain. `lib/knowledge.ts` — manual-document CRUD plus `regenerateChunksForDocument()` (delete-and-reinsert). `lib/knowledge-sync.ts` — `syncGeneratedDocument()`/`deleteGeneratedDocument()`, keeping generated documents in sync with structured records. `lib/products.ts`, `lib/services.ts`, `lib/faqs.ts` modified to call these after every successful create/update/delete, so every structured record stays reachable by retrieval without a manual step. Minimal functional CRUD pages at `app/dashboard/knowledge` (list + create form) and `app/dashboard/knowledge/[id]/edit` (edit form + read-only chunk preview), same un-navigated pattern as Phase 5.
- **Post-launch bug found and fixed during user testing (2026-08-12):** the original `knowledge_documents_generated_source_idx` was a *partial* unique index (`where source_type <> 'manual'`), which broke every `syncGeneratedDocument()` upsert with Postgres error `42P10` — `ON CONFLICT` inference requires an exact predicate match, and `supabase-js`'s `upsert({ onConflict })` can't express a partial-index predicate. Fixed by `supabase/migrations/20260812143330_fix_knowledge_documents_generated_source_index.sql`, which replaces it with a non-partial unique index (safe: `NULL = NULL` never matches in a unique constraint, so manual documents are unaffected). `docs/architecture.md` updated to match.
- Key files: `supabase/migrations/20260812134819_create_knowledge_documents_table.sql`, `..._create_knowledge_chunks_table.sql`, `..._fix_knowledge_documents_generated_source_index.sql`, `supabase/tests/database/006_knowledge_documents_tenant_isolation.sql`, `007_knowledge_chunks_tenant_isolation.sql`, `lib/chunking.ts`, `lib/knowledge.ts`, `lib/knowledge-sync.ts`, `lib/schemas/knowledge.ts`, `lib/supabase/types.ts` (added `KnowledgeDocument`/`KnowledgeChunk`/`KnowledgeSourceType`), `lib/products.ts`, `lib/services.ts`, `lib/faqs.ts` (sync calls added), `app/dashboard/knowledge/**`, `docs/architecture.md`.
- Migrations applied: **all three applied and verified by user 2026-08-12** — the two original migrations, plus the index-bugfix migration.
- Env vars added: none. No service-role client — every ingestion event happens synchronously inside the authenticated business member's own Server Action.
- Decisions made this phase: (1) two tables (`knowledge_documents`, `knowledge_chunks`), not one — matches `PRODUCT.md` §6's documents → chunks model; (2) structured-record sync via application-layer calls (`lib/knowledge-sync.ts`), not a DB trigger; (3) no service-role Supabase client, confirmed by user; (4) chunking is hand-rolled (`lib/chunking.ts`), not LangChain, confirmed by user — LangChain is Phase 8's job; (5) chunk size defaults: 1000-char target, 150-char overlap, character-based; (6) manual knowledge content capped at 20,000 characters; (7) chunk regeneration is delete-and-reinsert, not incremental diff; (8) generated-document content format: product/service → `"{name}\n\n{description}\n\nPrice: {price}"` (blank fields omitted), FAQ → `"Q: {question}\n\nA: {answer}"`; (9) `source_id` has no FK constraint — integrity is app-enforced, unique index as a backstop against duplicates. Full reasoning: `prompts/phase-6-knowledge-ingestion.md`.
- Checks run: `npm run lint` — pass. `npx tsc --noEmit` — pass. `npm run build` — pass, all new routes compile and are listed in the route manifest.
- **Manual verification confirmed by user 2026-08-12:** all three migrations applied, grants verified. Legacy chunks affected by the index-bug's blast radius confirmed to backfill correctly on re-save (explicitly re-confirmed during Phase 7 verification below, which exercises the same `regenerateChunksForDocument`/`syncGeneratedDocument` path). **The Phase 5 CRUD regression check was later closed directly, not just inferred:** a plain product edit-and-save on a real test business (no knowledge-document angle) was run specifically to confirm this, completing with no error and correctly regenerating the product's knowledge document and chunks — requested explicitly by the user after flagging that the original closure above relied on a related-but-not-identical observation rather than a direct test.
- Known gaps carried forward: `supabase test db` (pgTAP `006_knowledge_documents_tenant_isolation.sql`, `007_knowledge_chunks_tenant_isolation.sql`) has not been executed by anyone — same standing gap as every prior phase, superseded in practice by the user's manual cross-tenant verification (see Phase 7's entry below, which covers the same underlying tables).

### Phase 7 — Embeddings + pgvector — completed 2026-08-12
- What exists now: `pgvector` extension enabled; `knowledge_chunks` gained a nullable `embedding vector(1536)` column; `match_knowledge_chunks(p_business_id, p_query_embedding, p_match_count)` — a `security invoker` Postgres function, tenant-scoped by an explicit `business_id` parameter in its query body (not just RLS), granted to `authenticated` only. `lib/embeddings.ts` — `embedText`/`embedTexts`, both L2-normalizing every result (mandatory for `gemini-embedding-001`, which does not auto-normalize truncated output). Internally, `TruncatedGeminiEmbeddings` extends `@langchain/core`'s `Embeddings` base class but calls `@google/genai`'s `embedContent` directly with `outputDimensionality: 1536` — **not** `@langchain/google-genai`'s `GoogleGenerativeAIEmbeddings`, which was installed to inspect per the approved prompt's Implementation Requirement 1, found (by reading its installed `.d.ts`) to expose no dimension-control mechanism at all, and then removed. `lib/knowledge.ts`'s `regenerateChunksForDocument` calls `embedTexts()` and stores an embedding with every chunk it (re)creates. `lib/retrieval.ts`'s `searchKnowledgeChunks(businessId, queryText, limit)` is the only current caller of the RPC — no chat/RAG consumer yet (Phase 8).
- **Post-launch gap found and fixed during user grant verification (2026-08-12):** `match_knowledge_chunks`'s original migration granted `execute` to `authenticated` but never revoked Postgres's default `PUBLIC` execute grant on new functions — unlike this project's tables, which default to zero grants since the Phase 3 `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES` migration. The function was reachable by `anon` and by completely unauthenticated connections. Not a live data leak — `security invoker` meant `knowledge_chunks`' RLS still blocked any actual cross-tenant row access regardless of caller role — but a real defense-in-depth failure. Fixed by `supabase/migrations/20260812163653_revoke_public_execute_on_match_knowledge_chunks.sql` (`prompts/fix-match-knowledge-chunks-public-execute-grant.md`). `docs/architecture.md`'s Database section documents this as a standing rule for all future functions; the schema-wide equivalent of the Phase 3 table fix is still open — see §8.
- Key files: `supabase/migrations/20260812161845_enable_pgvector_extension.sql`, `..._add_embedding_to_knowledge_chunks.sql`, `..._create_match_knowledge_chunks_function.sql`, `..._revoke_public_execute_on_match_knowledge_chunks.sql`, `supabase/tests/database/008_match_knowledge_chunks_tenant_isolation.sql`, `lib/embeddings.ts`, `lib/retrieval.ts`, `lib/knowledge.ts` (embedding generation added to chunk regeneration), `lib/supabase/types.ts` (`KnowledgeChunk.embedding` added), `.env.example`, `docs/architecture.md`.
- Migrations applied: **all four applied and verified by user 2026-08-12** (the three original Phase 7 migrations plus the grant-fix migration), verified via `npx supabase db query --linked` in place of the Supabase Dashboard, which was affected by a Supabase-side outage during testing — now documented in `docs/architecture.md`'s Database section as a viable fallback verification method for future phases.
- Env vars added: `GEMINI_API_KEY`, `GEMINI_EMBEDDING_MODEL=gemini-embedding-001`, `GEMINI_EMBEDDING_DIMENSION=1536` now required (see §5). `GEMINI_CHAT_MODEL=gemini-3.1-flash-lite` pinned but not yet wired into any code (Phase 9). No service-role client. (Variable name corrected from an earlier `GOOGLE_API_KEY` mislabel — see the Phase 8 §2 entry and §8 for the full correction.)
- Decisions made this phase: (1) `embedding` column is nullable, no backfill of pre-Phase-7 chunks — confirmed working via re-save (see Manual verification below); (2) no vector index yet, per `docs/phases.md`'s explicit "don't add reflexively" guidance; (3) `match_knowledge_chunks` is `security invoker` with an explicit `business_id` parameter, never `security definer`; (4) embedding generation is synchronous, inside the same Server Action as chunk regeneration; (5) L2-normalization is mandatory and explicit (`lib/embeddings.ts`'s `l2Normalize`); (6) `@langchain/google-genai` was installed, inspected, found to have no dimension-control mechanism, and removed — kept `@langchain/core`, added `@google/genai`; (7) `GEMINI_EMBEDDING_DIMENSION` is a read env var asserted against actual API output, not a hardcoded literal. Full reasoning: `prompts/phase-7-embeddings-pgvector.md`.
- Checks run: `npm run lint` — pass. `npx tsc --noEmit` — pass. `npm run build` — pass, no route changes.
- **Manual verification confirmed by user 2026-08-12, via `npx supabase db query --linked`:** L2 normalization confirmed working (norm ≈ 1 on all new embeddings). `match_knowledge_chunks` correctly ranks a chunk against itself at similarity 1.0, and correctly distinguishes semantically different content (0.60 similarity for an unrelated topic — a real, meaningful ranking gap, not a trivial pass/fail check). Legacy pre-Phase-7 chunks (created before the `embedding` column existed) confirmed to backfill correctly on re-save. Cross-tenant isolation and empty-result cases confirmed. The PUBLIC/anon execute-grant fix separately verified: permission denied for `anon`, success for `authenticated`.
- Known gaps carried forward: `supabase test db` (pgTAP `008_match_knowledge_chunks_tenant_isolation.sql`) has not been executed as an automated pgTAP run — same standing gap as every prior phase — but its exact assertions (correct-business ranking, cross-tenant exclusion, empty result for no-knowledge business) were all independently confirmed live via `npx supabase db query --linked`, per the manual verification above. The schema-wide function-default-privileges fix (`ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES ... REVOKE EXECUTE ON FUNCTIONS FROM PUBLIC`) remains open — see §8.

### Phase 8 — LangChain RAG — completed 2026-08-12, fully verified

- What exists now: `lib/rag.ts` (server-only) — `KnowledgeRetriever`, a `@langchain/core` `BaseRetriever` subclass wrapping the existing (Phase 7) `searchKnowledgeChunks()`, fixed to one `businessId` at construction time; `answerFromKnowledge(businessId, question)` — the retrieval-to-generation pipeline: retrieves via the retriever, returns the exported `FALLBACK_MESSAGE` (implementing `PRODUCT.md` §7's category-4 behavior) immediately with `grounded: false` and no model call when zero chunks come back, otherwise builds a `ChatPromptTemplate` (deliberately minimal grounding-only system prompt — not the full sales persona, which is Phase 9's job) and invokes `ChatGoogleGenerativeAI` (`@langchain/google-genai`) with `GEMINI_CHAT_MODEL`, returning `{ answer, grounded: true, sourceChunkIds }`. Chat-generation failures are wrapped in `AppError`; the pre-existing `searchKnowledgeChunks` failure path is left as-is (already safe). `app/dashboard/ai-test/{page.tsx,actions.ts,ask-form.tsx}` — a minimal, unnavigated manual-test surface (same "not wired into dashboard nav" convention as Phases 5/6), protected via `requireBusinessContext()`, Zod-validates the question (1–2000 chars) in the Server Action, and displays the answer, groundedness, and source chunk IDs.
- Key files: `lib/rag.ts`, `app/dashboard/ai-test/page.tsx`, `app/dashboard/ai-test/actions.ts`, `app/dashboard/ai-test/ask-form.tsx`, `.env.example`, `package.json`.
- Migrations applied: none. No new table, column, index, or RLS policy — this phase only reads via the existing `match_knowledge_chunks` RPC through `searchKnowledgeChunks`.
- Env vars added: none new. `GEMINI_CHAT_MODEL` (already pinned since Phase 7 by resolved decision D3) is now actually wired into code, per the user's explicit Decision-1 confirmation at approval time — `.env.example`'s note corrected from "not used until Phase 9" to "wired starting Phase 8." **Correction, not "fixed in passing" as first reported:** the actual, only correct variable name for the Gemini API key has always been `GEMINI_API_KEY`. Mid-implementation, `.env.example` briefly showed `GEMINI_API_KEY` while `.env.local` and every line of application code (`lib/embeddings.ts`, `lib/rag.ts`) used `GOOGLE_API_KEY` — this agent incorrectly treated `.env.example` as the stale outlier and "restored" it to `GOOGLE_API_KEY` to match the (also wrong) code and `.env.local`. The user caught this, confirmed `GEMINI_API_KEY` is correct, and updated `.env.local` accordingly. `lib/embeddings.ts:95`, `lib/rag.ts:73` (`process.env.GOOGLE_API_KEY!` → `process.env.GEMINI_API_KEY!`), `.env.example`, `docs/security.md` §5's env var table, and this file's §5 have all been corrected to `GEMINI_API_KEY`. See §8 for the full incident note.
- Decisions made this phase (see `prompts/phase-8-langchain-rag.md` for full reasoning): (1) `GEMINI_CHAT_MODEL` wired starting this phase, not Phase 9 — confirmed explicitly by the user at approval time; (2) manual test surface is a throwaway `/dashboard/ai-test` page, not the Phase 11 chat API or Phase 12 chat UI; (3) chat model is `@langchain/google-genai`'s `ChatGoogleGenerativeAI` — inspected its installed `.d.ts` before use per Implementation Requirement 1, found no capability gap (unlike Phase 7's embeddings class, which lacked dimension control — that gap doesn't apply to chat), so no `@google/genai`-direct fallback was needed; (4) no numeric similarity threshold — "zero chunks retrieved" is the only pre-model fallback bypass, reliance on the system prompt's own grounding instruction as the second layer; (5) retrieval limit stays at the existing default of 5 chunks; (6) no conversation/message/lead persistence — single-turn pipeline only.
- Checks run: `npm run lint` — pass, zero errors/warnings. `npx tsc --noEmit` — pass. `npm run build` — pass, `/dashboard/ai-test` compiles and is listed in the route manifest.
- **Manual verification confirmed by user 2026-08-12, all five steps in `prompts/phase-8-langchain-rag.md` passed:** (1) a zero-knowledge business got the fallback message with no Gemini call, confirmed via server logs; (2) a real test business ("Acme") got a grounded, correct answer with `sourceChunkIds` shown and traced back to its own chunks; (3) an unrelated question asked of Acme (return policy) was correctly declined rather than answered with a guess — validates Decision 4's no-hard-similarity-threshold approach, relying on the system prompt's own grounding instruction; (4) cross-tenant isolation held — a second test business ("Ghost Test Co.") never surfaced Acme's data and correctly fell back instead; (5) a forced `GEMINI_API_KEY` failure produced the safe generic error message, no raw provider error or stack trace, with the real key restored afterward. This satisfies Phase 8's exit criterion (`docs/phases.md`): a question with no supporting knowledge produces the fallback, not an invented answer — proven by test, not by inspection.
