### Phase 9 — Gemini AI Sales Employee — completed 2026-08-12, fully verified

- What exists now: `lib/rag.ts` extended in place — `answerFromKnowledge()` renamed to `askSalesEmployee(businessId, businessName, question, history?)`; `SYSTEM_TEMPLATE` rewritten into the full `PRODUCT.md` §7 persona (business identity, the four information categories, category-4 fallback instruction, competitor/general-knowledge restrictions, qualification framing, escalation instruction); a new `SalesEmployeeResponseSchema` (Zod) and `.withStructuredOutput()` (`@langchain/google-genai`) replace free-text generation, so the model returns `{ answer, escalate, escalationReason }` as a typed object, not parsed text. `askSalesEmployee()` accepts an optional, non-persisted `history: { role, content }[]` (via a LangChain `MessagesPlaceholder`) for future Phase 11 use — no current caller passes a non-empty one. The Phase 8 zero-knowledge hard bypass (`FALLBACK_MESSAGE`, no model call) is unchanged. `KnowledgeRetriever` is unchanged. `lib/business-context.ts`'s `BusinessContext`/`requireBusinessContext()` gained `businessName`, sourced from the `Business` row `getBusinessForOrg()` already fetches — no extra query. `app/dashboard/ai-test/{actions.ts,ask-form.tsx}` updated to call `askSalesEmployee()` and display the `escalate`/`escalationReason` signal. `docs/architecture.md` gained an "AI orchestration: retrieval-to-generation pipeline (Phases 8-9)" section.
- Key files: `lib/rag.ts`, `lib/business-context.ts`, `app/dashboard/ai-test/actions.ts`, `app/dashboard/ai-test/ask-form.tsx`, `docs/architecture.md`.
- Migrations applied: none. No new table, column, index, or RLS policy — same as Phase 8, this phase only changes what's sent to and read back from Gemini.
- Env vars added: none new. Same `GEMINI_API_KEY`/`GEMINI_CHAT_MODEL` wired since Phase 8.
- Decisions made this phase (see `prompts/phase-9-gemini-sales-employee.md` for full reasoning): (1) business profile context limited to `businesses.name` — no richer profile invented; (2) `requireBusinessContext()` extended with `businessName` at no extra query cost, following the Phase 4 low-risk-extension precedent; (3) `answerFromKnowledge` renamed to `askSalesEmployee` and extended in place, not split into a parallel module — `KnowledgeRetriever`/`FALLBACK_MESSAGE` stay in `lib/rag.ts`, no file move; (4) structured output via `withStructuredOutput()` + Zod, not text parsing; (5) escalation scoped to what a single turn can determine (explicit human request, complaint, unauthorized-commitment request) — "repeated unknown" (needs persisted conversation state, Phase 11) and business-configured triggers (needs config UI, Phase 13) explicitly deferred, not faked; (6) `history` parameter added for future Phase 11 use, but no multi-turn UI built this phase; (7) the Phase 8 zero-chunk hard bypass preserved exactly, including `escalate: false` on that path; (8) no lead extraction/persistence, D6 still open, not this phase's job.
- Checks run: `npm run lint` — pass, zero errors/warnings. `npx tsc --noEmit` — pass. `npm run build` — pass, `/dashboard/ai-test` compiles and is listed in the route manifest.
- **Follow-up fix, same day, per `prompts/fix-rag-grounded-used-context-signal.md`:** manual testing found `grounded` was computed purely from "the retriever found ≥1 chunk" (`documents.length > 0`), independent of whether the model's answer actually used that context — Test 7 (a general-knowledge question, correctly declined) still showed `grounded: true` with source chunks displayed, misrepresenting a declined answer as data-backed. Fixed by adding `usedContext: boolean` to `SalesEmployeeResponseSchema` (model-self-reported, same trust level as `escalate` — a display signal only, never used for tenant scoping or authorization) and redefining `grounded` as `documents.length > 0 && result.usedContext`. `usedContext` is also exposed as its own field on `SalesEmployeeResponse` (not just folded into `grounded`), per the user's explicit request, for finer-grained future debugging — `app/dashboard/ai-test/ask-form.tsx` displays it alongside `grounded`. The zero-chunk hard bypass is unaffected (`grounded: false, usedContext: false`, still no model call). `docs/architecture.md`'s AI orchestration section updated to document the corrected semantics and why the first cut was wrong. Checks re-run after this fix: `npm run lint`/`npx tsc --noEmit`/`npm run build` all pass.
- **Manual verification confirmed by user 2026-08-12, all steps in `prompts/phase-9-gemini-sales-employee.md` and `prompts/fix-rag-grounded-used-context-signal.md` passed.** Notably: (1) Test 1's cross-business exit criterion — two different businesses gave two grounded, non-overlapping, correctly-voiced answers to the same question; (2) Test 7's `usedContext: false` result was verified as the genuine model-self-report path, not the zero-chunk hard bypass — confirmed by checking that `sourceChunkIds` showed three real chunk IDs alongside `usedContext: false`, meaning Gemini was actually invoked, received real context, and correctly declined to use it (see the two-branch distinction below); (3) the zero-knowledge/forced-provider-failure regressions and the escalation/persona/competitor/general-knowledge-decline tests all passed as specified.
- **Two investigation items from manual testing, both closed with confirmed root causes, not assumptions:**
  1. *`grounded`/`usedContext` ambiguity between the two "false" paths* — `askSalesEmployee()`'s zero-chunk hard bypass and its model-invoked-but-declined branch both return `usedContext: false`/`grounded: false`, and the UI's fallback label text ("No matching knowledge -- fallback response.") reads the same for both, even though only the hard-bypass case has literally zero matching knowledge. The reliable way to distinguish them is `sourceChunkIds`: non-empty means the model-invoked branch ran (real Gemini call, self-reported non-use); empty means the hard bypass fired (no Gemini call at all). Documented here since it wasn't previously written down anywhere; no code change made — this is a debugging note, not a bug, and the fields already carry enough information to disambiguate.
  2. *Stale-looking "server log" during Tests 3-6, recurring across two testing rounds* — root cause confirmed, not assumed: the terminal running `next dev` was checked directly and only shows normal request/status/timing lines (e.g. `POST /dashboard/ai-test 200 in 1234ms`), never structured result content. No `console.log`/successful-response logging exists anywhere in `app/dashboard/ai-test/` or `lib/rag.ts` (re-verified by full-file read, not just grep, across two separate investigation rounds), and no module-level shared/mutable state exists in `lib/rag.ts`/`lib/retrieval.ts`/`lib/embeddings.ts` that could leak one request's result into another's (each `askSalesEmployee()` call constructs its own `ChatGoogleGenerativeAI`/`KnowledgeRetriever` instance — no cache, no singleton — so this was never a cross-request data-isolation risk). The actual source was the browser's DevTools console, not the terminal: `useActionState` re-renders in two phases per submission (an immediate pending-state render, then the resolved-state render once the Server Action returns), and a hook-state-change log view in the browser devtools can display that sequencing in a way that reads as "one behind" the DOM, which already reflects the correct, current value. Confirmed cosmetic — a devtools-inspection-ordering artifact in React's hook lifecycle, not an application bug. No fix needed or made.
- Known gaps carried forward: none. Both this phase and its same-day follow-up fix are fully implemented and fully verified.

### Phase 10 — Lead extraction and creation — completed 2026-08-13, fully verified

- What exists now: two new tables, `conversations` (a deliberate minimal stub — `id`, `business_id`, `source`, `created_at` only, no messages/status/chat API — created lazily only when a lead is actually captured) and `leads` (the full `PRODUCT.md` §8 field spec, with a `leads_contact_required` check constraint requiring at least one of `contact_email`/`contact_phone`). Four new server-only modules, kept deliberately separate per `AGENTS.md` §9: `lib/lead-extraction.ts` (AI-only — `extractLead()`, `LeadExtractionSchema` via `withStructuredOutput()`, reuses `lib/rag.ts`'s now-exported `getChatModel()`), `lib/conversations.ts`/`lib/leads.ts` (database-only CRUD, same tenant-filter/`AppError`/boolean-return conventions as `lib/products.ts`), and `lib/lead-capture.ts` (orchestration — `captureLeadFromConversation()`: extracts, normalizes contact fields via `lib/schemas/lead.ts`'s `normalizeEmail()`/`normalizePhone()`, resolves `interest_id` via a tenant-scoped exact-name lookup against that business's own `products`/`services` — never a raw AI-suggested ID — applies the email/phone gate, and only then persists). Two new throwaway/minimal pages, same un-navigated convention as every prior phase: `/dashboard/leads-test` (multi-turn conversation tester, client-state transcript, finally exercising Phase 9's unused `askSalesEmployee()` `history` parameter, with an "End conversation & extract lead" action) and `/dashboard/leads` (list + a `status` dropdown per row wired to `updateLeadStatus()`). `docs/architecture.md` gained a "Lead extraction (Phase 10)" subsection.
- Key files: `supabase/migrations/20260813120000_create_conversations_table.sql`, `20260813120005_create_leads_table.sql`, `supabase/tests/database/009_conversations_tenant_isolation.sql`, `010_leads_tenant_isolation.sql`, `lib/conversations.ts`, `lib/leads.ts`, `lib/lead-extraction.ts`, `lib/lead-capture.ts`, `lib/schemas/lead.ts`, `lib/rag.ts` (`getChatModel()` exported for reuse), `lib/supabase/types.ts` (added `Conversation`/`Lead`/`LeadInterestType`/`LeadQualification`/`LeadStatus`), `lib/products.ts`/`lib/services.ts` (no changes — their existing `listProductsForBusiness()`/`listServicesForBusiness()` reused as-is), `app/dashboard/leads-test/**`, `app/dashboard/leads/**`, `docs/architecture.md`.
- Migrations applied: **applied and verified by user 2026-08-13** — `npx supabase db push --linked` succeeded (once past the neutralized `20260812213356_...sql` blocker, see the separate migration-file-fix entry below) for both `conversations` and `leads`.
- Env vars added: none new.
- Decisions made this phase (see `prompts/phase-10-lead-extraction-creation.md` for full reasoning): (1) **`conversations` is a minimal stub built ahead of Phase 11, confirmed directly with the user** (a real phase-ordering conflict — `leads.conversation_id` is required but Phase 11 owns real conversation creation — was surfaced and asked about rather than resolved unilaterally); (2) a conversation with no contact info leaves zero database trace (no `conversations` row, no `leads` row) — confirmed intentional for v1, not an accidental gap, since conversation-count telemetry isn't a `PRODUCT.md` goal yet; (3) a new, separate `/dashboard/leads-test` page, not a retrofit of `/dashboard/ai-test`; (4) `interest_id` is only ever resolved via a tenant-scoped exact-name match against real catalog rows, never taken directly from AI output; (5) contact-field validation happens in two layers (loose AI schema, then `lib/schemas/lead.ts`'s strict normalization/persistence schema); (6) the "at least one of email/phone" rule is enforced three times (app logic, Zod refine, DB check) by design, matching this project's existing defense-in-depth pattern; (7) `qualification`/`qualification_reason` are two separate DB columns, always populated, never recomputed after creation; (8) `status` allows free transitions via a simple dropdown, no enforced state machine; (9) lead CRUD authorization follows D7's "any authenticated org member" precedent; (10) `updateLeadStatus()` follows `updateProduct()`'s exact silent-no-op contract on a cross-tenant attempt — confirmed explicitly by the user before approval, not assumed.
- Checks run: `npm run lint` — pass, zero errors/warnings. `npx tsc --noEmit` — pass. `npm run build` — pass, both new routes (`/dashboard/leads-test`, `/dashboard/leads`) compile and are listed in the route manifest.
- **Manual verification confirmed by user 2026-08-13, all seven steps in `prompts/phase-10-lead-extraction-creation.md` passed:** (1) a multi-turn conversation naming a real catalog product plus an email correctly resolved `interest_id` to that product's row; (2) a phone-only lead (no email) was created correctly; (3) a conversation with no contact info produced zero rows in both `conversations` and `leads`; (4) an unmatched/made-up product name left `interest_type` set but `interest_id` null; (5) `qualification` correctly distinguished a hot lead (explicit purchase intent) from a cold one (explicit disinterest); (6) cross-tenant isolation held on both reads (`/dashboard/leads`) and a cross-tenant status-update attempt (silently affected zero rows, per `updateLeadStatus()`'s contract); (7) all four status transitions persisted correctly across a page refresh. This satisfies Phase 10's exit criterion (`docs/phases.md`): "a realistic conversation produces a correctly-attributed, validated lead row" — proven by test, not by inspection.

### Phase 11 — Chat API — completed and fully verified 2026-08-13

- What exists now: `app/api/chat/route.ts` (`POST`/`OPTIONS`) — the app's first public, unauthenticated endpoint. Resolves `{ widgetKey, conversationId?, message }` (Zod-validated, no `business_id` field exists in the schema at all) to a `business_id` via `lib/widget-auth.ts`'s `resolveBusinessFromWidgetKey(key, origin)`, which checks the request's `Origin` header (falling back to `Referer`'s origin) against the resolved business's `widget_allowed_origin` column *before* treating the key as valid, and fails closed (generic `WidgetAuthError`, no detail on which check failed) if the key is unknown, the origin doesn't match, or `widget_allowed_origin` is still unset. Three rate-limit checks (`lib/rate-limit.ts`'s `checkAndIncrementRateLimit`, scopes `ip`/`key`/`conversation`, hardcoded limits 30/120/20 per 5 minutes) gate the request at the IP, widget-key, and conversation stages, each backed by one atomic Postgres upsert-increment function (`public.increment_rate_limit_counter`, avoids a read-then-write race). A returning `conversationId` must belong to the resolved business (`lib/conversations.ts`'s new `getConversationForBusiness`) or the request is rejected with a generic `400`, indistinguishable from "doesn't exist." Every turn (both the prospect's message and the AI's reply) is now persisted unconditionally to a new `public.messages` table via `lib/messages.ts`, regardless of whether a lead is ever captured — a deliberate change from Phase 10's lazy, lead-triggered `conversations` row creation. `askSalesEmployee()` (Phase 9, unchanged) is invoked with the conversation's last 20 messages as `history`. The public response body is deliberately minimal: `{ conversationId, answer, escalate }` — no `sourceChunkIds`/`usedContext`/`escalationReason`, which stay dashboard-debugging-only fields. `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` plus an `OPTIONS` preflight handler are set on every response — a browser-compatibility concern, not the security boundary (the real check is the server-side stored-origin comparison, which runs regardless of CORS). `lib/supabase/service.ts` is the app's first service-role Supabase client (`SUPABASE_SECRET_KEY`), used only on this request path, where RLS has no Clerk session to key off of at all — the application-layer `business_id` filter in every query is the *only* tenant boundary here, not defense-in-depth on top of RLS. `lib/conversations.ts`'s `createConversation` and the new `lib/messages.ts` both take the Supabase client as an explicit parameter so the Clerk-session dashboard path (`lib/lead-capture.ts`, one call-site updated) and the service-role widget path share one query implementation per table. A new minimal, un-navigated `/dashboard/widget-settings` page shows the business's real `widget_key` (read-only) and a form to set `widget_allowed_origin`, restricted via a column-level `UPDATE` grant so this path can't touch `widget_key` or rename the business.
- Key files: `supabase/migrations/20260813130000_add_widget_columns_to_businesses.sql`, `..._create_messages_table.sql`, `..._create_rate_limit_counters_table.sql`, `..._create_increment_rate_limit_counter_function.sql`, `supabase/tests/database/011_messages_tenant_isolation.sql`, `lib/supabase/service.ts`, `lib/widget-auth.ts`, `lib/rate-limit.ts`, `lib/messages.ts`, `lib/conversations.ts` (client-injection refactor, new `getConversationForBusiness`), `lib/lead-capture.ts` (one call-site update), `lib/supabase/types.ts` (`Business` gained `widget_key`/`widget_allowed_origin`, new `Message`/`MessageRole` types), `.env.example` (`SUPABASE_SECRET_KEY` now required), `app/api/chat/route.ts`, `app/dashboard/widget-settings/page.tsx`, `..._/actions.ts`, `..._/widget-origin-form.tsx`, `docs/architecture.md` (new "Public chat widget (Phase 11)" subsection).
- Migrations applied: **all four applied and grants/function-privileges verified live 2026-08-13** via `npx supabase db push --linked` and `npx supabase db query --linked` — see §1 for the false-alarm `realtime.messages` investigation. Confirmed: `public.messages` = `authenticated: SELECT` only, no `anon` grant at all; `public.rate_limit_counters` = no `anon`/`authenticated` grant at all; `public.increment_rate_limit_counter` = `service_role`-only `EXECUTE` (`has_function_privilege` false for `anon`/`authenticated`, true for `service_role`); `businesses.widget_allowed_origin` has a column-level `UPDATE` grant for `authenticated`, `businesses.widget_key` does not (only `SELECT`/`INSERT`, the latter inherited from the existing whole-row `INSERT` grant, Phase 4); two existing test businesses backfilled with distinct real `widget_key` values via the column default, `widget_allowed_origin` correctly `null` on both (fails closed).
- Env vars added: `SUPABASE_SECRET_KEY` — **required as of this phase** (previously "planned, not yet required" since Phase 3). Secret, server-only, restricted to `lib/supabase/service.ts`.
- Decisions made this phase (see `prompts/phase-11-chat-api.md` for full reasoning): (1) widget key generated via a DB column default (`gen_random_uuid()`), not application code; (2) `widget_allowed_origin` starts `null`, widget fails closed until configured; (3) a new minimal `/dashboard/widget-settings` page, no key rotation/regeneration built; (4) first service-role client, restricted to the widget path, RLS-bypass is the deliberate exception on that path only; (5) `lib/conversations.ts`/`lib/messages.ts` take the Supabase client as a parameter rather than constructing one, so both auth paths share one query implementation; (6) rate limiting via one generic `rate_limit_counters` table and one atomic increment function, three scopes (`ip`/`key`/`conversation`), hardcoded limits (30/120/20 per 5 min) — not product-specified, flagged as adjustable; (7) `rate_limit_counters` gets RLS enabled/forced with zero policies even though it holds no business-owned data; (8) `messages` gets full RLS shape but `authenticated` = `SELECT` only, no write grant — only the service role writes in v1; (9) conversation/message persistence is now unconditional on every request, resolving the "decision for whenever Phase 11 lands" question Phase 10 explicitly deferred; (10) a mismatched/nonexistent `conversationId` gets a generic `400`, no existence-leak distinction; (11) origin validation is exact-string match, `Origin` header first then `Referer`'s origin, no wildcard/subdomain matching; (12) `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` on every response — CORS is a browser-compatibility concern here, not the security boundary; (13) public response body excludes `sourceChunkIds`/`usedContext`/`escalationReason`; (14) message length bound 1–2000 chars, matching the existing `ai-test` bound; (15) history capped at the last 20 messages, most-recent-last (queried descending-then-reversed — an earlier draft of the prompt had this backwards, caught and corrected before implementation, see the prompt's Implementation Requirement 6 note); (16) lead-capture triggering from the widget is explicitly **not** wired this phase — no trigger point is specified by any phase yet; (17) no `rate_limit_counters` cleanup job exists.
- Checks run: `npm run lint` — pass, zero errors/warnings. `npx tsc --noEmit` — pass. `npm run build` — pass, `/api/chat` and `/dashboard/widget-settings` both compile and are listed in the route manifest.
- **Corrective fix, same day, folded into this closure (`prompts/fix-widget-retrieval-client-injection.md`):** the first real `/api/chat` request during the user's own manual verification 500'd with `permission denied for function match_knowledge_chunks (42501)`. `lib/retrieval.ts`'s `searchKnowledgeChunks()` and `lib/rag.ts`'s `KnowledgeRetriever`/`askSalesEmployee()` had been missed from this phase's own client-injection pattern (Decision #5, already correctly applied to `lib/conversations.ts`/`lib/messages.ts`) — they still constructed a Clerk-session client internally, so every widget request queried `match_knowledge_chunks` as `anon`, which correctly has no `EXECUTE` grant on it (Phase 7's fix, untouched here). Fixed by giving both functions a leading `supabase` parameter and updating every call site: `app/api/chat/route.ts` now passes its service-role client through retrieval, not just persistence; `/dashboard/ai-test`/`/dashboard/leads-test` now pass `createServerSupabaseClient()` explicitly (signature-only change, no behavior change). `lib/lead-extraction.ts` confirmed to touch no Supabase client at all, unaffected. Checks re-run and pass, and this fix is covered by the same manual re-test below, not left as a separate open item.
- **Manual verification confirmed by user 2026-08-13, all 11 steps in `prompts/phase-11-chat-api.md` plus the corrective fix's re-test passed:** migrations/grants live-verified (`businesses` column-scoped `UPDATE`, `messages` `SELECT`-only for `authenticated`, `increment_rate_limit_counter` service-role-only `EXECUTE`); a fresh widget-key conversation returned `200` with a grounded answer, `conversations`/`messages` persistence confirmed via direct DB inspection; a multi-turn follow-up with the same `conversationId` retained context correctly, and correctly declined an out-of-knowledge question (offered human handoff) instead of fabricating; a wrong `Origin` header and an invalid/garbage widget key both correctly returned `401` with the same indistinguishable generic message; a cross-tenant `conversationId` (one business's key against another's conversation id) returned `400` with zero rows written to the wrong business before rejection; a forged `business_id` field in the request body was silently ignored, response and persisted rows both correctly scoped to the key-resolved business; the IP rate limit (30/5min fixed window) correctly tripped `429` under sustained load, with no Gemini call or message persistence for the rejected request; cross-tenant isolation held under a direct aggregation query across both test businesses (no cross-contamination); and an unconfigured `widget_allowed_origin` correctly returned `401` even with a fully valid widget key and a matching `Origin` header, proving decision 2's fail-closed default. The corrective fix was independently re-confirmed: the `42501` error is gone, and `/dashboard/ai-test`/`/dashboard/leads-test` are confirmed unaffected. This satisfies Phase 11's exit criterion (`docs/phases.md`): "the endpoint is exercised end-to-end with a widget key, is rate limited, and rejects a forged or mismatched `business_id`" — proven by test, not by inspection.
- Known gaps carried forward: `supabase test db` (pgTAP `011_messages_tenant_isolation.sql`) not run as an automated pgTAP run — same standing gap as every prior phase (the local pgTAP runner requires Docker, which this environment doesn't have — unrelated to the live `db push`/`db query`/manual-test verification, all of which are independent of that gap and all of which did happen). Lead-capture is not triggered from the widget (deliberately out of scope, decision 16 above). `rate_limit_counters` has no cleanup job (decision 17). Neither gap blocks this phase's closure.

### Phase 12 — Chat UI — completed and fully verified 2026-08-13

- What exists now: `public/widget-loader.js` (static, vanilla JS, the entire embed mechanism — a business embeds one `<script data-widget-key="...">` tag) creates a fixed-position `<iframe>` pointed at a new `/widget/embed` page and is the component that actually calls `/api/chat`, from the host page's own JS context. `app/(widget)/widget/embed/page.tsx` and its client component tree (`_components/`: `widget-app`, `launcher-button`, `panel`, `panel-header`, `message-list`, `message-bubble`, `typing-indicator`, `composer`, `escalation-banner`; `_lib/`: `use-widget-chat.ts`, `post-message.ts`) render the launcher bubble and chat panel, communicating with the loader entirely via a `widget:send`/`widget:response`/`widget:error`/`widget:resize`/`widget:viewport` postMessage protocol — the iframe never calls `fetch` itself. `app/(widget)/layout.tsx` is an independent root layout (own `<html>`/`<body>`, no `ClerkProvider`, no dashboard header) using Next.js 16's route-groups mechanism, confirmed against `node_modules/next/dist/docs/01-app/03-api-reference/03-file-conventions/{layout,route-groups}.md` before implementing rather than assumed. Every pre-existing route (`layout.tsx`, `page.tsx`, `globals.css`, `dashboard/`, `onboarding/`, `session-tasks/`, `sign-in/`, `sign-up/`) moved into `app/(dashboard)/` verbatim — same URLs, same behavior, confirmed via the build's route manifest. `app/api/**` untouched (route handlers don't participate in the layout tree). A from-scratch visual system (indigo/neutral palette, Inter, `--widget-*` CSS custom properties scoped to `app/(widget)/widget.css` only) was designed using `ui-ux-pro-max`'s reference CSVs (its Python search script could not run in this environment — no real Python interpreter, only Windows Store alias stubs — so its data files were read directly instead) and 21st.dev MCP search results used as structural/visual inspiration only, no component code installed.
- **A real architectural bug in the approved prompt's own draft was caught and corrected before implementation, not discovered after**: the original draft had the `/api/chat` fetch happening inside the iframe's own (same-origin-with-this-app) document, which would send this app's own `Origin` header on every request instead of the host page's real one — permanently breaking `lib/widget-auth.ts`'s per-business origin allowlist for every genuine cross-domain embed (it would only "work" if a business set `widget_allowed_origin` to this app's own domain, defeating the feature's entire purpose). The user identified this during prompt review; the prompt was corrected (fetch moved to `public/widget-loader.js`, iframe reduced to a pure postMessage-driven rendering surface) before any code was written.
- Key files: `public/widget-loader.js`, `app/(widget)/layout.tsx`, `app/(widget)/widget.css`, `app/(widget)/widget/embed/page.tsx`, `app/(widget)/widget/embed/_components/*.tsx`, `app/(widget)/widget/embed/_lib/{use-widget-chat,post-message}.ts`, `app/(dashboard)/**` (moved, not modified), `docs/architecture.md` (new "Public chat widget UI (Phase 12)" subsection).
- Migrations applied: none. No new table, column, env var, or npm dependency — confirmed against `package.json` before and after.
- Env vars added: none.
- Decisions made this phase (see `prompts/phase-12-chat-ui.md` for full reasoning, including the corrected Decision 1): (1) embed mechanism is a `<script>` loader + same-origin iframe, not a directly-mounted React component, for host-page CSS/JS isolation and to avoid introducing a bundler; (1, corrected) the loader — not the iframe — performs the real `/api/chat` fetch, so the request's `Origin` header is genuine; (2) the widget route gets its own independent Next.js root layout via route groups, verified against the installed docs rather than assumed; (3) no `businessName` personalization — the API contract has no such field, and adding one would extend rather than consume it; (4) no conversation persistence across a page reload, no new `GET` endpoint; (5) light-mode-only visual system, grounded in `ui-ux-pro-max` data; (6) no new npm dependency for UI components — hand-built Tailwind, 21st.dev used for inspiration only; (7) `escalate: true` renders as passive text guidance only, no new lead-capture form; (8) `401` on the first request of a session shows a quiet panel-level message; `429`/`500`/network errors get an inline per-message retry; (9) only launcher position (`data-position`) is configurable, no per-business theming.
- **Post-implementation-report polish fix, same session:** the `impeccable` design hook flagged `TypingIndicator`'s use of Tailwind's `animate-bounce` (a spring/overshoot easing) as dated/tacky. Rather than suppress the finding, it was fixed: a custom `widget-typing-dot` keyframe animation (`app/(widget)/widget.css`) using a smooth `cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1)` exponential ease-out replaced `animate-bounce`, with its own `prefers-reduced-motion` guard. Checks re-run and passed after this change.
- Checks run: `npm run lint` — pass. `npx tsc --noEmit` — pass (after `npx next typegen` regenerated route types following the `app/` restructure — the stale `.next/types` cache initially produced unrelated `Cannot find module '.../page.js'` errors for moved routes, resolved by clearing `.next` and regenerating, not a real type error). `npm run build` — pass; route manifest confirms all pre-existing routes unchanged plus the new `/widget/embed`. Re-confirmed clean after the typing-indicator polish fix above.
- **Verification happened in two stages.** The implementation session proved the origin-check fix at the HTTP/protocol level via direct `curl` requests carrying the exact `Origin` header `public/widget-loader.js` would send from a genuinely different origin, against a second real test business, including a successful multi-turn exchange and correctly-scoped persistence — but had no browser-automation tool, so real-browser behavior (iframe/postMessage wiring, visual rendering, responsive/mobile behavior, keyboard navigation, screen-reader behavior) was left as an explicit gap. **The user then ran all 12 manual testing steps from the prompt in a real browser and confirmed every one passed** — same-origin sanity, the real cross-origin case (now confirmed working in-browser, not just via curl), mobile/responsive full-screen behavior both directions, garbage-key and origin-mismatch quiet failure, IP rate limit inline retry with intact transcript and cooldown recovery, a simulated `500` with successful retry after the real key was restored, a full keyboard-only pass (focus rings, Enter/Shift+Enter, Escape-returns-focus), a Windows Narrator screen-reader pass (launcher/panel announced, new replies auto-announced via `aria-live`), and the `noindex` directive reconfirmed present. See the §1 entry above for the full list.
- Known gaps carried forward: none — the real-browser verification gap flagged at implementation time is now closed. No `businessName` personalization, no reload persistence, no lead-capture wiring, no per-business theming — all deliberately out of scope, see the prompt's "Out of scope" section. `impeccable`'s live critique/polish pass was not run as a full pass (no live browser available to the implementing session) beyond the one finding fixed above; not required for closure since the user's own real-browser pass covered visual/interaction correctness.

### Phase 14a — `check_product_details` tool — completed and fully verified 2026-08-14

`prompts/phase-14a-check-product-details-tool.md` was approved after one round of correction requested by the user before approval: the original draft's manual-test plan referenced `/dashboard/ai-test`, which no longer exists (deleted in Phase 13a — see that entry above); the corrected prompt moved black-box verification to the real `/api/chat` widget endpoint (the Phase 11/12-proven curl method) and had the implementing session itself run the fail-closed/tenant-forgery cases directly via a throwaway script, reporting real captured output, rather than asking the user to hand-edit a deleted page. A `docs/architecture.md` documentation requirement was also added at the same correction.

What exists now: `lib/tools/check-product-details.ts` (new, `server-only`) exports `checkProductDetailsTool` (a `{ name, description, schema }` object shaped for `bindTools()`) and `executeCheckProductDetails(supabase, businessId, rawArgs)` — an authorized executor that re-validates `rawArgs` with `CheckProductDetailsInputSchema` (a `query` field only, no tenant field), looks up an exact case-insensitive name match in `products` then `services` (both queries `.eq("business_id", businessId)`-scoped), and returns a structured `{ found: true, type, name, description, price }` / `{ found: false, reason: "not_found" | "invalid_input" | "lookup_failed" }` result — never throws. `lib/rag.ts`'s `askSalesEmployee()` now runs a two-stage flow: a tools-bound call (`getChatModel().bindTools([checkProductDetailsTool])`) looped up to `MAX_TOOL_ITERATIONS = 2`, executing any `tool_calls` and feeding results back as `ToolMessage`s, followed by the pre-existing tools-unbound `withStructuredOutput(SalesEmployeeResponseSchema, ...)` call for the final answer. `SYSTEM_TEMPLATE` gained one instruction line telling the model when to use the tool. `askSalesEmployee()`'s external signature is unchanged — no caller needed updating. `docs/architecture.md` gained an "AI tool-calling (Phase 14a)" subsection (inside the existing "AI orchestration" section) documenting the two-stage flow and the provider-level reason for it.

**Why two stages — a provider-level constraint discovered by inspecting the installed packages, not a stylistic choice:** the installed `@langchain/google-genai`'s `withStructuredOutput` is implemented via Gemini's native `responseSchema` JSON-mode config, not forced function-calling (confirmed by reading `node_modules/@langchain/google-genai/dist/chat_models.js`), and a single Gemini call cannot carry both a `tools` list and a `responseSchema`. So tool use and the final structured `SalesEmployeeResponse` can never happen in the same model call — this is why every answer that reaches the model now costs at least 2 calls (up from 1), and up to `MAX_TOOL_ITERATIONS + 1` when the tool is actually used. Accepted as the necessary shape of tool-calling on this provider via this integration, not an oversight.

Key files: `lib/tools/check-product-details.ts`, `lib/rag.ts`, `docs/architecture.md`.

Migrations applied: none. No schema change — the tool reads existing `products`/`services` columns via existing tenant-scoped queries.

Env vars added: none.

Decisions made this phase (full reasoning in the prompt): (1) one tool covers both products and services, not two separate tools, matching the user's own framing; (2) name matching is case-insensitive exact match (`ilike` on the literal query), not fuzzy/substring — deliberately more precise than retrieval; (3) if a product and a service share a name, the product wins (products checked first) — an edge case not covered by the user's spec, decided here; (4) the two-stage tool-calling flow, `MAX_TOOL_ITERATIONS = 2`; (5) the zero-retrieval `FALLBACK_MESSAGE` hard bypass (Phase 8) is completely unchanged — the tool never runs when `KnowledgeRetriever` returns zero documents; (6) the tool executor re-validates its own Zod schema as defense in depth beyond `bindTools`'; (7) "authorization before execution" is satisfied by construction — `businessId` is a closure/parameter value, structurally absent from the tool's model-facing schema, never read from the model's arguments; (8) a tool-level DB failure returns `{ found: false, reason: "lookup_failed" }` rather than throwing, so it can't take down the whole answer; (9) no automated test framework was added — `npm test` still has no real implementation (same standing project-wide gap since Phase 3); fail-closed verification used a throwaway, uncommitted script instead, per the corrected prompt.

Checks run: `npm run lint` — pass, zero errors/warnings. `npx tsc --noEmit` — pass. `npm run build` — pass, route manifest unchanged (no new routes, this is a `lib/`-only change).

**Self-verification performed by the implementing session 2026-08-14 (not yet independently confirmed by the user):**
- Direct fail-closed tests (Group B, throwaway `scratch/check-product-details-manual-test.ts`, run via `NODE_OPTIONS=--conditions=react-server npx tsx ...`, deleted after use — confirmed via `git status` that nothing under `scratch/` remains): a sanity-check call for Business ("Acme Test Co.")'s own real product `"Test Product - 1"` correctly returned `{ found: true, type: "product", name: "Test Product - 1", description: "This is the edited product...", price: 99 }`. **Malformed input:** `executeCheckProductDetails(businessId, { query: 123 })` returned `{ found: false, reason: "invalid_input" }`, logged, no throw. **Forged/mismatched tenant (the Phase 14 exit criterion):** `executeCheckProductDetails(<"Ghost Test Co." businessId>, { query: "Test Product - 1" })` — a product that only exists for Acme — returned `{ found: false, reason: "not_found" }`, proving the tool is structurally incapable of reading another business's row regardless of the query string.
- Black-box tests (Group A) via a real local dev server hitting the actual `/api/chat` widget endpoint with real widget keys for both test businesses: happy path returned the exact stated price/description (`$99`, matching the DB description) for Acme's real product; a nonexistent product name returned fallback-style "I don't have that" language, no invented price; asking Ghost Test Co's widget key about Acme's product name returned the zero-retrieval `FALLBACK_MESSAGE` (Ghost Test Co. has zero knowledge documents at all, so the Phase 8 hard bypass fired before any model/tool call, so this particular run never actually reached Stage 1's `bindTools()` call — see the live cross-tenant re-test below, which the user correctly flagged as still needed); a same-conversation follow-up ("how much does that cost again?") correctly resolved "that" from history and re-ran the tool, confirming conversation continuity survived the two-stage change; an explicit "I want to speak to a person" message returned `escalate: true` in the response, confirming Stage 2's structured-output contract is intact. Server logs during all of this showed the tool's log line (businessId + query + outcome only, e.g. `check_product_details 96870a7a-... "Test Product - 1" found:product`) with no secrets or full prospect message content logged. The dev server was stopped after testing.
- **Live cross-tenant re-test, requested by the user after reviewing the report above (2026-08-14):** the original black-box cross-tenant test never exercised the tool loop at all (zero-retrieval fallback intercepted it before Stage 1), so it only proved the Group B direct-function test's guarantee a second time, not the live route-handler-to-tool-executor path. Re-tested properly: a temporary product (`"Phantom Exclusive Bundle"`, price 777) was inserted directly on Ghost Test Co (a business with zero products otherwise) via a throwaway script, so a real cross-tenant name collision existed. Acme Test Co (which has its own real knowledge, so retrieval is non-empty and Stage 1 genuinely runs) was then asked, via its real widget key against a fresh local dev server, "What is the price of the Phantom Exclusive Bundle?" The response: *"I apologize, but I do not have information regarding a product called the Phantom Exclusive Bundle at Acme Test Co...."* — no price, no description leaked. The server log confirmed the mechanism directly: `check_product_details 96870a7a-62c2-42d0-b1aa-3995802df8aa Phantom Exclusive Bundle not_found` — `96870a7a-...` is **Acme's own `businessId`** (the querying business), never Ghost's `e6fda150-...`, proving the live path injects the correct tenant scope into the tool call even when the model genuinely attempts to look up a name that only exists elsewhere. The temporary product and its row were deleted immediately after (confirmed zero products remain on Ghost Test Co), the dev server was stopped, and the throwaway scripts were deleted (`git status` confirmed clean, no fixture data or scratch files left behind).
- One incidental behavior observed, not a bug: on the escalation-trigger message, the model chose to call `check_product_details` with the entire message text as the query (`"I want to speak to a real person right now."`), which correctly returned `not_found` and did not affect the final answer's correctness — a slightly wasteful tool call the model made on its own initiative, not a security or correctness issue, but worth knowing if tool-call volume/cost is ever monitored.

**Manual verification confirmed by user 2026-08-14, all 7 Group A steps against the real `/api/chat` endpoint passed:** (1) happy path — exact price/description returned, matching the real Products record; (2) not-found path — graceful "don't have that" answer, no fabrication; (3) cross-tenant fail-closed (black-box) — Acme's widget key asking about the second business's real product returned the not-found case, no leaked data; (4) conversation continuity — the same `conversationId` correctly carried Test 1's context into a follow-up with no product name repeated; (5) `escalate` unaffected — an explicit human-handoff request correctly returned `escalate: true`; (6) zero-knowledge business — `FALLBACK_MESSAGE` still returns promptly, no tool round-trip; (7) server logs — the tool's log line was present and clean for tool-using requests, no secrets or full message content logged. Combined with the two Group B direct fail-closed proofs and the live cross-tenant re-test (both above, implementing-session-run with real captured output), this closes out the Phase 14 exit criterion (`docs/phases.md`) in full: both the isolated tool function and the live model-invoked path are proven tenant-safe, by test, not by inspection.

### Phase 14b — `check_faq_topic` tool — completed and fully verified 2026-08-14

`prompts/phase-14b-check-faq-topic-tool.md` was approved without correction and implemented this session, reusing 14a's proven two-stage pattern exactly — no new architectural discovery, per the user's own framing of this prompt going in.

What exists now: `lib/tools/check-faq-topic.ts` (new, `server-only`), same shape as `lib/tools/check-product-details.ts` — exports `checkFaqTopicTool` (`{ name, description, schema }` for `bindTools()`) and `executeCheckFaqTopic(supabase, businessId, rawArgs)`, which re-validates `rawArgs` against `CheckFaqTopicInputSchema` (a `topic` field only, no tenant field) and returns `{ found: true, question, answer }` / `{ found: false, reason: "not_found" | "invalid_input" | "lookup_failed" }` — never throws. `lib/rag.ts`'s tool loop now binds both tools in one call — `getChatModel().bindTools([checkProductDetailsTool, checkFaqTopicTool])` — with each `tool_calls` entry dispatched to its executor by `toolCall.name` (an unrecognized name fails closed to `{ found: false, reason: "invalid_input" }`, logged, rather than throwing). Same single loop, same `MAX_TOOL_ITERATIONS = 2`, no second cap. `SYSTEM_TEMPLATE` gained one more instruction line, matching the existing one's style. `docs/architecture.md`'s existing "AI tool-calling (Phase 14a)" subsection was extended (not duplicated) with the second tool and its matching-strategy rationale.

**FAQ topic matching, decided explicitly (per the user's requirement, since a "topic" is fuzzier than a product name):** case-insensitive **substring** `ilike` (`'%topic%'`) against `question` only — not exact match. `check_product_details`'s exact match works because product/service names are short labels a prospect names precisely; FAQ `question` values are full sentences a prospect will essentially never phrase verbatim, so an exact match would almost never fire. A substring match can legitimately return more than one row for a common keyword — rather than erroring (`.maybeSingle()` would) or an arbitrary database-order pick, the query takes the first match ordered by `created_at ascending` (same ordering `listFaqsForBusiness()` already uses), documented as a real, not-always-most-relevant tradeoff. The returned `answer` is always the literal stored text, never a paraphrase, same guarantee as `check_product_details`'s price/description.

Key files: `lib/tools/check-faq-topic.ts`, `lib/rag.ts`, `docs/architecture.md`.

Migrations applied: none. No schema change.

Env vars added: none.

Decisions made this phase (full reasoning in the prompt): (1) substring `ilike` on `question` only, not exact match, not full-text search/`pg_trgm`/embeddings; (2) first match by `created_at ascending` on multiple substring hits, not an error; (3) both tools share one `bindTools([...])` array and one bounded loop, dispatched by `toolCall.name`; (4) an unrecognized tool name in dispatch fails closed rather than throwing (defensive — should not occur with only two tools bound); (5) authorization/error-handling/logging conventions identical to 14a's, applied to this tool.

Checks run: `npm run lint` — pass, zero errors/warnings. `npx tsc --noEmit` — pass. `npm run build` — pass, route manifest unchanged (`lib/`-only change).

**Self-verification performed by the implementing session 2026-08-14 (not yet independently confirmed by the user):**
- Group B (direct, throwaway `scratch/check-faq-topic-manual-test.ts`, deleted after use): a sanity-check topic lookup for Acme Test Co.'s real FAQ ("What does Acme Test Co. provide?") correctly returned `{ found: true, question: "What does Acme Test Co. provide?", answer: "Acme Test Co. provides software and technology services." }`. **Malformed input:** `executeCheckFaqTopic(businessId, { topic: 123 })` returned `{ found: false, reason: "invalid_input" }`, logged, no throw. **Forged/mismatched tenant:** `executeCheckFaqTopic(<Ghost Test Co. businessId>, { topic: "provide?" })` — a topic that only matches Acme's FAQ — returned `{ found: false, reason: "not_found" }`.
- Group A (black-box, real local dev server, real `/api/chat` requests, Acme's real widget key): a message closely quoting the real FAQ's wording ("Quoting your FAQ directly, what does Acme Test Co. provide?") returned the exact stored answer verbatim, log line `check_faq_topic 96870a7a-... "What does Acme Test Co. provide?" found`. An unrelated made-up topic ("unicorn insurance claims") returned graceful "I don't have that" language, no fabrication. A regression request for `check_product_details` ("What is the price of Test Product - 1?") still worked correctly in the same session, confirming both tools coexist in one `bindTools([...])` call with no interference. **Live cross-tenant re-test** (same method as 14a's, user-endorsed): a temporary FAQ (`"What is Ghost Test Co.'s secret phantom refund process?"`, with a literal secret answer/code word) was seeded on Ghost Test Co, then Acme Test Co (real knowledge, so retrieval is non-empty and Stage 1 genuinely runs) was asked about that exact topic via its own real widget key. The response contained no trace of the secret answer or code word — a graceful "I don't have that" reply — and the server log confirmed the mechanism: `check_faq_topic 96870a7a-62c2-42d0-b1aa-3995802df8aa secret phantom refund process not_found` — **Acme's own `businessId`**, never Ghost's, proving the live route-handler-to-tool-executor path stays tenant-safe when the model genuinely attempts a cross-tenant `check_faq_topic` call. The temporary FAQ was deleted immediately after (confirmed zero FAQs remain on Ghost Test Co), the dev server was stopped, and all throwaway scripts were deleted (`git status` confirmed clean).
- One incidental behavior observed, not a bug, worth knowing: the model does not always call `check_faq_topic` even when a stored FAQ conceptually answers the question — on the first happy-path attempt (phrased as "What does your company provide?"), the model answered directly from retrieved context (pricing/plan details) without invoking the tool, since the reference context already seemed to answer the question. The tool only fired once phrasing more explicitly signaled a need for the FAQ's literal/official wording. This is a model judgment call within the system prompt's existing "use the tool when you need exact wording" instruction, not a security or correctness issue. Also observed: a topic phrased *not* as a substring of the stored question (e.g. "what you provide" vs. the stored "What does Acme Test Co. provide?") correctly returns `not_found` even though a human would recognize it as the same topic — the documented, accepted tradeoff of substring matching (Decision 1 above), not a defect.

**Manual verification confirmed by user 2026-08-14, all 8 steps passed:** happy path — a message closely quoting the FAQ's own wording returned the exact verbatim stored answer, not a paraphrase; not-found path — a graceful "don't have that" answer for an unrelated topic, no fabrication; regression check — `check_product_details` still works correctly in the same session, confirming both tools coexist properly in one `bindTools([...])` call; live cross-tenant re-test — a temporary FAQ seeded on the second business never leaked when queried via the first business's own real-knowledge-backed session, with the server log confirming the correct querying-business `businessId` was used, not the FAQ-owning business's; and a clean server-log/cleanup check.

Live testing then showed the model does not always choose `check_faq_topic` even given the FAQ's own exact official wording as the message ("What does Acme Test Co. provide?" invoked `check_product_details` instead, 3/3 times, since Acme's retrieved context is dominated by product/pricing chunks) — but a more explicitly FAQ-framed message ("Quoting your FAQ directly, what does Acme Test Co. provide?") reliably invoked `check_faq_topic`, returned the exact verbatim answer, and logged correctly (`check_faq_topic 96870a7a-... What does Acme Test Co. provide? found`). This confirmed the tool and its logging are both correct — tool invocation is a genuine model judgment call (same "incidental behavior, not a bug" already noted at implementation time), not a defect. Test 5 (server-log cleanliness) was then confirmed passing on that basis, and Tests 6–7 (malformed input, forged tenant) were independently re-run live with fresh captured output at the user's request, matching the implementation session's original results exactly.

### Phase 14c — `request_callback` tool — completed and fully verified 2026-08-14

`prompts/phase-14c-request-callback-tool.md` was approved after the user confirmed two flagged decisions before implementation: Decision 3 (`qualification: "warm"`, fixed `qualification_reason: "Prospect explicitly requested a callback via chat."`) and Decision 7 (add `unique (conversation_id)` on `leads` in the same migration, after a pre-flight duplicate check, with a stop-and-report instruction if any violations were found). The user also asked for the exact consent-instruction wording to be reported, not just described — see below.

**Why neither existing lead-creation path fit, confirmed by inspection before writing any code:** `lib/lead-capture.ts`'s `captureLeadFromConversation()` has **zero live callers anywhere in the codebase** — its only caller was `/dashboard/leads-test`'s "End conversation & extract lead" button, deleted in Phase 13a, so there is currently no live path that creates a `leads` row in production at all. Even if it were still wired up, it re-runs a separate whole-transcript Gemini extraction and always mints a brand-new `conversations` row — the wrong shape for a tool call that already has explicit args and an existing conversation. `lib/leads.ts`'s `createLead()`/`getLeadForConversation()` both construct a Clerk-session client internally (`createServerSupabaseClient()`), which has no valid session on the widget's service-role path — the same bug class `STATE.md`'s fix-widget-retrieval-client-injection entry already fixed once for retrieval, confirmed as a real risk here too, not hypothetical. The tool's executor instead does its own tenant-scoped `leads` queries using the passed-in `supabase` client (same convention `check-product-details.ts`/`check-faq-topic.ts` already established), reusing `lib/conversations.ts`'s `getConversationForBusiness()` (already client-injected) as the tenant-ownership guard before any write.

What exists now: `lib/tools/request-callback.ts` (new, `server-only`) exports `requestCallbackTool` and `executeRequestCallback(supabase, businessId, conversationId, rawArgs)` — both `businessId` **and** `conversationId` are injected from `askSalesEmployee`'s own trusted parameters, structurally absent from the tool's model-facing Zod schema. The executor: re-validates `rawArgs`; normalizes contact info via `lib/schemas/lead.ts`'s existing `normalizeEmail()`/`normalizePhone()` (reused, not duplicated) and fails closed with `missing_contact_info` if both end up null, before any write; verifies the conversation actually belongs to the business via `getConversationForBusiness()`, failing closed with `lookup_failed` on a mismatch; checks for an existing lead on that conversation and either **updates** it (`requested_callback = true` always; contact fields filled in only where currently `null`, never overwritten; notes appended with a `"\n\n"` separator, capped at 2000 chars) or **inserts** a new one (`qualification: "warm"`, fixed `qualification_reason`, `source: "request_callback_tool"`, `interest_type`/`interest_id: null`, `requested_callback: true`) — never a duplicate, backstopped by the new DB-level unique constraint. Every outcome is logged as one line (businessId + conversationId + outcome only — **never** the prospect's actual contact info or notes, since this is the first tool touching real PII).

`lib/rag.ts`'s `askSalesEmployee()` signature changed to `(supabase, businessId, conversationId, businessName, question, history)` — a real, deliberate breaking change (unlike 14a/14b, which added no parameter) — with `app/api/chat/route.ts`'s one call site updated to pass `conversation.id`. `bindTools([...])` now includes all three tools; the dispatch gained a third `else if` branch. `lib/supabase/types.ts`'s `Lead` type gained `requested_callback: boolean`. `PRODUCT.md` §8's field table gained a `requested_callback` row (required per `AGENTS.md`'s "no inventing lead fields beyond the approved spec" rule, since this prompt is the user's own explicit extension of it). `docs/architecture.md`'s tool-calling subsection was extended with the write-specific design.

**The exact consent-instruction wording added to `SYSTEM_TEMPLATE`, reported verbatim per the user's request:**
> "A prospect may want a callback in two ways: they ask for one directly, or you proactively offer one (for example, as part of deciding to escalate). Offering a callback is always just conversation -- it never calls a tool by itself. Only call the request_callback tool after the prospect has clearly agreed to a callback, in response to either their own request or your offer, AND you already have their email or phone number from this conversation. Never call this tool based only on your own guess that they might want one -- wait for their explicit agreement first, and if you don't have contact info yet, ask for it before calling the tool."

Key files: `lib/tools/request-callback.ts`, `lib/rag.ts`, `app/api/chat/route.ts`, `lib/supabase/types.ts`, `docs/architecture.md`, `PRODUCT.md`, `supabase/migrations/20260814070328_add_requested_callback_to_leads.sql`.

Migrations applied: **applied and grants verified live 2026-08-14** by the implementing session, via `npx supabase db push --linked`. Pre-flight duplicate check (`select conversation_id, count(*) from leads group by conversation_id having count(*) > 1`, run before writing the migration) found **zero** existing duplicates across the 6 pre-existing `leads` rows — safe to add the unique constraint, no unilateral resolution needed. Post-push, `has_column_privilege('authenticated', 'public.leads', 'requested_callback', 'INSERT'/'UPDATE')` both confirmed `true` live via `npx supabase db query --linked` — the existing table-level grant (no column list) already covered the new column automatically, exactly as predicted by inspection, no new grant statement needed. The `leads_conversation_id_unique` constraint confirmed live (`pg_constraint`, `contype = 'u'`).

Env vars added: none.

Decisions made this phase (full reasoning in the prompt; user-confirmed decisions noted): (1) `askSalesEmployee()` gains a required `conversationId` parameter; (2) the tool's own tenant-ownership check reuses `getConversationForBusiness()` rather than duplicating that query; (3) **user-confirmed** — `qualification: "warm"` / fixed `qualification_reason`, not a second AI-extraction call; (4) `source: "request_callback_tool"`, a fixed constant; (5) `interest_type`/`interest_id` left `null` — catalog resolution is out of scope for this tool; (6) existing-lead updates are fill-blank-only for contact fields, append-only for notes, never overwrite/clobber; (7) **user-confirmed** — `unique (conversation_id)` added in the same migration, gated on the pre-flight check; (8) `PRODUCT.md` §8 updated to keep the approved field spec in sync; (9) no new pgTAP file — a column/constraint addition to an already-isolation-tested table, no new RLS policy, same precedent as Phase 13b; (10) result shape uses `success`/`leadId`/`created`, deliberately distinct from the read tools' `found`-shaped results, since this is an action, not a lookup; (11) no `prospectConfirmed`-style boolean in the tool's schema — consent is enforced by system-prompt instruction only, per the user's explicit design, since a model-settable flag would add no real guarantee; (12) the Phase 8 zero-retrieval hard bypass is unchanged and still blocks all tool access (including this one) for a business with no knowledge at all — an inherited limitation, not new here; (13) a live cross-tenant re-test (14a/14b's pattern) doesn't apply the same way to this tool and wasn't attempted as one — `conversationId` is always derived server-side for the same business the widget key resolves to, so there's no way to reach `/api/chat` with a genuinely mismatched pair; the forged-tenant proof is necessarily a direct Group B call instead, stated explicitly rather than faking an impossible live test.

Checks run: `npm run lint` — pass, zero errors/warnings. `npx tsc --noEmit` — pass. `npm run build` — pass, route manifest unchanged (no new routes).

**Self-verification performed by the implementing session 2026-08-14 (not yet independently confirmed by the user):**
- Group B (direct, throwaway script against two fresh throwaway conversations — one on Acme, one on Ghost Test Co — deleted after use along with any lead they produced): **malformed input** (`{ contactEmail: 123 }`) → `{ success: false, reason: "invalid_input" }`, zero rows written. **No contact info** → `{ success: false, reason: "missing_contact_info" }`, zero rows written. **Forged/mismatched tenant** (Ghost's `businessId` + Acme's real `conversationId`) → `{ success: false, reason: "lookup_failed" }`, zero rows written to either business. **Successful create** → a single correctly-attributed row (`business_id`/`conversation_id` both correct, `requested_callback: true`, `qualification: "warm"`, exact fixed `qualification_reason`, `source: "request_callback_tool"`). **Successful update** on a second call for the same conversation → same `leadId` returned (not a new one), lead count stayed at exactly 1, the previously-null `contact_phone` got filled in, the already-set `contact_email` was correctly preserved (not overwritten by a `null` in the second call), and `notes` correctly appended with the `"\n\n"` separator.
- Group A (black-box, real local dev server, real `/api/chat` requests against Acme Test Co's real widget key): a single-turn explicit request with contact info in the same message correctly created a lead, verified via a direct DB read matching every expected field (one honest observation: the model filled `contact_name: "Jamie"` by inferring it from the email address's local part rather than the prospect stating a name outright — a mild inference, not a fabricated unrelated fact, noted for awareness). A **multi-turn consent flow** — the AI proactively offered a callback after declining an out-of-knowledge question (turn 1, confirmed via server log that `request_callback` did **not** fire on that turn, only `check_faq_topic` did), then the prospect agreed and gave a phone number in a follow-up turn (turn 2, confirmed `request_callback` fired and created a correctly-attributed row) — proved multi-turn consent, not just single-turn, works as designed. A **vague, non-committal message** with no contact info produced no tool call at all (confirmed via server log) and a reply asking for a phone number rather than silently succeeding. **Regression:** `check_faq_topic` and `check_product_details` both still fired correctly in the same session. **Logging:** the full server log was scanned for every piece of contact info used in this session (email, phone, notes text) — none of it appeared anywhere in the logs; every `request_callback` log line contained only businessId + conversationId + outcome, exactly as designed. Three real `leads` rows now exist on Acme Test Co. from this session's live testing (not deleted, since Group A test conversations/leads are treated the same as every prior phase's Group A test data — left as realistic test history, not throwaway fixtures) — noted here for transparency, not hidden.

**Manual verification confirmed by user 2026-08-14, all 5 independent steps passed, each with direct DB verification (not response-body-only):** (1) single-turn explicit request — exactly one lead row created, correctly attributed, correct phone/qualification/reason/source; (2) multi-turn proactive offer + consent — confirmed **no** lead row existed after the AI's offer alone (turn 1), and the row was only created after the prospect's explicit agreement plus contact info on turn 2, proving the consent gate holds across turns, not just within one message; (3) vague/no real consent — the AI asked for clarification rather than acting, and zero rows were written, proving the tool doesn't fire on inferred intent alone; (4) regression — `check_product_details` and `check_faq_topic` both still work correctly with the third tool in the loop; (5) server logs confirmed PII-free across all requests — no phone/email/notes text logged, only businessId/conversationId/outcome. Combined with the implementation session's five Group B direct fail-closed proofs (malformed input, no-contact-info, forged tenant, create, update-without-duplicate), this closes out the Phase 14 exit criterion (`docs/phases.md`) for `request_callback` in full — the first write-capable tool in this codebase, proven tenant-safe, consent-gated, and PII-safe on both the isolated-function and live-conversation paths, by test, not by inspection.

Known gaps carried forward: none.

### Phase 15a — Handoff state model and the AI-pause guard — completed and fully verified 2026-08-14

`prompts/phase-15a-handoff-state-and-ai-pause.md` was approved with one required addition before implementation: the user confirmed that escalation setting `needs_attention` without also flipping `control` is a deliberate, confirmed reinterpretation of `docs/phases.md`'s literal Phase 15 exit-criterion wording ("an escalation trigger reliably moves a live conversation to human control"), not an oversight — recorded in the prompt's "Decisions and assumptions" #1 and formally logged as decision D9 below. The user also flagged that `docs/phases.md`'s own Phase 15 entry is worth a small clarifying edit once all three stages close, not now.

What exists now: `conversations` gained `control` (`text`, `not null default 'ai'`, `check (control in ('ai', 'human'))`) and `needs_attention` (`boolean`, `not null default false`), plus a partial index on `(business_id, needs_attention) where needs_attention = true`. `lib/conversations.ts` gained `setConversationControl(supabase, businessId, conversationId, control)` (Clerk-session client, same no-existence-leak boolean-return contract as `updateLeadStatus()`) and `flagConversationNeedsAttention(supabase, businessId, conversationId)` (service-role client only, logs rather than throws on failure so it can never break the prospect's response). `app/api/chat/route.ts` now persists the prospect's message unconditionally, then checks `conversation.control === "human"` **before** calling `askSalesEmployee()` — if human-controlled, it returns a static, module-level `HUMAN_CONTROL_MESSAGE` string without ever calling the AI and without persisting that string as a message row. When the AI does run and returns `escalate: true`, the route calls `flagConversationNeedsAttention()` after persisting the assistant's real answer — this never changes `control`. A new Server Action (`app/(dashboard)/dashboard/conversations/actions.ts`, `setConversationControlAction`) and client component (`_components/control-toggle.tsx`) let any authenticated business member take over or hand back a conversation from the existing conversation detail page (`PRODUCT.md` §3's "business member... can take over conversations," same authorization tier as D7). `docs/architecture.md` gained a "Human handoff: control state and the AI-pause guard (Phase 15a)" subsection.

Key files: `supabase/migrations/20260814074411_add_conversation_control_and_attention.sql`, `lib/conversations.ts`, `lib/supabase/types.ts` (`ConversationControl` added, `Conversation` extended), `app/api/chat/route.ts`, `app/(dashboard)/dashboard/conversations/actions.ts`, `app/(dashboard)/dashboard/conversations/_components/control-toggle.tsx`, `app/(dashboard)/dashboard/conversations/[id]/page.tsx`, `docs/architecture.md`.

Migrations applied: **applied and grants verified live 2026-08-14** via `npx supabase db push --linked` and `npx supabase db query --linked`. Confirmed: `authenticated` has `UPDATE` on `conversations.control` but not on `conversations.needs_attention`; `anon` has neither.

Env vars added: none.

Decisions made this stage (full reasoning in the prompt): (1) polling, not WebSocket/SSE, chosen as the live mechanism for the whole of Phase 15 — no new server process, consistent with D4's Postgres-counter-table-not-Redis precedent; not implemented this stage, recorded ahead of 15b's build (see D8 below); (2) **escalation sets `needs_attention` only, never `control`** — a deliberate, user-confirmed reinterpretation of `docs/phases.md`'s literal exit-criterion wording, since auto-switching `control` on every escalation would silence the AI before any human is actually watching (no alert UI exists until 15c) — see D9 below; (3) the canned human-control acknowledgment is not persisted as a message row, deliberately deferring the "what role represents a staff reply" decision to 15b; (4) the dashboard control toggle is a binary button, not a dropdown (unlike `status-select.tsx`'s 4-way select), since this is a true binary state; (5) authorization for taking over a conversation follows D7's "any business member" precedent, per `PRODUCT.md` §3's explicit text, not `org:admin`-only.

Checks run: `npm run lint` — pass, zero errors/warnings. `npx tsc --noEmit` — pass (after `npx next typegen`). `npm run build` — pass, route manifest unchanged (no new routes, this stage only adds a Server Action, a client component, and edits existing files).

**Manual verification confirmed by user 2026-08-14, all 7 steps in `prompts/phase-15a-handoff-state-and-ai-pause.md` passed:** (1) a fresh widget conversation behaved with normal, unchanged AI responses before any takeover (regression check); (2) taking over the conversation from the dashboard correctly flipped the visible state to "Human-controlled"; (3) **the pause guard's core correctness proof** — a widget message sent on the same `conversationId` after takeover returned the exact static `HUMAN_CONTROL_MESSAGE`, not an AI-generated response, and a direct DB query confirmed zero new `assistant`-role rows were written for that turn, proving `askSalesEmployee()` was never invoked; (4) the prospect's message during human control was still visible in the dashboard transcript; (5) "Hand back to AI" correctly resumed normal AI-answered behavior on the next widget message; (6) a real escalation trigger on an AI-controlled, knowledge-backed conversation correctly set `needs_attention = true` on that row while leaving `control` unchanged, confirming D9's design holds in practice, not just in code; (7) a cross-tenant take-over attempt against another business's `conversationId` affected zero rows. This satisfies this stage's scope of the Phase 15 exit criterion (`docs/phases.md`), under the D9 reinterpretation — proven by test, not by inspection.
