### Phase 15b — Staff reply persistence and live polling — completed and verified 2026-08-14

`prompts/phase-15b-staff-reply-and-live-polling.md` was approved after an earlier draft (proposing a further 15b-1/15b-2 split) was rejected by the user in favor of one combined prompt — the split's design content was folded directly into the single approved document, with the same explicit reasoning for the pause/resume conditions, de-duplication, and rate-limit sizing the split version would have given them, not shortcut for being combined.

What exists now: `messages.role` gained `human_agent` (constraint widened, confirmed live via `pg_constraint` before and after). `authenticated` gained a column-scoped `INSERT` grant (`business_id`/`conversation_id`/`role`/`content` only) plus RLS policy `messages_insert_human_agent_reply`, requiring `role = 'human_agent'`, a business match, and the target conversation's `control = 'human'` — the first-ever `authenticated`-role write into this table, defense-in-depth alongside `sendHumanReplyAction`'s own re-check of `control === "human"` before attempting the insert. `lib/messages.ts` gained `listMessagesForConversationAfter()`, shared by both poll paths. A new public route, `app/api/chat/poll/route.ts`, mirrors `/api/chat`'s tenant/origin resolution exactly (`resolveBusinessFromWidgetKey()`, unmodified) and returns new non-`user`-role messages plus the conversation's current `control`; `/api/chat`'s own success response gained `control`/`asOf` fields on every path. `public/widget-loader.js` — the only script making the real cross-origin `/api/chat`/`/api/chat/poll` requests, unchanged from Phase 12's design — now runs a self-rescheduling 6-second poll loop, started the first time a response shows `escalate: true` or `control: "human"` (a one-way latch for the rest of the browser session, not re-gated per request), paused while the panel is closed (`widget:panel_open`, a new postMessage type), the tab is hidden, or a send is in flight, with an immediate poll on resume/reopen. The widget iframe renders any delivered `human_agent` messages with a distinct "A team member replied" label. On the dashboard side, `app/(dashboard)/dashboard/conversations/[id]/page.tsx` now delegates its control toggle, a new `ReplyComposer` (rendered only while `control === "human"`), and the transcript to a new client component, `LiveConversationPanel`, which polls every 3 seconds via a new `pollConversationAction` and is the single source of truth for what's rendered once mounted — `ControlToggle`'s and `ReplyComposer`'s own Server Actions keep their existing `revalidatePath` calls (still correct for a fresh page load) but the live panel treats their success as a trigger for one immediate extra poll, not as the source of truth (avoids a race between RSC prop-refresh timing and the poll loop). Two new rate-limit scopes, `poll_ip` (300/5min) and `poll_conversation` (100/5min), bound the new recurring traffic separately from Phase 11's message-send scopes; the dashboard's own polling gets no new rate limit (Clerk-authenticated Server Action, not public traffic — this codebase has never rate-limited a Server Action). `lib/http/widget-cors.ts` extracts the CORS/origin/IP helpers `/api/chat/route.ts` used to define locally, now shared with the new poll route. `docs/architecture.md` gained a "Phase 15b" subsection.

Key files: `supabase/migrations/20260814084209_add_human_agent_message_role.sql`, `..._add_poll_rate_limit_scopes.sql`, `lib/supabase/types.ts`, `lib/messages.ts`, `lib/rate-limit.ts`, `lib/http/widget-cors.ts`, `app/api/chat/route.ts`, `app/api/chat/poll/route.ts`, `app/(dashboard)/dashboard/conversations/actions.ts`, `app/(dashboard)/dashboard/conversations/_components/{reply-composer,live-conversation-panel,control-toggle,message-bubble}.tsx`, `app/(dashboard)/dashboard/conversations/[id]/page.tsx`, `app/(widget)/widget/embed/_lib/{post-message,use-widget-chat}.ts`, `app/(widget)/widget/embed/_components/{widget-app,message-bubble}.tsx`, `public/widget-loader.js` (implemented, then fixed post-implementation — see the verification section below), `docs/architecture.md`.

Migrations applied: **applied and verified live 2026-08-14** via `npx supabase db push --linked` and `npx supabase db query --linked`. Confirmed: `authenticated` has column-scoped `INSERT` on `messages` (`role`/`business_id`/`conversation_id`/`content`); both new check constraints (`messages_role_check`, `rate_limit_counters_scope_check`) and the new RLS policy read back exactly as written; `anon` unchanged on both tables.

Env vars added: none.

Decisions made this stage (full reasoning in the prompt's "Decisions and assumptions"): (1) new role `human_agent`, not a reused `assistant`/`system`; (2) **polling is a one-way latch, started by `escalate: true` OR `control: "human"`, never re-gated per request and never stopped again** — closes a real gap where a proactive staff take-over (before the prospect sends anything else) would otherwise give the widget no signal to ever poll, since it only ever learns `control` from responses to its own outgoing messages; (3) widget polls at 6s (unbounded anonymous traffic), dashboard at 3s (bounded, authenticated, staff actively waiting) — reasoned separately; (4) both poll loops self-reschedule via `setTimeout`, never `setInterval`; (5) pause conditions spelled out per side (widget: panel-closed/tab-hidden/send-in-flight; dashboard: tab-hidden/unmount), both firing one immediate poll on resume; (6) de-duplication by message `id` on both sides, layered on the `after`/`asOf` cursor, not a substitute for it; (7) two new rate-limit scopes sized around the actual 6s cadence, no new limit for the dashboard's own polling; (8) the widget's poll endpoint excludes `role: 'user'`, the dashboard's includes every role; (9) the dashboard's live view is the single source of truth for `control`/`messages` once mounted — `revalidatePath` stays for a fresh load, but in-page feedback comes from an immediate re-poll after a successful action, not from RSC prop-refresh timing.

Checks run: `npm run lint` — pass, zero errors/warnings (one real issue caught and fixed: `live-conversation-panel.tsx`'s `poll` callback originally self-referenced its own binding inside its body for the recursive `setTimeout` call, tripping `react-hooks/immutability`; fixed via a `pollRef` that always holds the latest `poll`, referenced from the self-rescheduling call instead). `npx tsc --noEmit` — pass, after `npx next typegen`. `npm run build` — pass; `/api/chat/poll` present in the route manifest, every other route unchanged.

**Direct RLS proofs performed by the implementing session 2026-08-14** (simulated-authenticated-session SQL via `npx supabase db query --linked`, `set local role authenticated` + `set local request.jwt.claims`, every insert attempt wrapped in a transaction that was rolled back — confirmed via a follow-up `select` that no test row or control-state change persisted): (1) a forged `role = 'assistant'` insert as Acme's authenticated session — denied, Postgres `42501`; (2) a `human_agent` insert into one of Acme's own conversations while it was genuinely still `control = 'ai'` — denied, same error; (3) the identical insert after that same conversation's `control` was set to `'human'` (via the service role, bypassing RLS as intended for test setup) — succeeded, returned the correct row shape, then rolled back; (4) the identical insert attempted as Ghost Test Co.'s authenticated session against Acme's now-human-controlled conversation — denied. The test conversation's `control` was reverted to `'ai'` immediately after, confirmed via a direct read.

**User-confirmed manual verification, 2026-08-14:** Test 1 (end-to-end live takeover — escalated widget conversation → dashboard takeover → staff reply appeared in the widget with "A team member replied") and Test 2 (dashboard live view — a new widget message appeared in the open dashboard conversation without a manual refresh) both **PASS**, confirmed in a real browser.

**Remaining Tests 3–9, taken over by the implementing session per the user's explicit request** (the user asked for automated/direct verification rather than further manual browser testing for the timing-sensitive pieces), run 2026-08-14:

- **Test 3 — Widget pause/resume — PASS**, verified via a throwaway Node harness (`vm`-sandboxed, mocked `document`/`window`/`fetch`, a hand-rolled fake clock — no new npm dependency) that loaded the **actual** `public/widget-loader.js` source and exercised its real `schedulePoll`/`doPoll`/`pollNow`/visibilitychange/`widget:panel_open` logic. Sub-results: polling active while panel open (0 polls before the handoff signal, exactly 1 after the first 6000ms tick, 2 after the second); polling correctly frozen while the panel is closed; reopening the panel fires an immediate poll (measured delta: 0ms, not the 6000ms interval); backgrounding the tab freezes polling; returning fires an immediate poll (delta: 0ms). **A real bug was caught and fixed during this run**: closing the panel called `schedulePoll()`, whose only guard (`if (pollTimeoutId) return;`) prevents double-scheduling but never cancels an *already-armed* timer — so a poll scheduled moments before the panel closed would still fire on its original schedule. The harness's first run showed exactly this (poll count grew from 2 to 3 during a 30-second closed-panel window that should have stayed flat). Fixed in `public/widget-loader.js`'s `widget:panel_open` handler to explicitly `clearTimeout` on close, matching the tab-hidden branch's existing pattern; re-ran the harness against the fixed file and all sub-results passed. `npm run lint`/`npx tsc --noEmit`/`npm run build` re-run clean after the fix.
- **Test 4 — Dashboard pause/resume — verified by targeted code audit, not an automated harness.** `LiveConversationPanel` is a React client component; exercising its `useEffect`-driven polling would require a DOM-testing dependency (jsdom/React Testing Library) this project doesn't have, and `react-test-renderer` is deprecated under React 19 — installing one unilaterally for a verification pass would be a real, unrequested dependency addition, against `AGENTS.md` §9. Instead, read the component specifically checking for the same bug class Test 3 caught: confirmed `poll()` itself always clears any pending timer before running (not a shared "schedule-if-none-pending" helper reused as a stop signal, the actual root cause in the widget), the `visibilitychange`-hidden branch clears explicitly, and unmount cleanup clears explicitly — structurally sound, but this is inspection-based confidence, not harness-executed proof, and is recorded here as a real, distinct gap from Tests 3/5/6/7/8's stronger evidence.
- **Test 5 — No-handoff-signal case — PASS.** Same harness: a fresh conversation with no `escalate`/`control:"human"` signal ever seen, panel open and visible for 60000ms virtual time (10x the poll interval) → zero `/api/chat/poll` calls. Cross-checked server-side: sent a real non-escalating message to a fresh conversation via the live `/api/chat`, then queried `rate_limit_counters` for `scope = 'poll_conversation'` on that conversation's id — zero rows, confirming nothing ever polled it.
- **Test 6 — De-duplication — PASS.** Same harness: simulated the server returning the same staff-reply message id across three consecutive poll ticks (the race/overlap scenario described). The `widget:poll_result` postMessage to the iframe carried that id exactly once, across all three ticks — `knownMessageIds`' loader-layer de-dup (Decision 7) confirmed directly. The iframe's own React-state-layer de-dup (the second, defense-in-depth layer) was not independently harness-verified for the same DOM-testing-dependency reason as Test 4, but its logic is a simple id-filter, code-reviewed as correct.
- **Test 7 — Rate limiting — PASS.** A real 110-request burst against the live `/api/chat/poll` endpoint for one conversation: requests 1–100 returned `200`, request 101 was the first `429`, and every request after stayed `429` — an exact match to the configured 100/5min `poll_conversation` limit. The `rate_limit_counters` row for that `(scope, identifier, window_start)` read back `request_count: 110` in the still-active 5-minute fixed window, and the identifier's paired `poll_ip` counter (also 110) confirmed it was `poll_conversation`'s limit that tripped, not `poll_ip`'s (whose 300 limit was nowhere close). Normal single-tab polling at the nominal 6-second cadence (≈50 requests per 300-second window) stays well under the 100 threshold by construction — not separately burst-tested at real-time cadence (would cost ~8+ minutes of wall clock for no additional evidence beyond the arithmetic and the exact-threshold precision already demonstrated).
- **Test 8 — Cross-tenant — PASS (poll endpoint); dashboard-URL 404 not independently re-run.** A real request to `/api/chat/poll` using Business A's (Acme) widget key against Business B's (Ghost Test Co.) real `conversationId` returned the generic `400 "Invalid request."`, no data in the response; the identical shape of request against Acme's *own* other conversation correctly returned `200` with real data, confirming the endpoint isn't just failing universally. The dashboard-side check (authenticated as Business B, opening Business A's conversation URL directly, expecting `404`) requires a real Clerk browser session and was **not** re-run this pass — the underlying code path (`getConversationForBusiness` returning `null` → `notFound()`) is unchanged by Phase 15b and was already verified in Phase 13c.
- **Test 9 — Regression — PASS for the parts checkable without a browser; UI-level spot checks not re-run.** A normal, non-escalating widget message still produced a correctly grounded AI answer end-to-end. The 15a control-toggle's underlying RLS/grant path (unchanged by 15b) was re-confirmed directly: a simulated-authenticated-session `UPDATE conversations SET control = 'human'` succeeded and was rolled back. A fresh real escalation (`/api/chat`, "I want to speak to a real person...") correctly returned `escalate: true`, and the conversation row showed `control: 'ai'` (unchanged) and `needs_attention: true` — D9's design holding exactly as designed. Products/Leads tables were confirmed still queryable (data-layer smoke check) — the actual dashboard pages' visual rendering was not re-opened in a browser this pass, since none of those files were touched by Phase 15b (confirmed via `git status` at implementation time).

All temporary scripts (the Node harness, its result file) were deleted after use; `git status` after cleanup showed only the deliberate `public/widget-loader.js` bug fix, no stray artifacts.

**Follow-up real-browser pass, same day (2026-08-14), closing the remaining gaps this entry originally flagged:** at the user's explicit request, Test 4 (dashboard pause/resume), Test 6's iframe/React-layer de-dup, Test 8's dashboard-URL 404 check, and Test 9's remaining browser-UI regression were all re-verified in a real headless-Chromium session (Playwright, installed isolated in the session scratchpad only, never added to the project) rather than left as inspection-only or skipped. Real Clerk auth via a Backend API sign-in token (existing test user, no new user/org); the widget side used the project's own pre-existing `public/test-widget.html` fixture as a real host page, so the full loader → iframe → postMessage bridge ran for real. All four passed on the fully-debugged run; the first raw run had failures that were all root-caused to the test script itself (fixed-sleep races against real Gemini latency, an over-tight timing assertion, a wrong 404-body-text regex, noisy-but-benign CSP console notices) — **zero of them were application defects, and per the user's explicit instruction, no production code was changed as a result.** Screenshots visually confirmed the two most consequential outcomes: the widget showing exactly one "Browser-verify staff reply..." bubble with the "A team member replied" label after real dashboard-to-widget delivery, and the dashboard's real "Human-controlled" badge/composer/transcript. All temporary Playwright/script artifacts were deleted after use; `git status` showed no residual changes from this pass. This closes every gap the original Phase 15b verification entry above flagged — **nothing in Phase 15b remains inspection-only.**

Known gaps carried forward: none from Phase 15b itself. The `needs_attention` alert badge/sound UI — Phase 15c, unaffected by this stage. `docs/phases.md`'s Phase 15 entry still has its original literal exit-criterion wording — still flagged (per 15a's entry) for Phase 15's overall closure, once 15c also lands.

### Phase 15c — In-app attention alerts (badge + sound) — implemented and fully verified 2026-08-14

`prompts/phase-15c-attention-alerts.md`, approved by the user without changes. The third and final planned Phase 15 stage — closes the gap 15a's own entry flagged (`needs_attention` had no path back to `false`).

What exists now: `lib/conversations.ts`'s `setConversationControl()` now clears `needs_attention` in the same update when transitioning to `"human"` (hand-back to `"ai"` untouched); new `dismissConversationAttention()` clears it without touching `control`; new `countConversationsNeedingAttention()` backs the nav badge. New migration grants `authenticated` a column-scoped `UPDATE` on `needs_attention` (same shape as 15a's `control` grant, same unchanged `conversations_update_own_business` RLS policy) — `authenticated` still has no way to ever set it back to `true`. New `app/(dashboard)/dashboard/actions.ts` (`pollAttentionCountAction`). `app/(dashboard)/dashboard/conversations/actions.ts` gained `dismissAttentionAction`; `pollConversationAction`'s result gained `needsAttention`. New `AttentionProvider` client component (mounted once in `dashboard/layout.tsx`, wrapping the existing `Sidebar`/`MobileNav`/`{children}`) owns one shared 3-second poll (not duplicated per nav surface), exposing the count via `useAttentionCount()`; both `Sidebar` and `MobileNav` render a numeric badge (capped "9+") on the "Conversations" item only when the count is nonzero. A synthesized Web Audio chime (no new dependency, no audio asset) plays only on a genuine increase over what the tab already knew, never on initial mount; `AudioContext` is created lazily on the dashboard's first click/keydown per the standard autoplay-policy workaround. New `DismissAttentionButton` client component, used on the conversation detail page (only rendered while `needsAttention === true`) via `LiveConversationPanel`, which now also tracks `needsAttention` state fed by every poll tick. The conversations list page gained a static, server-rendered per-row "Needs attention" pill (amber, same visual language as `ControlToggle`'s existing "Human-controlled" badge). `docs/architecture.md` gained a "Phase 15c" subsection.

Key files: `supabase/migrations/20260814150000_grant_needs_attention_update.sql`, `lib/conversations.ts`, `app/(dashboard)/dashboard/actions.ts` (new), `app/(dashboard)/dashboard/conversations/actions.ts`, `app/(dashboard)/dashboard/_components/{attention-provider,dismiss-attention-button}.tsx` (new), `app/(dashboard)/dashboard/_components/{sidebar,mobile-nav,nav-items}.tsx`, `app/(dashboard)/dashboard/layout.tsx`, `app/(dashboard)/dashboard/conversations/page.tsx`, `app/(dashboard)/dashboard/conversations/[id]/page.tsx`, `app/(dashboard)/dashboard/conversations/_components/live-conversation-panel.tsx`, `docs/architecture.md`.

Migrations applied: **applied and verified live 2026-08-14** via `npx supabase db push --linked`; `has_column_privilege('authenticated', 'public.conversations', 'needs_attention', 'UPDATE')` confirmed `true`.

Env vars added: none. No new npm package — the chime uses the native Web Audio API.

Decisions made this stage (full reasoning in the prompt): (1) taking over also clears `needs_attention` (avoids a second click for the common path), a separate Dismiss action covers the "AI is handling it fine" case — a modest, explicitly-flagged scope addition beyond "just show a badge," approved as part of the prompt; (2) one shared poller in a Context provider, not one per nav surface; (3) 3-second poll interval, matching the dashboard's existing cadence; (4) sound fires only on a genuine increase relative to this tab's own prior known count, never on mount or on a decrease; (5) synthesized tone via Web Audio, not an audio file; (6) `AudioContext` created lazily on first click/keydown, an accepted, documented limitation for an alert firing before any interaction on a freshly loaded idle tab; (7) nav badge shows the actual count (capped "9+"), not just a dot; (8) the conversations list's per-row badge is static, not live-polled — only the nav badge/sound and the conversation detail page are live; (9) no Overview stat card added; (10) not split into further stages.

Checks run: `npm run lint` — pass, zero errors/warnings. `npx tsc --noEmit` — pass. `npm run build` — pass, all routes compile and are listed in the route manifest, no route changes.

**Real-browser verification — PASS, all 8 tests, run 2026-08-14** (same day, follow-up session, at the user's explicit request for real evidence). Full detail and per-test evidence in §1. Summary: real Clerk auth (Backend API sign-in tokens, two real test users, one forced to Ghost's org via `window.Clerk.setActive()`), real Gemini escalations (no mocked model output), real Supabase reads/writes, headless Chromium via Playwright (installed isolated in the session scratchpad, never added to the project). Badge timing/chime-on-genuine-increase-only/cross-tab sync/takeover+Dismiss (both DB-verified)/regression+dual-pill rendering/live cross-tenant isolation (including mid-poll, not just query-time)/polling-stops-on-sign-out-and-on-leaving-`/dashboard/*` all confirmed. Zero application defects found; two test-script timing bugs were found and fixed in the test script only, per the user's explicit instruction not to touch production code to make tests pass. All temporary artifacts deleted, clean `needs_attention` baseline restored via the app's own Dismiss action, `git status` clean of stray files, `npm run lint`/`npx tsc --noEmit`/`npm run build` re-run clean afterward.

Known gaps carried forward: `docs/phases.md`'s Phase 15 exit-criterion wording ("an escalation trigger reliably moves a live conversation to human control...") still reflects the pre-D9 model, not the confirmed D9 reinterpretation (escalation flags attention; a human's deliberate take-over moves control) — flagged at 15a's closure, flagged again at 15b's closure, and left unedited here too since editing `docs/phases.md` wasn't part of this stage's approved scope (the prompt listed it explicitly under "Out of scope"). Worth fixing in a small, separately-approved follow-up now that all three Phase 15 stages are implemented.

## 3. Next up

**Phase 24 — Commercial readiness is complete** (see the entry near the top of this file). **Next phase is Phase 25 — UI/UX production polish** (`docs/phases.md`): widget conversation restored on refresh, widget branding, language/localization, funnel analytics, a full accessibility pass, a responsive audit of the dashboard, slow-network/timeout UX, consistent empty/error/loading patterns, onboarding polish (a "test your AI before publishing" workspace), and handoff/lead notifications that reach the team outside the browser tab — this last item is what Phase 24's SLA routing is currently missing (it reassigns and logs, but doesn't notify anyone). Billing (Phase 17) and WhatsApp (Phase 16) remain explicitly out of scope, by user decision, not forgotten.

**Before relying on Phase 24's RBAC tiers, the user needs to create two custom roles in the Clerk Dashboard** (Organizations → Roles & Permissions) with the exact key strings `org:sales_agent` and `org:analyst_viewer` — the application-side gating is already live and safe without them (an unrecognized role is denied every gated action, never granted), but the two new tiers won't actually differentiate anyone until those roles exist and are assigned.

Also flagged (not blocking): Phase 24's nine features were checked via lint/typecheck/build/pgTAP against production, all passing, but none had a live real-browser click-through this session (unlike Phase 23's live-verified ingestion/caching work) — worth a manual pass through Publish/Unpublish, widget key rotation, a real webhook delivery, and the business-hours form before depending on them for real customer traffic. Phase 24's seven migrations were only applied to production, not staging (same standing gap as Phase 22g/23 — no session yet has had the second Supabase account's credentials).

Phase 15 is complete — all three stages (15a, 15b, 15c) implemented and fully verified, 15c's own real-browser pass (8/8 tests) confirmed 2026-08-14 (see §1/§2). `docs/phases.md`'s Phase 15 exit-criterion wording (flagged across 15a/15b/15c's entries) is still worth a small, separately-approved fix reflecting the confirmed D9 reinterpretation.

**Phase 16 (WhatsApp) and Phase 17 (Razorpay) are explicitly deferred by user decision, 2026-08-14 — see §1 and the Resolved decisions table in §4.**

**Phase 18 — Analytics / monitoring is complete** (implemented and fully verified 2026-08-14, all 8 manual test steps run with real evidence — see §1/§2). **Next phase is Phase 19 — Production hardening** (see `docs/phases.md`): security review, tenant-isolation review, auth review, server/client secret review, validation review, error handling, rate limiting and abuse, database indexes, vector query performance, build checks, deployment configuration, environment validation, logging, responsive UI checks, accessibility checks, production smoke tests.

**Standing process change, effective Phase 18 onward:** implement each phase as a single prompt end-to-end, not split into staged sub-prompts (a/b/c) the way Phases 13, 14, and 15 were. If a phase genuinely looks too large or risky to safely implement and verify in one prompt, say so explicitly and ask before splitting it — do not decide unilaterally to split, the way the multi-stage phases did. See the Resolved decisions table, §4, for the full record.

## 4. Open decisions

These must be resolved before the phase noted. **Do not implement past a decision's deadline phase while it is still open** — ask the user to decide first.

None currently open.

### Resolved decisions

| # | Decision | Resolved | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|
| D4 | Public chat widget identity mechanism | 2026-08-13 | **One widget key per business, one allowed origin per business, both as columns on `businesses` — no separate `widget_keys` table, no key rotation/multiple-keys support for v1 (deferred until actually needed).** `widget_key` is generated automatically at business creation (DB column default); `widget_allowed_origin` is nullable and set on-demand later from the dashboard (a minimal Phase 11 settings page — key rotation/regeneration is explicitly not built this phase). The origin is checked against the request's `Origin`/`Referer` header **server-side, before the key is resolved to a `business_id`** — resolution lives in a new server-only module, `lib/widget-auth.ts`'s `resolveBusinessFromWidgetKey(key, origin)`, returning a validated `business_id` or throwing; no code path accepts a client-supplied `business_id`. Rate limiting is a lightweight Postgres counter table (`rate_limit_counters`), not Redis or an external service, consistent with this project's "no new infra unless justified" discipline — enforced per key, per IP, and per conversation as `docs/security.md` §4 requires, via an atomic increment-and-check Postgres function (avoids a read-then-write race). Because the widget is unauthenticated (no Clerk session), this phase also introduces a service-role Supabase client (`lib/supabase/service.ts`, `SUPABASE_SECRET_KEY`) for the widget's own request path only — RLS is bypassed there by design, so application-layer `business_id` filtering is the only tenant boundary on that path, same principle `docs/security.md` §3 already documents for narrow service-role use. |
| D1 | Tenancy model: Clerk Organizations vs. one business per user | 2026-08-11 | **Clerk Organizations.** Multi-member businesses are already specified in `PRODUCT.md` §3, and retrofitting orgs after Phase 3's schema exists would be expensive. |
| D2 | Tenant isolation enforcement: Postgres RLS vs. application-layer only | 2026-08-11 | **Defense in depth.** RLS enabled on every business-owned table, plus mandatory `business_id` filtering in the application data-access layer, with Clerk session tokens wired into Supabase so RLS policies can see the caller. |
| D3 | Embedding model and vector dimension | 2026-08-12 | **`gemini-embedding-001` via `@langchain/google-genai`'s `GoogleGenerativeAIEmbeddings`, truncated to 1536 dimensions.** Chosen by the user over the model's 3072-dimension default: 1536 matches full 3072-dimension MTEB retrieval quality (Google's MRL truncation preserves quality at this size), fits under pgvector's 2000-dimension HNSW index limit with the standard `vector` type (avoiding `halfvec` casting complexity a 3072-dim vector would require), and halves storage/memory versus 3072 at scale. Chat generation model: `gemini-3.1-flash-lite` (confirmed live against `ai.google.dev` — see Phase 7 prompt). **Live-doc verification during Phase 7 prompt-writing surfaced two things the recommended default in this row's old text didn't anticipate:** (1) `gemini-embedding-001`'s non-3072-dimension outputs are **not** auto-normalized by Google — the caller must manually L2-normalize the truncated vector (this is unlike the newer `gemini-embedding-2-preview`, which does auto-normalize but is preview-status and multimodal-only, not needed for this text-only v1 knowledge base); (2) whether `@langchain/google-genai`'s TypeScript `GoogleGenerativeAIEmbeddings` class exposes `outputDimensionality` as a constructor option or only per-call (confirmed pattern in the Python `langchain_google_genai` package) could not be conclusively confirmed from public docs/search alone — the Phase 7 prompt makes inspecting the actual installed package's TypeScript types the mandatory first implementation step, with a documented fallback if the constructor option doesn't exist. |
| D5 | Approved knowledge source types for v1 | 2026-08-11 | **Pasted/typed text plus the structured records from Phase 5 (products, services, FAQs) only.** Already specified in `PRODUCT.md` §6 ("For v1, supported knowledge input is pasted or typed text plus the structured records above"); formally closed here at the start of Phase 6. File upload, URL ingestion, and web crawling remain explicitly out of scope until separately scheduled (`PRODUCT.md` §10). |
| D7 | Products/services/FAQs CRUD authorization: any org member vs. `org:admin`-only | 2026-08-11 | **Any authenticated org member.** `PRODUCT.md` §3 defers the full role model to a later phase and doesn't distinguish owner/member for structured-knowledge CRUD; RLS policies gate on org match only, same shape as `businesses`' existing policies. Confirmed explicitly by the user as a real authorization decision, not just an implementation default — revisit if a stricter model is ever wanted. |
| D8 | Human handoff live/near-real-time mechanism | 2026-08-14 | **Polling, not WebSocket/SSE — implemented Phase 15b.** Decided at Phase 15a's start, applies across all of Phase 15. This app has no long-running server process (Next.js route handlers/Server Actions on a serverless-oriented deployment target) — a WebSocket server would be new infrastructure this project has never needed, and a serverless-hosted SSE connection is fragile without an added pub/sub/keep-alive layer. Polling reuses the existing `conversations`/`messages` tables and existing Clerk-session/service-role clients, the smallest-blast-radius option that still delivers the "real time or near-real-time" behavior the user scoped this to. Same shape as D4's Postgres-counter-table-not-Redis reasoning. Implemented Phase 15b: `app/api/chat/poll/route.ts` (widget, 6s) + `pollConversationAction` (dashboard, 3s), with two new `rate_limit_counters` scopes (`poll_ip`/`poll_conversation`) sized separately from Phase 11's message-send limits. |
| D10 | Widget poll trigger: gate strictly on `control === "human"`, or something broader? | 2026-08-14 | **Broader — a one-way latch, started by `escalate: true` OR `control === "human"`, never re-gated per request and never turned off again for the rest of the browser session.** Surfaced during Phase 15b implementation: gating strictly on `control` learned only from the prospect's own outgoing message responses would leave the widget with no way to ever learn about a proactive staff take-over that happens before the prospect sends anything further — a real gap against the phase's own "human staff member must be able to join an in-progress widget conversation" goal. Gating on "has ever escalated" closes it in the realistic case (per D9, a staff member only discovers a conversation to take over via the Phase 15c attention alert, which only ever fires on `escalate: true`); `control === "human"` remains a second trigger for a business member proactively taking over a never-escalated conversation. Not stopping again on hand-back keeps the state machine a simple latch rather than a toggle — a passive poll that usually returns nothing is cheap and bounded (the `poll_ip`/`poll_conversation` rate limits size for continuous polling, not just windows of human control). |
| D9 | Escalation vs. control: does an AI escalation trigger move a conversation to human control automatically? | 2026-08-14 | **No — escalation only sets `needs_attention = true`; `control` changes only via an explicit dashboard take-over action.** A confirmed, deliberate reinterpretation of `docs/phases.md`'s literal Phase 15 exit-criterion wording ("an escalation trigger reliably moves a live conversation to human control"). Auto-switching `control` on every escalation would silence the AI and show the prospect a canned "someone will reply shortly" message before any human is necessarily watching, since the attention-alert UI doesn't exist until Phase 15c — worse than letting the AI keep helping until a human deliberately takes over. The literal phrase is satisfied at the level of the full Phase 15 flow (trigger → attention flag → a human's deliberate take-over → control genuinely moves to human), not by the trigger alone. `docs/phases.md`'s Phase 15 entry is flagged for a small clarifying edit once all three stages close (not done yet — see §2's Phase 15a entry). |
| D11 | Skip Phase 16/17, single-prompt-per-phase from Phase 18 on | 2026-08-14 | **Reorder only — Phase 16 (WhatsApp) and Phase 17 (Razorpay) are deferred, not cancelled, by the user's explicit call; next active phase is Phase 18 — Analytics / monitoring.** `PRODUCT.md` §10 already framed both as "out of scope until explicitly scheduled," and `docs/phases.md`'s phase list/ordering already correctly describes them as deliberately late-stage — neither document needed editing. No security rule in `AGENTS.md` §3 changes; those apply unconditionally to Phase 18 onward. **Also decided, effective Phase 18 onward:** each phase is implemented as one prompt end-to-end, not split into staged a/b/c sub-prompts the way Phases 13, 14, and 15 were. If a future phase genuinely looks too large/risky for one prompt, the implementing session must say so and ask before splitting — not decide unilaterally, as the multi-stage phases did. |
| D6 | Lead field specification | 2026-08-12 | **Full spec written into `PRODUCT.md` §8** — 12 fields (`business_id`, `conversation_id`, `contact_name`, `contact_email`, `contact_phone`, `interest_type`, `interest_id`, `notes`, `qualification`, `status`, `source`, `created_at`/`updated_at`). Key rules: a lead is only created once at least one of `contact_email`/`contact_phone` is present (no junk/empty leads); `qualification` (hot/warm/cold + AI reason) is AI-generated, untrusted, UI/display-only — same trust category as Phase 9's `escalate`/`usedContext`, never the sole gate for human review; `interest_type`/`interest_id` reuse `knowledge_documents`' exact polymorphic pattern (Phase 6) — no FK, app-enforced integrity, not a new mechanism. |

## 5. Environment variables in use

Only variables actually required by implemented phases. Keep in sync with `.env.example`.

- `NEXT_PUBLIC_CLERK_PUBLISHABLE_KEY` — client-safe. Required since Phase 2.
- `CLERK_SECRET_KEY` — **secret**, server-only. Required since Phase 2.

- `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL` — client-safe. Required since Phase 3.
- `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY` — client-safe. Required since Phase 3.

`SUPABASE_SECRET_KEY` — **secret**, server-only. Required since Phase 11 (`lib/supabase/service.ts`, the app's first service-role client — used only by the public `/api/chat` widget endpoint, which has no Clerk session for RLS to key off of). Phases 6 and 7 had both confirmed the per-request Clerk-authenticated client was sufficient up to that point.

Pinned by resolved decision D3 (2026-08-12). `GEMINI_API_KEY` and `GEMINI_EMBEDDING_MODEL=gemini-embedding-001`/`GEMINI_EMBEDDING_DIMENSION=1536` required since Phase 7 (embedding generation). (Corrected 2026-08-12 from a `GOOGLE_API_KEY` mislabel that had been in code and docs since Phase 7 — see §8.) `GEMINI_CHAT_MODEL=gemini-3.1-flash-lite` — **required since Phase 8** (`lib/rag.ts`'s chat generation), not Phase 9 as previously noted here; that note was revised at the user's explicit request when approving the Phase 8 prompt, since Phase 8's own exit criterion (grounded generation that doesn't fabricate) can't be proven without actually wiring the chat model. Phase 9 extends the system prompt with the full sales persona on top of this same model/env var — it does not introduce a new one. Chat model string confirmed live against `ai.google.dev` during Phase 7 prompt-writing.

`SENTRY_DSN`/`NEXT_PUBLIC_SENTRY_DSN`/`SENTRY_AUTH_TOKEN` (Phase 21), `AI_MONTHLY_TOKEN_LIMIT` (Phase 22h) — both optional, see `docs/security.md` §5 for why each degrades safely rather than failing startup.

`CRON_SECRET` — **secret**, server-only, optional. Added Phase 23 (`app/api/cron/process-ingestion-queue/route.ts`). Required in production for the daily cron backstop to authenticate (Vercel signs cron requests with `Authorization: Bearer $CRON_SECRET` when the var is set); unset locally, where the route just fails closed (404) and ingestion still works via the immediate `after()` trigger. **Not yet set on Vercel production** — flagged in the Phase 23 entry above, should be set before relying on the cron backstop for real.

## 6. Database state

Migrations applied and verified (2026-08-11 businesses migrations; 2026-08-12 products/services/faqs, knowledge, and embeddings migrations):
1. `supabase/migrations/20260811124354_create_businesses_table.sql` — creates `public.businesses`, RLS, SELECT policy.
2. `supabase/migrations/20260811145006_tighten_businesses_grants.sql` — revokes excess `anon`/`authenticated` grants on `businesses` down to `authenticated: SELECT` only.
3. `supabase/migrations/20260811150450_default_privileges_least_privilege.sql` — `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES` so future tables in `public` no longer inherit broad grants automatically.
4. `supabase/migrations/20260811151559_add_businesses_insert_policy.sql` — grants `insert` on `businesses` to `authenticated`; adds policy `businesses_insert_own_org` (org-match only, same shape as the `SELECT` policy).
5. `supabase/migrations/20260811170015_create_products_table.sql` — creates `public.products`, RLS, four policies (select/insert/update/delete), `authenticated` grant.
6. `supabase/migrations/20260811170017_create_services_table.sql` — creates `public.services`, same shape as `products`.
7. `supabase/migrations/20260811170020_create_faqs_table.sql` — creates `public.faqs`, same shape as `products`/`services`.
8. `supabase/migrations/20260812134819_create_knowledge_documents_table.sql` — creates `public.knowledge_documents`, RLS, four policies, `authenticated` grant.
9. `supabase/migrations/20260812134821_create_knowledge_chunks_table.sql` — creates `public.knowledge_chunks`, RLS, select/insert/delete policies (no update), `authenticated` grant.
10. `supabase/migrations/20260812143330_fix_knowledge_documents_generated_source_index.sql` — replaces `knowledge_documents_generated_source_idx` with a non-partial unique index (see Phase 6 §2 entry for why).
11. `supabase/migrations/20260812161845_enable_pgvector_extension.sql` — `create extension vector with schema extensions`.
12. `supabase/migrations/20260812161847_add_embedding_to_knowledge_chunks.sql` — adds nullable `embedding vector(1536)` to `knowledge_chunks`.
13. `supabase/migrations/20260812161850_create_match_knowledge_chunks_function.sql` — creates `public.match_knowledge_chunks(p_business_id, p_query_embedding, p_match_count)`, `security invoker`, `execute` granted to `authenticated`.
14. `supabase/migrations/20260812163653_revoke_public_execute_on_match_knowledge_chunks.sql` — revokes the default `PUBLIC`/`anon` execute grant on `match_knowledge_chunks` (see Phase 7 §2 entry).

15. `supabase/migrations/20260813120000_create_conversations_table.sql` — creates `public.conversations` (minimal stub), RLS, select/insert policies, `authenticated` grant.
16. `supabase/migrations/20260813120005_create_leads_table.sql` — creates `public.leads` (full D6/`PRODUCT.md` §8 spec), RLS, four policies, `authenticated` grant, `leads_contact_required` check constraint.
17. `supabase/migrations/20260813130000_add_widget_columns_to_businesses.sql` — adds `widget_key`/`widget_allowed_origin` to `businesses`, an `UPDATE` RLS policy, and a column-level `UPDATE` grant scoped to `widget_allowed_origin` only.
18. `supabase/migrations/20260813130005_create_messages_table.sql` — creates `public.messages`, RLS, one select policy, `authenticated` = `SELECT` only (no write grant — only the service role writes).
19. `supabase/migrations/20260813130010_create_rate_limit_counters_table.sql` — creates `public.rate_limit_counters`, RLS enabled + forced, zero policies, no grants to `anon`/`authenticated`.
20. `supabase/migrations/20260813130015_create_increment_rate_limit_counter_function.sql` — creates `public.increment_rate_limit_counter(p_scope, p_identifier, p_window_seconds)`, `security invoker`, atomic upsert-increment; `execute` revoked from `public`/`anon`/`authenticated`, granted only to `service_role`.
21. `supabase/migrations/20260813140000_add_business_profile_fields.sql` — adds four nullable columns to `public.businesses` (`description`, `contact_email`, `contact_phone`, `website`, Phase 13b); extends the existing column-level `UPDATE` grant to cover `name`/`description`/`contact_email`/`contact_phone`/`website` (previously `widget_allowed_origin` only).
22. `supabase/migrations/20260814070328_add_requested_callback_to_leads.sql` — adds `requested_callback boolean not null default false` to `public.leads` (Phase 14c); adds `unique (conversation_id)` constraint (`leads_conversation_id_unique`), after a live pre-flight check confirmed zero existing duplicates. No new grant statement — the existing table-level `grant ... on public.leads to authenticated` (no column list) already covers the new column, confirmed live post-push.
23. `supabase/migrations/20260814074411_add_conversation_control_and_attention.sql` — adds `control text not null default 'ai' check (control in ('ai','human'))` and `needs_attention boolean not null default false` to `public.conversations` (Phase 15a); adds a partial index on `(business_id, needs_attention) where needs_attention = true`; grants `update (control)` only to `authenticated` (no grant on `needs_attention` — service-role-write-only, by design); adds a `for update` RLS policy, business-match only, same shape as the table's existing `select`/`insert` policies.
24. `supabase/migrations/20260814084209_add_human_agent_message_role.sql` — widens `messages_role_check` to include `'human_agent'` (Phase 15b); grants column-scoped `INSERT` (`business_id`/`conversation_id`/`role`/`content`) to `authenticated`; adds RLS policy `messages_insert_human_agent_reply` requiring `role = 'human_agent'`, business match, and the target conversation's `control = 'human'` — the first `authenticated`-role write into `messages` ever.
25. `supabase/migrations/20260814084211_add_poll_rate_limit_scopes.sql` — widens `rate_limit_counters_scope_check` to include `'poll_ip'`/`'poll_conversation'` (Phase 15b). No grant/RLS change — `rate_limit_counters` already has zero `authenticated`/`anon` grants.
26. `supabase/migrations/20260814150000_grant_needs_attention_update.sql` — grants column-scoped `update (needs_attention)` on `public.conversations` to `authenticated` (Phase 15c). No new RLS policy — reuses the existing `conversations_update_own_business` policy from 23. `authenticated` still cannot set `needs_attention` back to `true` (no grant path exists for that direction — only the service role, via `flagConversationNeedsAttention()`, can raise it).

**This numbered list was not kept current through Phases 18–22 — a pre-existing gap in §6's own maintenance, not fixed retroactively here (out of Phase 23's approved scope). Phase 23's own three migrations are recorded below by name, applied and verified live against production:**
- `supabase/migrations/20260820220000_add_ingestion_status_to_knowledge_documents.sql` — adds `ingestion_status`/`ingestion_attempts`/`ingestion_last_error`/`ingestion_next_attempt_at`/`ingestion_updated_at` to `public.knowledge_documents` (backfilled `'complete'` for every pre-existing row); creates `public.claim_knowledge_ingestion_jobs(p_limit)` (`security definer`, `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`), `execute` revoked from `public`/`anon`/`authenticated`, confirmed `true` only for `service_role` via `has_function_privilege`. No new grant on `knowledge_documents` itself — it already grants `authenticated` full row-level `UPDATE`.
- `supabase/migrations/20260820220500_create_ai_response_cache_table.sql` — creates `public.ai_response_cache`, RLS enabled + forced, zero policies, no `anon`/`authenticated` grant (service-role only, same pattern as `ai_response_metrics`).
- `supabase/migrations/20260820221000_schedule_operational_table_cleanup.sql` — creates `public.delete_expired_rate_limit_counters()` and `public.delete_expired_ai_response_cache()` (both `security definer`, `execute` revoked from `public`/`anon`/`authenticated`), schedules both daily via `pg_cron` (03:30/03:35 UTC).

Tenant isolation confirmed live via `npm test` (pgTAP `016_ai_response_cache_tenant_isolation.sql`) and function-privilege checks (above); the two cleanup functions confirmed functionally via `017_operational_cleanup_functions.sql`. **Not yet pushed to staging** — see the Phase 23 entry's "Known limitations."

Tables:
- `public.businesses` — columns `id` (uuid pk), `clerk_org_id` (text, unique), `name`, `description` (text, nullable — Phase 13b), `contact_email` (text, nullable — Phase 13b), `contact_phone` (text, nullable — Phase 13b), `website` (text, nullable — Phase 13b), `widget_key` (uuid, unique, default `gen_random_uuid()` — Phase 11), `widget_allowed_origin` (text, nullable — Phase 11), `created_at`, `updated_at` (auto-maintained via trigger). RLS enabled + forced. Three active policies: `SELECT`/`INSERT` for `authenticated` (org-match, unchanged since Phase 3/4) and `UPDATE` for `authenticated` (Phase 11, same org-match condition), paired with a column-level grant restricting the actual `UPDATE` privilege to `name`/`description`/`contact_email`/`contact_phone`/`website`/`widget_allowed_origin` (extended Phase 13b — previously `widget_allowed_origin` only). Grants: `authenticated` = `SELECT, INSERT` (whole-row) + `UPDATE` (column-scoped, six columns); `anon` = none; `service_role` = default (bypasses RLS, unrestricted, as intended). The four Phase 13b fields are dashboard-display-only — not read by `lib/rag.ts`'s AI pipeline (see `docs/architecture.md`'s "Business profile fields and the AI boundary" note).
- `public.products` — columns `id` (uuid pk), `business_id` (uuid, fk → `businesses.id` on delete cascade), `name`, `description`, `price` (numeric(12,2), nullable), `created_at`, `updated_at`. Indexed on `business_id`. RLS enabled + forced, four policies (select/insert/update/delete) all scoped via `business_id in (select id from businesses where clerk_org_id = (select auth.jwt()) -> 'o' ->> 'id')`. Grants: `authenticated` = `SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE`; `anon` = none.
- `public.services` — same shape as `products` (`name`, `description`, `price`).
- `public.faqs` — columns `id` (uuid pk), `business_id` (uuid, fk → `businesses.id` on delete cascade), `question` (not null), `answer` (not null), `created_at`, `updated_at`. Same RLS/grant shape as `products`.
- `public.knowledge_documents` — columns `id` (uuid pk), `business_id` (uuid, fk → `businesses.id` on delete cascade), `source_type` (`manual`/`product`/`service`/`faq`), `source_id` (nullable uuid, no FK — app-enforced), `title`, `content`, `created_at`, `updated_at`. Unique index on `(business_id, source_type, source_id)`. Same RLS/grant shape as `products` (four policies, `authenticated` = `SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE`).
- `public.knowledge_chunks` — columns `id` (uuid pk), `business_id` (uuid, fk → `businesses.id` on delete cascade), `document_id` (uuid, fk → `knowledge_documents.id` on delete cascade), `chunk_index`, `content`, `char_count`, `embedding` (nullable `vector(1536)`), `created_at`. RLS enabled + forced, three policies (select/insert/delete — no update; chunks are always deleted and reinserted). Grants: `authenticated` = `SELECT, INSERT, DELETE`; `anon` = none.
- `public.match_knowledge_chunks(p_business_id uuid, p_query_embedding vector(1536), p_match_count int default 5)` — `security invoker` function, tenant-scoped by an explicit `business_id` filter in its query body (not just RLS). Grants: `authenticated` = `EXECUTE` only; `PUBLIC`/`anon` explicitly revoked (see Phase 7 §2 entry for why this needed a follow-up migration).

- `public.conversations` — columns `id` (uuid pk), `business_id` (uuid, fk → `businesses.id` on delete cascade), `source` (nullable text), `control` (text, not null, default `'ai'`, check `ai`/`human` — **Phase 15a**), `needs_attention` (boolean, not null, default `false` — **Phase 15a**), `created_at`. Partial index on `(business_id, needs_attention) where needs_attention = true`. RLS enabled + forced, three policies (select/insert/update — the update policy added Phase 15a). Grants: `authenticated` = `SELECT, INSERT` (whole-row) + `UPDATE` (column-scoped to `control` only, Phase 15a); `anon` = none. `needs_attention` has no `authenticated` grant at all — only the service role writes it, from `app/api/chat/route.ts`.
- `public.leads` — columns `id` (uuid pk), `business_id` (uuid, fk → `businesses.id` on delete cascade), `conversation_id` (uuid, fk → `conversations.id` on delete cascade, **unique — Phase 14c**), `contact_name`/`contact_email`/`contact_phone` (nullable), `interest_type` (nullable, check `product`/`service`/`general`), `interest_id` (nullable uuid, no FK — app-enforced), `notes` (nullable), `qualification` (not null, check `hot`/`warm`/`cold`), `qualification_reason` (not null), `status` (not null, default `new`, check `new`/`contacted`/`converted`/`lost`), `source` (nullable), `requested_callback` (not null, default `false` — **Phase 14c**, set `true` by the `request_callback` AI tool), `created_at`, `updated_at`. Check constraints: `leads_contact_required` (at least one of `contact_email`/`contact_phone`), `leads_conversation_id_unique` (Phase 14c — at most one lead per conversation, backstopping the app-layer fill-blank-not-duplicate logic). Indexed on `business_id`, `conversation_id`. RLS enabled + forced, four policies (select/insert/update/delete). Grants: `authenticated` = `SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE` (table-level, automatically covers `requested_callback` — no new grant statement needed, confirmed live); `anon` = none.

- `public.messages` — columns `id` (uuid pk), `business_id` (uuid, fk → `businesses.id` on delete cascade), `conversation_id` (uuid, fk → `conversations.id` on delete cascade), `role` (text, check `user`/`assistant`/`human_agent` — **`human_agent` added Phase 15b**), `content` (text, not null), `created_at`. Indexed on `(business_id, conversation_id, created_at)`. RLS enabled + forced, two policies: `SELECT` (org-match via `business_id`, unchanged) and `INSERT` (**Phase 15b**, `messages_insert_human_agent_reply` — `role = 'human_agent'` AND business match AND the target conversation's `control = 'human'`). Grants: `authenticated` = `SELECT` (whole-row) + `INSERT` (column-scoped to `business_id`/`conversation_id`/`role`/`content`, Phase 15b); `anon` = none. The service role (`lib/supabase/service.ts`) still does all `user`/`assistant` writes from `app/api/chat/route.ts`; `authenticated`'s new grant is scoped by both the column list and RLS to `human_agent` rows on human-controlled conversations only.
- `public.rate_limit_counters` — columns `id` (uuid pk), `scope` (text, check `ip`/`key`/`conversation`/`poll_ip`/`poll_conversation` — **the two `poll_*` values added Phase 15b**), `identifier` (text), `window_start` (timestamptz), `request_count` (int, default 1), unique on `(scope, identifier, window_start)`. Not business-owned data — no `business_id` column. RLS enabled + forced, zero policies. Grants: none to `anon`/`authenticated`; only the service role touches it, via `public.increment_rate_limit_counter`.
- `public.increment_rate_limit_counter(p_scope text, p_identifier text, p_window_seconds int)` — `security invoker` function, atomic upsert-increment for `rate_limit_counters`' current fixed window. Grants: `service_role` = `EXECUTE` only; `PUBLIC`/`anon`/`authenticated` explicitly revoked in the same migration that creates it (not a follow-up fix, unlike `match_knowledge_chunks`'s Phase 7 gap).

## 8. Known limitations / debt

- **`GEMINI_API_KEY` naming correction (2026-08-12) — resolved, recorded for the record.** The Gemini API key's env var has always correctly been `GEMINI_API_KEY`. `lib/embeddings.ts` (Phase 7) and `lib/rag.ts` (Phase 8) were both written reading `process.env.GOOGLE_API_KEY`, and `.env.local`/`.env.example`/`docs/security.md`/`STATE.md` all documented `GOOGLE_API_KEY` to match — a naming mistake that went uncaught through two phases because everything was *internally* consistent (code, example file, and docs all agreed with each other, just on the wrong name) and no live Gemini call had been exercised yet to surface it as a missing-env-var failure. Compounding it: mid-Phase-8, `.env.example` briefly showed the correct `GEMINI_API_KEY` on one line while everything else still said `GOOGLE_API_KEY`; this agent treated `.env.example` as the stale outlier and "restored" it to match the wrong name, which was the opposite of the right fix. The user caught the actual mismatch directly, confirmed `GEMINI_API_KEY` is correct, and updated `.env.local` to it. **Fixed:** `lib/embeddings.ts:95`, `lib/rag.ts:73` (`process.env.GEMINI_API_KEY!`), `.env.example`, `docs/security.md` §5's env var table, and this file's §2/§5 entries all now read `GEMINI_API_KEY`. `docs/architecture.md` was checked and has no `*_API_KEY` references, so needed no change. Historical prompt files (`prompts/phase-7-embeddings-pgvector.md`, `prompts/phase-8-langchain-rag.md`) still say `GOOGLE_API_KEY` in prose — left as-is, since prompts are point-in-time implementation contracts, not living documentation; this bullet is the durable record of the correction.
- `npm run build` and `npx tsc --noEmit` pass cleanly as of Phase 1.
- `npm run lint` previously reported 15 errors / 304 warnings, all inside `.agents/` and `.claude/` skill-package files (not application code). Fixed 2026-08-11 under the trivial-change exemption: `eslint.config.mjs` now ignores `.agents/**` and `.claude/**` (they are skill packages, not app code, and were never intended to be linted as part of this project). `npm run lint` reports zero errors and zero warnings across the whole repo.
- **Function-level default-privileges: schema-wide fix abandoned, resolved via per-function discipline instead (closed 2026-08-12, see §1).** Three migrations were attempted for a schema-wide `REVOKE EXECUTE ... FROM anon, authenticated` on functions in `public`. First (`20260812191914_...`, revoke from `public`) assumed the gap was Postgres's ordinary `PUBLIC` default — wrong, live `has_function_privilege()` check still failed. Second (`20260812200105_...`, revoke from `anon, authenticated` explicitly) corrected which roles to name but still failed the same live check, because neither migration specified `FOR ROLE` and both only ever edited the `postgres`-owned default ACL entry — `pg_default_acl` revealed a *second*, separate entry owned by `supabase_admin` (the role Supabase's own tooling actually provisions objects under). Third (`20260812213356_...`, `alter default privileges for role supabase_admin ...`) correctly targeted that owning role, but **failed to apply**: `npx supabase db push` returned `permission denied to change default privileges` (`42501`) — the `postgres` role a migration connects as has no membership in `supabase_admin` on managed Supabase, and altering another role's default privileges requires that membership (or superuser). **This is a platform constraint, not a fixable bug — no normal-connection workaround exists, and it will not be retried.** All three migration files remain in `supabase/migrations/` as a record of the investigation; none are retroactive and `match_knowledge_chunks` was unaffected throughout (fixed individually via its own direct `revoke`). **Neutralized 2026-08-13:** the third migration's failure was permanent and was blocking every later `db push` (including Phase 10's) from getting past it, so its statement was removed in place (comments kept, per `prompts/neutralize-blocked-default-privileges-migration.md`) — it's now an intentional no-op, not a retroactive change to the live database. The first two migrations were left untouched. **Standing rule adopted instead, recorded in `docs/architecture.md`:** every new Postgres function must get its own explicit `revoke execute ... from anon` (or equivalent least-privilege grant) in its own migration, verified live via `has_function_privilege()` — the pattern already proven for `match_knowledge_chunks`. This must be checked per new function going forward; there is no schema-wide safety net for functions the way there is for tables. **Still flagged, not yet done:** the Phase 3 table-level default-privileges fix has never been independently re-checked against `pg_default_acl` for this same two-owning-roles issue.

**Phase 0 exit criteria (docs/phases.md) — all met:**
- `npm run lint` passes on a clean checkout — confirmed, zero errors/warnings.
- `npm run build` passes — confirmed.
- `.env.example` exists — confirmed.
- Folder conventions are documented — confirmed, `docs/architecture.md`.

**Phase 1 exit criterion (docs/phases.md) — met:**
- A request flows through a route handler with validated input, a typed result, and a controlled error response — confirmed via `GET /api/health`: valid requests return HTTP 200 with a typed JSON body; an invalid `verbose` value returns HTTP 400 with a safe error body containing no leaked internals (manually verified with curl, see prompt's manual testing steps).

**Phase 2 exit criterion (docs/phases.md) — met:**
- An unauthenticated visitor cannot reach `/dashboard` by any path — verified: `curl -i http://localhost:3000/dashboard` while signed out returns `307` redirecting to the Clerk-hosted sign-in URL. Server code reliably obtains the authenticated identity via `lib/auth.ts`'s `requireAuthContext()`.
- **Signed-in click-through confirmed by user 2026-08-11:** `/dashboard` renders the correct `userId`/`orgId`/`orgSlug` for a real test account, both before and after the resource-based auth migration below.
- **Resolved 2026-08-11** (`prompts/clerk-resource-based-auth.md`): Clerk's `createRouteMatcher` deprecation warning is gone. `proxy.ts` now only runs bare `clerkMiddleware()` (no path-based protection); `lib/auth.ts`'s `requireAuthContext()` wraps `auth.protect()` and is called directly in `app/dashboard/page.tsx`, matching Clerk's current recommended pattern. Redirect behavior for unauthenticated visitors confirmed unchanged (same sign-in URL as before). `docs/architecture.md` gained an "Authentication" section documenting the per-resource pattern, including the document-vs-non-document (redirect vs. 404) behavior difference Phase 11 will need.

**Phase 3 exit criterion (docs/phases.md) — met and fully verified (by user, not the agent — this implementation environment has no Docker/Supabase CLI project access):**
- Migration applied cleanly from scratch. ✓
- Tenant-isolation proven: Business A cannot read Business B's row. ✓ (verified via `supabase link` + `supabase db push` + a manual SQL-editor spot-check — two rows, two simulated Clerk-org sessions — not the automated `supabase test db` pgTAP run, which remains written but never executed by anyone.)
- Data API reachability for `authenticated` confirmed working. ✓
- Grants hardened to least privilege and re-verified twice (see the two grant-fix entries in §2): `authenticated` = `SELECT` only, `anon` = none, `TRUNCATE` denied for both, isolation still holds after tightening, and a throwaway table proved the schema-wide default-privileges fix applies to future tables too.
- **Still genuinely unconfirmed (low-stakes, not blocking):** the exact internal mechanism pgTAP's `001_businesses_tenant_isolation.sql` assumes (`auth.jwt()` reading `request.jwt.claims` via `set_config`) was never independently verified against this project's live instance, because the manual SQL-editor spot-check was used instead of running that test file. If `supabase test db` is ever run and that specific test fails, treat it as "go verify the claims-simulation technique in that file," not "the RLS policy itself is wrong" — the policy is proven correct by the manual verification above, independent of whether that particular test file works.

**Phase 4 exit criterion (docs/phases.md) — met and fully verified by user 2026-08-11:**
"A new user can go from sign-up to an owned business record with no manual database work." ✓ Confirmed end-to-end: org-admin create flow, empty-name validation, re-onboarding redirect, non-admin "ask your admin" gate, negative RLS insert check, double-submit race handling, and a direct-POST Server Action bypass (replayed under a non-admin session, outside the UI) all passed — see the Phase 4 entry in §2 for the full list. The role-check behavior of `auth.protect({ role: "org:admin" })` for an authenticated-but-unauthorized Server Action caller, previously only type-verified, is now confirmed live: it rejects server-side and no `businesses` row was created for the non-admin's org. Exact observed status/response recorded in `docs/architecture.md`'s Authentication section.

**Phase 5 exit criterion (docs/phases.md) — met, per user confirmation 2026-08-12:**
"Full CRUD works for each type, all queries are tenant-scoped, and isolation tests cover every new table." Migrations applied and grants verified; user confirmed a full regression pass across Phases 0–5 including real cross-tenant RLS verification. `supabase test db` (pgTAP) itself was not run — same standing gap as every prior phase, superseded by the manual verification described in the Phase 5 §2 entry.

**Phase 6 exit criterion (docs/phases.md) — met, per user confirmation 2026-08-12:**
"A business can add knowledge and see it correctly chunked and stored against its own `business_id`." All three migrations applied and verified; user confirmed live testing including the index-bugfix blast radius resolving correctly on re-save. `supabase test db` (pgTAP) itself was not run — same standing gap as every prior phase — but its assertions were covered by manual live verification (see the Phase 6 §2 entry).

**Phase 7 exit criterion (docs/phases.md) — met, per user confirmation 2026-08-12:**
"Similarity search returns relevant chunks for the correct business and returns nothing for a business with no matching knowledge — proven by a test, not by inspection." All four migrations applied and verified via `npx supabase db query --linked` (Dashboard was affected by a Supabase-side outage during testing — see `docs/architecture.md`'s Database section for this fallback method, now documented for future phases). User confirmed: L2 normalization correct (norm ≈ 1), self-similarity ranks at 1.0, an unrelated topic ranks at 0.60 (a meaningful, non-trivial ranking gap), legacy chunks backfill correctly on re-save, cross-tenant isolation and empty-result cases both hold, and the separate `PUBLIC`/`anon` execute-grant fix is confirmed working (permission denied for `anon`, success for `authenticated`). `supabase test db` (pgTAP) itself was not run — same standing gap as every prior phase — but `008_match_knowledge_chunks_tenant_isolation.sql`'s exact assertions were independently confirmed live.

**Phase 8 exit criterion (docs/phases.md) — met, per user confirmation 2026-08-12:**
"A question with no supporting knowledge produces the fallback behavior from `PRODUCT.md` §7, not an invented answer." All five manual test steps in `prompts/phase-8-langchain-rag.md` passed: zero-knowledge fallback with no Gemini call (confirmed via server logs); a grounded, correct answer with traceable `sourceChunkIds` on a real test business; an unrelated question correctly declined rather than hallucinated (validating Decision 4's no-hard-threshold approach); cross-tenant isolation held on a second test business; and a forced `GEMINI_API_KEY` failure produced a safe generic error, not a raw provider error, with the real key restored afterward. No new table/migration this phase, so no pgTAP gap to note.

**Phase 9 exit criterion (docs/phases.md) — met, per user confirmation 2026-08-12:**
"The same question asked against two different businesses yields two correctly-grounded, non-overlapping answers." Confirmed via Test 1 of `prompts/phase-9-gemini-sales-employee.md`. All remaining manual steps from that prompt and from the same-day `prompts/fix-rag-grounded-used-context-signal.md` follow-up also passed, per the Phase 9 §2 entry above — including two investigation items (the `grounded`/`usedContext` two-branch ambiguity, and a recurring stale-looking browser-console artifact) both closed with confirmed root causes rather than left as assumptions. No new table/migration either phase, so no pgTAP gap to note.

**Phase 11 exit criterion (docs/phases.md) — met, per user confirmation 2026-08-13:**
"The endpoint is exercised end-to-end with a widget key, is rate limited, and rejects a forged or mismatched `business_id`." All four migrations applied with grants/function-privileges confirmed live via `npx supabase db query --linked`, and all 11 manual testing steps in `prompts/phase-11-chat-api.md` passed, including the negative cases (wrong origin, invalid key, cross-tenant `conversationId`, forged `business_id` field, rate-limit exceeded, unconfigured origin fails closed) — see the Phase 11 §2 entry for the full list. **The user's first attempt at this manual test caught one real gap** (the `match_knowledge_chunks` 42501 permission error, `lib/retrieval.ts`/`lib/rag.ts` missing Phase 11's own client-injection pattern) — a concrete demonstration of why this exit criterion requires an actual test, not inspection: the earlier grant/build verification alone did not catch it. Fixed same day (`prompts/fix-widget-retrieval-client-injection.md`) and independently re-confirmed by the user as part of the same manual test pass. This satisfies the exit criterion — proven by test, not by inspection.

**Phase 10 exit criterion (docs/phases.md) — met, per user confirmation 2026-08-13:**
"A realistic conversation produces a correctly-attributed, validated lead row." All seven manual test steps in `prompts/phase-10-lead-extraction-creation.md` passed — see the Phase 10 §2 entry for the full list (catalog-match interest resolution, phone-only lead, no-contact-info non-creation, unmatched interest name, hot-vs-cold qualification distinction, cross-tenant isolation on reads and status updates, and status transitions persisting across a refresh). Both migrations (`conversations`, `leads`) applied and verified, including grants and cross-tenant RLS isolation. `supabase test db` (pgTAP `009_conversations_tenant_isolation.sql`, `010_leads_tenant_isolation.sql`) itself was not run — same standing gap as every prior phase — but its assertions were covered by the manual live verification above.

---

## How to update this file

At the end of every implementation, the agent must:

1. Move the phase entry into §2 if the phase is complete, and update §1 and §3.
2. Add any new env vars to §5 and migrations/tables to §6.
3. Update the prompt's status in §7.
4. Record any decision that got resolved, and any new decision that got deferred, in §4.
5. Add anything knowingly left unfinished to §8.
6. Update the "Last updated" date at the top.

If the agent implemented something but did not update this file, the work is not finished.
